# BACKLOG — Phase 6: Verification & Documentation — gates, tests, anchors, comments

> **Phase 6 of 6.** Split out of `BACKLOG.md` on 2026-08-23; `BACKLOG.md` is now the routing index and holds the chronological §RESUME map across all six files.
>
> **Scope of this file.** What proves the rest is true: CI gates and parity fences, Playwright and MUD suites, the anchor resolver, engine comments, maintained-doc rot, and the §DOC-02 lab-report verification program.
>
> **Siblings:** [1. Playable Truth](BACKLOG-1-playable-truth.md) · [2. Engine & Systems](BACKLOG-2-engine-systems.md) · [3. Content & Narrative](BACKLOG-3-content-narrative.md) · [4. World & Navigation](BACKLOG-4-world-navigation.md) · [5. Platform & Tooling](BACKLOG-5-platform-tooling.md) · **6. Verification & Documentation** *(this file)*

## The Backlog Directive — restated in full

> **This file is the list of outstanding activities for Phase 6 (Verification & Documentation).** The *how-we-work* rules live in **[CONTRIBUTING.md](../../CONTRIBUTING.md)**; closed/shipped work is archived in **[plan-archive.md](plan-archive.md)**. Split out of the former `plan.md` on 2026-07-09.
>
> **⚠️ Before starting ANY row — Existing-Work-First (CONTRIBUTING.md § Existing-Work-First Policy):** `git status` + `git log` first (uncommitted/fresh work matching the row = it's done or in progress — finish it, don't redo it); check for a second live session (`ps aux | grep claude`) before editing shared files; grep to *disprove* the row's premise (rows go stale — four rows have closed as ALREADY SHIPPED). A §RESUME entry says where the last session *stopped*, not what the tree looks like *now*.
>
> **Legend:** `[ ]` open · `[~]` in-progress / recurring · `[x]` closed (kept here for context; migrate to plan-archive.md over time).

> **⚠️ This file is one of six.** A row's neighbours may live in a sibling phase file, and the Existing-Work-First grep MUST cover all six — `grep -n '§DX-02xx' BACKLOG*.md` — before you conclude a row is unclaimed. The §RESUME entry that closed a row may sit in a different phase file than the row itself.

## Standing Directive — Code Comments (user directive 2026-08-23)

> **Binding on every increment in this file.** Where this and a §-row's own instructions disagree, this wins.

**The failure this prevents:** writing a comment that narrates the change being made right now. That context is real, but it expires the instant the change merges — the defect it describes no longer exists, so the comment becomes a story about a problem no future reader can observe. It is a changelog entry in the wrong file, and a third copy of text already required in the commit body and the PR description.

- **CC-1 (MUST NOT)** Write a comment describing a change, a fix, a defect, its cause, or what the code used to do. No *"was / now / previously / instead of"*, no *"this fixes"*, no *"needed because otherwise"*, no *"note that we no longer"*.
- **CC-2 (MUST)** Apply the **survival test** to every comment before writing it: *would this still be true and useful to someone reading this file a year from now, who never saw the diff?* If it only makes sense beside the diff, it is changelog — delete it and put it in the commit body.
- **CC-3 (MUST)** Default to **zero** comments. Declarative config — Terraform, DNS records, k8s manifests, CI YAML, Helm values — is self-describing and takes none. A resource named `dmarc-example-com` does not need a comment saying it is the DMARC record.
- **CC-4 (MAY)** Comment only when a future editor would actively break something without it: a non-obvious external constraint, a required out-of-band manual step, an invariant the surrounding code cannot show. **One line.** If it needs a paragraph it belongs in a design doc, not inline.
- **CC-5 (MUST)** Before every commit, re-read the comment lines added: `git diff --cached | grep '^+' | grep -E '#|//|/\*'`. Each hit must pass CC-2 on its own. **Deleting is always an acceptable outcome.** *"I already wrote it"*, *"it is only one line"*, and *"this one is genuinely useful"* are not exemptions — the last one is the exact thought that precedes every violation.
- **CC-6 (MUST)** Applies to comments **edited** as well as added. When a change invalidates an existing comment, the default action is **DELETE**, not rewrite it into a new narrative.

> **⚠️ KNOWN TENSION, flagged not resolved — needs a user call.** This repo currently runs an *opposite* convention in `play.html`: **§AUDIT-03n / §DX-02c "annotate, don't rewrite"**, which deliberately preserves defect history inline (e.g. `const NODE_NPC_KEYS`'s 8-line comment explaining that 21 of 26 rows named retired node codes, or `_renderFinalMap`'s §AUDIT-03e note on why the node filter is held at the historical set). Those are exactly the *"what the code used to do"* narratives CC-1 forbids, and several are load-bearing: they are the only record of why a value is held where it is, and deleting them would re-open closed audit rows. **Until the call is made, the working rule is:** CC-1..CC-6 bind all **new** comments; existing §AUDIT-/§DX-annotated blocks are **not** swept under CC-6 — they are a documented archaeology convention, not accidental changelog. Filed as **§DX-02fa** below.

## §BACKLOG — Open Items (Phase 6)

### §DX-02fx — the anchor-convention suite repairs the docs it is auditing, so its own verdict depends on run order (NEW 2026-08-23 during §DX-01g, 🟡 ONE DESIGN CALL: assert or repair, not both)

- [ ] **`src/tests/integration/dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js` has a write side-effect on tracked files.** Its third test runs the real gate with `--fix` against the repo root — `expect(run(['--fix']).code).toBe(0)` at `src/tests/integration/dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js:115` — and `--fix` rewrites stale `symbol@NNNN` hints **in place, in the working tree**. Running the suite is therefore a mutation of tracked documentation, not a read-only check.
- **The consequence is an order-dependent verdict.** The second test (`:71`) asserts every game-file anchor in `MIGRATED` is **line-exact**, and it runs *before* the `--fix` test. At `HEAD` (`c06d4f7`) `BACKLOG.md` carried **121 stale hints**, so that assertion was **red on a clean checkout** and went green only on the *next* run, after the earlier run's `--fix` had repaired the docs. A suite that green-lights itself on the second pass is reporting the state it created, not the state it found.
- **Evidence, this session.** First run: `1 failed` on `:71`, the other 4 green. No source change between runs — only the `--fix` write. Second run: `5 passed`.
- **The design call.** Either (a) the `--fix` test operates on a **temp copy** so the suite is read-only and `:71` tells the truth about a clean checkout, or (b) `:71` drops to the gate's own standard (*resolves*, not *line-exact*) and staleness stays a warning, as `check:anchors` already treats it. **(a) is the one that keeps a real assertion.** What must not stay is both.
- **Grep to disprove:** `grep -n "\-\-fix" src/tests/integration/dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js`.

### §AUDIT-03af — the engine's own comment is wrong about the past, and the wrong diagnosis produced the wrong fix (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02u, 🟡 mixed)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03af — `§VM-01-G1-FIX: originally keyed to 'LJ3', a dead@31341` states two things about history and both are false.** The comment reads *"originally keyed to `'LJ3'`, a dead node code **no NODE_MAP entry ever carried**, so this panel had **NEVER rendered**; remapped to LSO … the quest's own `activateNode`."* At §SIREN-01's birth commit `42c2f82` (2026-05-28) the file carries `LJ3:{ num:117, code:'LJ3', name:'junction', label:'The Serpent Passage', act:4 …}` — a real `NODE_MAP` entry carrying the arc's hardest battle (`battle:{label:'The Serpent of the Passage', key:'sea_serpent', count:1}`). **The panel therefore DID render, for 13 days, exactly where designed.** `LJ3` was **RETIRED** on 2026-06-10 by `a61d6eb`, not born dead.
> **The correction is CAUSAL, not cosmetic.** `{ id:'story-lso-trigger', nodes:['LSO'],@31345` is a *teaser*: it exists to tell the player there is something in the water **to the east** and send them to find it. Believing it had never worked, the fix pointed it at **`LSO` — the fog bank itself** — so it now announces the discovery to a player standing inside it: *"They are also, in a second and quieter register, speaking to something in the water to the east."*
> **This is §DOC-02g's rule in live form, 2nd confirmed instance: a migration commit's own comment is a CLAIM ABOUT THE PAST, usually written from memory rather than from the diff. Being "in the code" confers no authority about history — verify it with `git show <sha>` like any other claim.** The generalisable shape: ***a dead node code is usually a RETIREMENT, and diagnosing it as born-dead licenses a remap that a retirement would forbid.***
> **Fix, two halves.** (a) 🟢 **Correct the comment** — one line, no behaviour change, and worth doing alone so the next reader is not misled again. (b) 🟡 **Re-place the panel** — small design call, and it is downstream of **§SIREN-01-FU**: with LJ0–LJ3 gone there is no "crossing before the fog", so either the teaser moves to whatever node ends up preceding LSO, or it folds into LSO's own node text and stops being a teaser. **Do not do (b) before §SIREN-01-FU decides the geography.** *(Worth a sweep: this is the only §VM-01-G1 dead-panel remap whose premise §DOC-02u checked — the other remaps in that pass, §PLAY-01-G / §NPC-01-SF4, cite the same "born dead" standard and were not re-verified.)*
> **(c) THE SWEEP IS DONE — §DOC-02y, 2026-08-12, and the verdict is the OPPOSITE of `LJ3`'s: fix the comments, keep the remaps.** `const birkaNpcs@35139` justifies the §PLAY-01-G remap and closes *"**No NODE_MAP entry ever existed for CI/IN/TV/BA/CY**, so these cards previously rendered nowhere"*; two lines later §NPC-01-SF4 adds *"No NODE_MAP entry ever existed for CQ/SQ/GC either."* **Eight claims; at `32c10c5` (2026-05-24, earliest surviving build) SEVEN ARE FALSE** — `CI:{num:1,'city',"City Streets — Birka"}` · `IN:{num:2,'inn','The First Inn'}` · `TV:{num:3,'tavern','Birka Tavern'}` · `BA:{num:4,'bar','The Rough Bar'}` · `CY:{num:6,'cyberpunk_streets','Neon Undercity'}` · `SQ:{num:35,'scholars_qtr',"Scholar's Quarter — Weimar"}` · `GC:{num:26,'goblin_cave','Goblin Warrens'}` are all real `NODE_MAP` entries. **Only `CQ` holds.** ***And the comment transcribes those very labels — "The First Inn", "Birka Tavern", "The Rough Bar", "Neon Undercity" — character for character off the records the same sentence denies:*** self-refutation inside one comment, §DOC-02q's rule appearing in engine source. **But all seven remaps are CORRECT RENAMES**, verified by a `num` + terrain-key + label triple-match against the archive (`IN`→`TLL` num 2, `TV`→`MHQ` num 3, `BA`→`LLA` num 4, `CY`→`HKG` num 6, `CI`→`LHR` num 1, `SQ`→`NUE` num 35, `GC`→`TRD` num 26) — §DOC-02f's *"the node survived, only the key was renamed"* confirmed across seven pairs. The false premise produced seven right answers because the author was silently matching on labels. **So (c) is 🟢 and mechanical:** replace *"never existed"* with *"retired by §WALK/§NAV-01; the node survived under a new key, `num` preserved"* in both comments. ***The durable form: a migration comment's "never existed" is the least reliable sentence in a codebase — it is the one claim the migrator had no reason to check, because the remap already worked. Its danger is not the sentence, it is the STANDARD it sets for the next remap, which may have no label to match on — which is exactly how `LJ3` went wrong.***

### §AUDIT-03ba — 47 engine comments name a retired node code as a live scene's location, and that is where documentation authors read the world from (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bg, **EXTENDED 2026-08-14 by §DOC-02bi: 22 → 47**, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03ba — the guard says `LHR`, the comment above it says `CI`, and every doc written from this file says CI.** Census at HEAD: **22** `//` section comments name a node by a code that is dead (`CQ`/`SQ`/`SF`/`GC`/`MM`) or **live-as-a-different-node** (`CI` → Chancery Court `num:429`; `CO` was never a `NODE_MAP` key at all — §AUDIT-03p born-dead class). Eight name `CI` alone: `` `// ── Layer 50: §XV Entry 42 — blank journal page at CI (qualifying NG+ run) ──@34633` `` sits directly above `` `if (node.code === 'LHR' && (S_story.ngPlusRun || 0) >= 1@34636` ``; the same pairing repeats at `// ── L45-D/E: Nivers at CI ──@32228`, `// ── S7: Blue Shutters Archive at CI ──@32258`, `// ── Layer 63: Froberger Memorial at CI ──@32336`, `// ── No Fishing Sign at CI ──@32418`, `// ── Layer 66a: S54 Yael + Brynn joint witness at CI (Act VIII) ──@32479` and `// ── Layer 74 §XXXIX — Yael Named Report at CI ──@32488`. `CO` appears in six (`// ── §D02-10 — "The Seventh Shard" (CO — Loop Heart / Codex Core Chamber) ──@21717`, `// ── §D01-10: Codex Core Chamber — pre-boss choice room at CO ──@31811`, …) over blocks that all guard on `TLS`. `MM` in one (`// ── §D02-08 — "The Dropped Coin" (Node MM — Mimic Meadows) ──@21828`) over `quest_d0208_a1@21829`, whose five acts all carry `activateNode:'LIM'`.
> **No gate can see this, and the reason is a deliberate design choice made for a good reason.** `check:legacycodes` (gate #16) scans `*.md` only. `check:noderegs` phase 6 scans **comparison literals**, and every scanner in this repo is **comment-aware on purpose** — §AUDIT-03f was the row where comment arrow-fn examples silently ate whole `NODE_MAP` entries. So comment-awareness protects the scanner from the comments and leaves the comments themselves unaudited. *A documentation author reads comments.*
> **The harm is measured, not hypothetical.** §DOC-02bg re-verified `lab-report-synthesis-part6-quest-arcs.md`: **59 of 62 line citations exact, every statline and flag table byte-correct — and six node codes wrong, every one of them the code written in the comment above the guard the author cited.** The document names `CO` six times as a live location while the line it cites for that very claim reads `activateNode:'TLS'`. It is not a careless document; it is a document that read the only prose the file offers.
> **The engine already contains the fix, written once.** `` `// its arc's own render gate — CQ→CDG (Layer 44 Ally Cat Arc gates on node.code==='CDG'@35141` `` through 35,144 spells out CQ→CDG, SQ→NUE, GC→TRD and records that **no `NODE_MAP` entry ever existed for CQ/SQ/GC**. That annotation is correct, complete, and thirty comments away from the ones that need it.
> **Fix (mechanical, one sweep):** rewrite each of the 22 to the live code, keeping the historical one in the §AUDIT-03n/§DX-02c annotate-don't-erase form — `at LHR (ex-CI)` — so the arc's own history stays greppable. **Full mapping is settled and needs no research:** CI→LHR · CO→TLS · CQ→CDG · SQ→NUE · SF→STN · GC→TRD · MM→LIM.
> **Detector wanted, and it is cheap:** a `check:noderegs` **phase 7** that scans comment text *only* for a two-letter token in a place context (`at X`, `X node`, `Node X`, `(X — …)`) and fails when it resolves in neither `NODE_MAP` nor the classification lists — the same explicit-classification style as phases 1–6, with `NOT_A_NODE_CODE` already built by §AUDIT-03q. Phase 6's stated limit is that it reads comparison literals; this is its exact complement.
>
> **EXTENDED 2026-08-14 (§DOC-02bi) — the census missed its four largest members, and the largest of all outnumbers both codes the row leads with.** The original census was scoped to a fixed seven-code list (`CI`/`CO`/`CQ`/`SQ`/`SF`/`GC`/`MM`). Four more **born-dead** codes carry `// ── … ──` section comments naming them as live scene locations, and none was in that list: **`CY` ×11** (`21607`, `24686`, `32530`, `32565`, `32589`, `32596`, `32608`, `32633`, `32668`, `32673`, `35909`), **`IN` ×8** (`32155`, `32725`, `32740`, `32764`, `32779`, `32814`, `32839`, `35287`), **`BA` ×5** (`32027`, `32076`, `32107`, `32118`, `32127`), **`TV` ×1** (`32872`). That is **25 further comments, raising the census 22 → 47** — and `CY` alone (11) outnumbers `CO` (8) and `CI` (7), the two the row opens with. The canonical instance: `` `// ── Layer 41: Drunk Pit Fight (CY only) ──@24686` `` sits **two lines above** `` `_pb41.nodeCode === 'HKG'@24688` ``.
> **The mapping needs no research either — the engine states all four in one line.** `` `const birkaNpcs@35139` `` records *"dead pre-§WALK sublocation codes remapped to real nodes, each proved by its NPC's quest activateNode — IN→TLL (brynn) · TV→MHQ (quill) · BA→LLA (pachelbel) · CY→HKG (crov Weckmann + auros Bruhns/'Neon Undercity')"* and adds that **no `NODE_MAP` entry ever existed for CI/IN/TV/BA/CY**. **Extended mapping: CI→LHR · CO→TLS · CQ→CDG · SQ→NUE · SF→STN · GC→TRD · MM→LIM · CY→HKG · IN→TLL · BA→LLA · TV→MHQ.**
> **Second independent document confirms the vector.** §DOC-02bi re-verified `lab-report-meta-corpus-analysis.md` (written 53 minutes after part 6): **65 of 65 catalogued filenames real, 13 of 13 named characters resolve, 7 of 9 node codes correct — and both wrong ones are comment-sourced.** Its *"Loop Heart (CO)"* is traceable to a single site: the string **"Loop Heart" occurs exactly once in the whole file**, inside `` `(CO — Loop Heart / Codex Core Chamber)@21717` `` — the very comment this row already cites. Its *"CY madness"* is the `24686`/`32530` pair. **Two authors, two documents, one comment surface.**
> **Note for the phase-7 detector:** `CY`/`IN`/`BA`/`TV` are exactly the tokens a naive scan gets wrong in the *other* direction — `IN` is an English word, `BA`/`TV` are common abbreviations, `CY` is a prefix of `cyberpunk_streets`. All four must be classified explicitly (place-context cue required), which is the §AUDIT-03q lesson arriving on schedule rather than being rediscovered.
> **Related, not duplicate:** §AUDIT-03s is dead codes in **player-facing strings** (the player sees them); this is dead codes in **engine comments** (only authors see them, and then they publish them). §AUDIT-03am is one instance of the `CI` residue that reached the screen (`story-ci-tagline@31319`). §DX-02q wants semantic collisions in *code*. **Risk:** none — comments only. **Verify:** the phase-7 scan at 0 after the sweep, plus `npm run check:walk` green.
> **⟶ §DOC-02ci (2026-08-21) — a lab report committed this class in the same document that diagnosed it, and one of the seven codes needs reclassifying.** `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-play-review.md` §1 placed the final battle *"at node CO"*; **`CO` resolves in no `NODE_MAP` in any tree searched** — the final battle is at `` `TLS:{ num:42,@8726` `` via `` `_isFinalBoss: true };@28011` ``. The same report's §PLAY-01-G correctly flags the docs for citing a start node of `CI` — so the review broke §AUDIT-03m in §1 while diagnosing it in §7. **Reclassification (instrument 129, second instance):** `CI` should NOT be carried in this row as a dead code. `` `CI: { num:429, name:"city"@9229` `` is **live**; the defect around it was never non-resolution but **mis-resolution** — `birkaNpcs` keyed Yael to a real, wrong node, so her card rendered in Chancery Court rather than failing to render, which is a different signature and needs a different detector than the `at X` comment scan this row proposes.

### §DX-02ck — the road net grew on 2026-07-28 and five maintained documents still describe the one it replaced (NEW 2026-08-17 during §DOC-02bt, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02ck — `400 cells / 88 junctions / 1.4% / 235 settlements` is the §NAV-01b net; the live one has been `410 / 89 / 1.5% / 244` for twenty days.** 🟢 **five one-line figure edits + two path fixes, ~15 minutes.** `fa8f9e4` (§DX-01a, 2026-07-28) ran `build-roads.js --apply` to lay the real Tungas–Station 7 road and reroute the TLL→SPB corridor off a sea cell; the generator's own header in the game file was rewritten to *"410 road cells, 89 junctions"* and `check:roads` has printed **R1 244/244 · R4 410 cells = 1.5%** ever since. **Five maintained docs still carry the old figure:** `index.md:189` (§NAV-01 registry row), `maps.md:581` and `maps.md:754`, `mechanics.md:711` (*"a fungal highway net (400 road cells, 88 intersections/T-junctions)"* — the player-facing description), and `docs/notes/docs-node-network.md:468` (the L3 row of the L0–L8 stack). **The correction already exists in two places and never propagated:** `BACKLOG.md:713` and `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-walk15fu-grid-resolution.md:133` both say *"410 road cells and 89 junctions across all 244 settlement cells"*, written by §DOC-02bn/bo. **Two of the same lines also carry pre-§CLEANUP-02 paths** — `index.md:422` lists `rooms.js` and `docs/notes/docs-node-network.md:468` says pins live in `roads-pins.json`; `cc35c08` (2026-07-09) moved them to `src/js/rooms.js` and `src/config/roads-pins.json`, and neither file exists at the root. **Blast radius is documentation only** — every gate reads the data, not the prose — but `mechanics.md` is the one a player-facing writer would quote, and the figure will drift again on the next reweave. **Fix:** update the five figures and the two paths; consider having `check:roads` print the figure in a form a doc-sync pass can diff, so the next `--apply` surfaces the debt instead of burying it. **Same family as §DX-02ce and §DX-02cg** — older prose asserts, newer prose corrects, and nothing reconciles them; machines back-propagate, prose has to be pushed. **Risk:** none (docs). **Verify:** `grep -rn "400 road cells\|88 junctions\|88 intersection" --include='*.md' .` returns only `docs/lab-reports/` and `plan-archive.md` (HISTORY, correct for their day).

### §DX-02co — the two orientation figures a lab report copied in 2026-07 are still wrong in the docs it copied them from (NEW 2026-08-17 during §DOC-02bv, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02co — `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md:55` says "216+ monsters" (live 398) and `docs/spec/spec-migration.md:46` says "40 terrain types" (live 111).** 🟢 **two figure edits, ~5 minutes.** `lab-report-mesh-sync-architecture.md`'s §I.A opened with five orientation figures. Two — the line count (34,494) and the cell grid (`` `const GEO_PROJ = { ROWS: 90, COLS: 360 }@9902` ``) — were byte-exact for the day it was written. Three were wrong, and **the provenance is recoverable by grep**: *"216+ monsters"* is a verbatim lift from `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md:55` (`+` included) and *"40 terrain types"* from `docs/spec/spec-migration.md:46`. The file held **392 monsters and 106 terrains** that day. **The instructive part is why:** the one figure the author measured himself is the one `wc -l` answers in a second; the three that needed a parse of the data sections were copied out of prose, because **`npm run stats` did not exist until `8c9a482` (§DX-01g, 2026-07-23)** — three weeks later, and filed to *"single-source the orientation counts"* for exactly this reason. **Both source docs are still stale at HEAD**, so the same lift is available to the next author. `spec-migration.md` is a spec doc and `docs-dev-environment.md` an onboarding note, so both are read by newcomers with no way to tell a live figure from a 2026-05 one. **Fix:** replace both figures with a pointer to `npm run stats` rather than a fresher number — a corrected constant is a constant that will be wrong again, and §DX-01g exists to end the class, not to reset its clock. **Same family as §DX-02ce · §DX-02cg · §DX-02ck** (older prose asserts, newer machinery corrects, nothing reconciles them) and the direct twin of §DX-02ba, which found four dead claims in the same `docs-dev-environment.md`. **Risk:** none (docs). **Verify:** `grep -rn "216+ monsters\|40 terrain type" --include='*.md' .` returns only `docs/lab-reports/` (HISTORY, annotated).

### §DX-02cr — `check:duelparity` is the only parity gate no automation runs (NEW 2026-08-17 during §DOC-02bw, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02cr — the duel kernel's byte-identity gate exists, passes, and is invoked by nothing.** 🟢 **one entry in the `check:walk` chain + two CI path lines, ~10 minutes.** `lab-report-mesh-multiuser.md` §6.3 specified `DUEL:CORE` as a shared pure kernel *"inlined verbatim into `play.html` and `require`d by the server, guarded by a `check:duelparity` gate (byte-identical, **exactly like `check:parity`/`check:roomsparity`** for MOVER:CORE and ROOMS:CORE)"*. The gate shipped and works — `npm run check:duelparity` prints *"DUEL:CORE identical in duel.js and play.html (7987 bytes)"* six weeks on. **The comparison to its two siblings is where it fails: both of those are inside the sixteen-gate `check:walk` chain (`package.json:40`) and `check:duelparity` is not**, nor is it a step in `.github/workflows/walk-invariants.yml` (whose steps are `check:walk` · `check:arraypatch` · `check:itemchain` · `check:laddermigration` · `check:worlddiff` · `test:mud`), **nor is `src/js/duel.js` in either `paths:` filter** — so editing the standalone kernel triggers no CI at all. Of the ten checkers enumerated in the *"consolidate `src/scripts/check-*.js`"* row at `BACKLOG.md:206`, which asserts each is *"**individually wired** into both `package.json` scripts **and** the CI path-filters + steps"*, **`check:duelparity` is the only one for which the second half is false** — that row's premise needs the same correction. **Why it matters more than a missing gate usually does:** the duel design's entire anti-cheat premise is that a cheater is *"a machine that disagrees with a pure function of committed inputs"*, and the client prints **"Replay verified ✓"** to the player on the strength of it. If `src/js/duel.js:function __duelSha256(msg) {@28` and the inlined `const DUEL = {@10321` ever drift, the two sides compute different transcripts, the server's outcome event and the client's replay disagree — and the checkmark that tells the player the duel was fair becomes the first thing to lie. Presence drift blinks a dot; kernel drift falsifies a durable, dual-signed economy event appended to both players' chains. **Related:** §DX-02cp option (b) proposes *deriving* `ENGINE_VER` from the `DUEL:CORE`/`MOVER:CORE`/`QUEST:CORE` parity spans, which presumes those spans are continuously verified — this row is that presumption's precondition, so land it first. **Fix:** append `&& npm run check:duelparity` to the `check:walk` chain (it is a sub-second byte compare), and add `src/js/duel.js` + `src/scripts/check-duel-parity.js` to both `paths:` filters in the workflow. **Risk:** none — the gate is green today, so adding it turns nothing red. **Verify:** `npm run check:walk` prints 17 gates and includes duel parity; a deliberate one-byte edit to `src/js/duel.js` fails `check:walk` locally.

### §DX-02ct — the retired four-tab Map bar still ships in two docs, one of which also calls a closed increment open (NEW 2026-08-17 during §DOC-02bx, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02ct — `docs/notes/docs-node-network.md` and `index.md` still name a sub-tab bar that has not existed since 2026-07-08.** 🟢 **two sentences, ~5 minutes.** §MESH-02's D1 shipped the Map sheet with four sub-tabs — *"🗺 Map · 🌐 Connect · 🔭 Discover · 🛡 Lists"* — and **§MP-MAPTABS added two more the next day** (`msub-world`, `msub-full`), renaming two of the originals in the process. HEAD's `<div id="map-subtab-bar">@4585` reads **🗺 Local · 🌍 World · 🛰 Full · 🌐 Multiplayer · 🔭 Discover · 🛡 Lists**, and `src/tests/integration/mesh-connections-ui.test.js:sub-tab shell: 6 tabs / 6 panes@48` asserts the six. **Two docs still print the retired four:** `docs/notes/docs-node-network.md:451` and `index.md:191`. `mechanics.md:928` was updated correctly and is the model — *"🌐 Multiplayer · 🔭 Discover · 🛡 Lists"* — so this is a two-file sync, not a rewrite. **`index.md:191` carries a second, worse staleness in the same row:** it lists (a)–(f) and (h)–(j) as ✅ and then closes with *"— (g) docs/CLI closes it"*, but **(g) shipped 2026-07-07 as `c03cdc5`** and is exactly what wrote the rest of that row; the §MESH-02 registry line therefore reads as an open track when the track has been complete for six weeks. **Why it matters beyond tidiness:** these are the two *operator-facing* entry points (the network doc and the registry), so a reader following either one looks for a 🌐 Connect tab that no longer exists and concludes the connection UI is missing, when it is one tab over under a different name. The label is the only thing that changed — the pane ids and every function behind them are untouched. **Fix:** update both roster strings to the shipped six, and change `index.md:191`'s tail to record (g) ✅ with its ship hash, matching the a–j table in `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-mesh02-connections-ui.md`. **Risk:** none — docs only. **Verify:** `grep -rn "🌐 Connect" docs/ index.md mechanics.md` returns nothing, and `check:anchors` stays green.

### §DX-02cw — `index.md` is classified HISTORY, so the constant table `prompt.md` sends you to is outside gate #16 (NEW 2026-08-17 during §DOC-02bz, 🟢)

- [ ] **§DX-02cw — the doc most likely to be read as current is fenced as though it were an archive.** 🟢 **a classification decision plus one sweep.** `src/scripts/legacy-codes.js` lists `'index.md'` in `HISTORY_FILES`, beside `plan-archive.md` and the quarantined `maps.md` tables. But `prompt.md` §1 names `index.md` as one of the **three governing files** and points at its *Design Constants / State Fields quick reference* as the place to look a constant up — and §9's cheat sheet repeats it. **A live lookup table is being gate-fenced as history.** Found via the five Void Archaeology rows, which read *"Void Archaeology mark found at CI / SL / DF / WM / MT"* — five retired codes, of which `CI` is the §AUDIT-03m hazard in pure form (it resolves to a **different** live node, Chancery Court) and `WM` names the very key whose staleness cost §XVII thirty-nine days of dead content. **Those five rows are annotated in this increment**, so the row is about the classification, not the instances.
> **Why the mixed shape is real and the fix is a judgement call:** `index.md` genuinely holds both halves — a doc map and ship history (HISTORY-shaped) *and* the constants/state-fields reference (SWEEP-shaped). **Two options.** (a) **Promote it to SWEEP and sweep it** — measure the hit count first (`node src/scripts/legacy-codes.js` reports per-file counts) and expect the annotation cost to land mostly in the historical half. (b) **Split the file**, moving Design Constants + State Fields into `docs/reference/` and classifying only that as SWEEP. Prefer (a) unless the count is large; (b) is the better structure and the bigger change. **Related:** §DX-02ct is already correcting two stale rosters in this same file, so whoever takes that row should take this one. **Verify:** `npm run legacy` reports `index.md` under SWEEP with zero bare legacy codes; `check:legacycodes` exit 0.

### §DX-02cz — `check:questgraph` calls every `countMin` clause satisfiable because counters are monotone, and never asks whether the counter has a writer (NEW 2026-08-18 during §DOC-02cb, 🟡 one detector extension)

- [ ] **§DX-02cz — the written-by-nothing detector covers flags and stops there.** 🟡 **extend one scan; the resource is already collected.** The gate already parses counters into its resource pool at `src/scripts/check-questgraph.js:out.resources.add('count:' + c.path.split('.')[0])@253`, then its satisfiability pass lists `countMin` among the clauses that are *"monotone-satisfiable (the clock always reaches the window)"* and moves on. For a flag the gate asks **who writes it**; for a counter it asks nothing. That is the exact blind spot §DX-02cy hid in for 85 days, in the arc the gate was written to protect.
> **The check is cheap and the corpus is small.** Three counters are read by `completion.countMin` today — `catKills` (5 quests), `monsterKills` (6) and `frCatKillCount` (1) — and a host-writer scan of the inline script for `S_story.<counter>[` / `S_story.<counter> =` answers all three. **Self-test it by deleting a writer** (instrument 114): with the fix in place, reverting §DX-02cy's rename must move the gate's output, and today it does not move at all.
> **Note the polarity against §DX-02bs.** That row is a *false negative* from a host-writer pattern that credits too much (`missionBit:`); this is a false negative from a whole resource class that is never asked. Same detector, opposite causes — fix them in one pass and re-pin the printed counts once.

### §DX-02db — commit `3c86055` announces four game-file edits and contains none of them, so `git log` reports a revert that never happened (NEW 2026-08-18 during §DOC-02cb, 🟢 one note, plus one open question)

- [ ] **§DX-02db — the history says the `monsterKills` counter was reverted. It was not.** 🟢 **a corrective note; the walkability question may be larger.** `3c86055` (2026-07-09, *"§KG Inc 3 follow-up: wire SPB→Moscow corridor into walkable map + revert generic monsterKills counter"*) enumerates four game-file changes in its body: road-junction chain **J16–J21** linking TLL→SPB→KMS→ZVD→FBR, trimming the old eastern `ROAD_RUNS` rows, dropping the generic counter from `_S_DEFAULTS`/`_storyBattleVictory`/`storyRenderQuests`, and removing SPB's *"stray"* `Sealed Recruit Manifest` loot. **Its diff does not touch `play.html` at all** — the file is byte-identical between `d6aeefd` and `3c86055`. Only `milepoints/patches/_last.html`, a *snapshot*, carries the reverted text (`monsterKills` 3 → 0). The edits were made in the working tree and never staged.
> **What is true at HEAD:** `monsterKills` is present (`catKills: {}, monsterKills: {}, catKingDefeated: false,@23120` + the increment + the HUD read), SPB still carries `loot:"Sealed Recruit Manifest"`, and **J16–J21 do not exist** in `NODE_MAP` or `NODE_COORDS`. `git log -S "monsterKills" --all` returns `3c86055` and therefore *reads* as a revert to anyone searching the history — the trap this row exists to defuse.
> **The open question is the walkable map.** The corridor sits on a contiguous diagonal, TLL `{r:10,c:204}` → SPB `{r:10,c:210}` → KMS → ZVD → FBR → TVR → SVO `{r:14,c:217}`, but the junction chain that was meant to link it was never laid. `check:walk` is 16/16 green and does not answer node-to-node walkability. **Measure whether a player can actually walk from TLL to SPB before deciding whether this is a note or a §NAV row** — the answer changes §DX-02cy's blast radius from *"11 quests cannot complete"* to *"11 quests cannot be reached."*

### §DX-02dn — `mechanics.md` says two drop functions were "retired" and "deleted"; neither has ever existed in the engine (NEW 2026-08-21 during §DOC-02ci, 🟠 two-line prose fix, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02dn — a doc-sync fix corrected the numbers and promoted the wrong diagnosis to fact.** `mechanics.md:231` reads *"The old parallel 15%/12% separate dagger and weapon rolls are retired"* and `mechanics.md:530` reads *"the old `_rollWeaponDrop()` 12%/battle path is **deleted**"*. **Instrument 67 (`git log -S <sym> --all`, NO pathspec, then re-scoped):** `_rollMainWeaponDrop` and `_rollWeaponDrop` appear in exactly seven commits, **every one a documentation commit** (`BACKLOG.md`, `plan-archive.md`, `mechanics.md`, `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md`, `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-play-review.md`). Re-run scoped to `*.html`: **zero commits, ever, in any HTML file in the repository history.** The only drop function that has ever shipped is `` `function _rollMonsterWeaponDrop(monsterDmgDie) {@24581` `` (4 HTML commits). **The docs were never describing deleted code — they were describing an API that was never implemented.**
> **PROVENANCE, and it is the point of the row.** The pre-fix doc (`mechanics.md:490`–`:511` at `3a0db52^`) was wrong about the *engine* — *"42 entries"* against a real 70, `minLevel = baseLv + magic × 4` against a real `level ≥ magic × 5`, and two invented drop rates. §PLAY-01-G caught all four and fixed three correctly. But the parent review had concluded *"Both functions are deleted"*, and `ac651a3` wrote that conclusion **into the doc as fact**. A file that carried one unverifiable claim now carries a confident, sourced, wrong one. **This is §AUDIT-03m-FU in its live form: annotation without verification launders a wrong claim into a confident-looking live one** — and the laundering agent was the fix, not the drift.
> **Fix (two lines, no call):** rewrite both to say the rates and functions were **specified but never implemented**, in the §AUDIT-03n/§DX-02c annotate-do-not-erase form, so the history stays greppable — e.g. *"a 15%/12% split roll was specified in the Layer-24 notes and never built; the shipped path is the single guaranteed `_rollMonsterWeaponDrop()`."* **Do NOT write "removed"** — that is the error this row exists to retire. **Verify:** `git log -S_rollWeaponDrop --all -- '*.html'` returns empty, and no doc asserts a deletion.
> **Detector wanted, and it is cheap and general:** a gate that flags any doc sentence asserting **removal** (*deleted · retired · removed · replaced*) of a `symbol()` whose name has **0 commits ever in the code files** — a removal claim is falsifiable by exactly one `git log -S`, and the corpus now has two instances (this row, and §DOC-02c's seven §WALK corridor symbols). Cheaper than `check:deadconsts` because it needs no parse.

### §DX-02fa — the new Code Comments directive contradicts the repo's own annotate-don't-rewrite convention (NEW 2026-08-23, 🟡 POLICY, ONE DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02fa — CC-1 forbids exactly what §AUDIT-03n/§DX-02c requires.** 🟡 The Standing Directive at the top of this file (user directive 2026-08-23) says **CC-1 (MUST NOT): write a comment describing a change, a fix, a defect, its cause, or what the code used to do** — no *"was/now/previously"*, and **CC-6** makes DELETE the default when a change invalidates a comment. `play.html` runs the opposite convention on purpose: **§AUDIT-03n / §DX-02c "annotate, don't rewrite."** Live examples, all load-bearing: `const NODE_NPC_KEYS = {@27413` carries an 8-line note recording that **21 of 26 rows named a node code that does not exist**, why the remap chose the values it did, and why removing the 20 `E*` placeholders is a no-op; `function _renderFinalMap()@27605` carries the §AUDIT-03e note explaining that the node filter is **deliberately held at the historical 129-entry set** and that widening it is an authored call (§AUDIT-03e-FU); `const NPC_FAREWELLS = {@27427` records the one **content override** where the text was treated as the authority over the node code.
> **These are the only record of why those values are what they are.** Delete them under CC-6 and the next agent re-derives a closed audit row from scratch, or "fixes" a deliberate hold. But they are also, literally, *"what the code used to do"* — CC-1's exact prohibition.
> **THE CALL:** (a) **Scope CC-1..CC-6 to NEW comments only** and carve out `§`-tagged archaeology blocks as a named exception in the directive — *recommended*, it keeps both rules honest and is a two-sentence edit; (b) **apply CC uniformly** and migrate the ~dozen §AUDIT/§DX annotation blocks into a `docs/` archaeology file with `→ doc:` pointers back — faithful to CC-3/CC-4, more work, and the pointers are the class §DX-02ey(b) just found unfenced; (c) **exempt `play.html`** entirely and apply CC to `src/js/`, `src/scripts/`, `src/tests/` only. **(a) recommended.** *Interim working rule, already written into the directive: CC binds all new comments; existing §-tagged blocks are not swept.*

### §DX-02fw — the shipped comment explaining `set:1` gives a reason that is the opposite of the code (NEW 2026-08-23 during §DOC-02dc, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02fw — `play.html` l. 34361 carries the comment *"`set:1`, not `add:1` — `_setNpcFavor` takes an ABSOLUTE level and only ever raises it"* and `lab-report-vm01g4-per-verb.md` §12⅞ stated the same rationale as *"`add:1` would have **lowered** a favor already at 2."* Both halves are wrong, and they were wrong when written.** 🟢 `function _setNpcFavor(key, level)@23462` opens `if (level <= prev) return;` — **it cannot lower anything, ever**; that is the whole point of the guard. And the kernel's `favor` handler does not pass `add` through as an absolute: `E.setFavor(bit.npc, Math.min(bit.cap == null ? 3 : bit.cap, (E.getFavor ? E.getFavor(bit.npc) : 0) + bit.add))` reads the **live** level first, and `getFavor` has been bound in the host env since `9f10bfe` (**2026-07-22, twelve days before §VM-01-G4c**). So `add:1` on a favor already at 2 **raises Yva to 3 — Dear Friend** — an unearned promotion, not a demotion. **The shipped bit `set:1` is still correct** (it reproduces the inline handler byte-for-byte and is pinned by a test in `uqf-node-verbs-d1.test.js`); only the stated reason is inverted. **Fix:** rewrite the comment to say what it actually avoids. **Why it matters beyond one line:** the comment is the only in-file documentation of `favor`'s two modes, and the next author who reaches for `add` will read it and conclude the opcode is unsafe when it is merely absolute-vs-relative.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02dc, finding N4.

### §DX-02ey — five `→ doc:` pointers wrong in one layer, and the gate that cannot see them (NEW 2026-08-23 during §DOC-02cy, 🟢 four comments + a gate)

- [ ] **§DX-02ey(a) — repoint four comments.** 🟢 `PETRA_STALL_STATES → world.md §Act III Optional Detour` — the heading exists and is about **Yugurt Lake** (`BOO`/`SSJ`); wrong subject, and the comment also says *"Petra's CI bread stall"* (wrong name, wrong node). `WORLD_PROGRESSION_EVENTS → world.md §World Progression` — **0 occurrences** in `world.md`. `NODE_NPC_KEYS → world.md §NPC Locations` — **0 occurrences**. `NPC_FAREWELLS → story.md §Act VIII NPC Farewell Beats` — lives at **`world.md:1258`**, right heading, wrong file. A fifth, `DEACON_CODE_TEXT → world.md §Deacon's Code (… Pachelbel Dear Friend)`, resolves — but **there is no favor gate on the button**, correctly so: §IX's whole point is *"The code is public. He posted it on the wall."* The pointer invents a gate the spec forbids. *(Deferred out of §DOC-02cy because it edits `play.html`, whose working tree carries the user's uncommitted CSS recolor.)*
- [ ] **§DX-02ey(b) — grow `check:anchors` to resolve section pointers.** 🟢 `check:anchors` is green (**4,507** `symbol@line` anchors across **109** docs, 117 stale hints, zero dead) because it audits *symbol* anchors only — it does not resolve `→ doc: <file> §<Section>` pointers at all. This is the same unfenced class §DX-02er(b) filed from §DOC-02cx; **five more instances in a single layer** argue the gate should grow rather than the rows accumulate. The check is cheap: split on `→ doc:`, take the file and the `§`-heading, and grep the target. Every existing pointer becomes falsifiable in one pass.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02cy, finding F11; §DX-02er(b) is its predecessor.

### §DX-02ef — two statements about the doom clock, both wrong, both in files that point at the clock feature (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02ct, 🟢 two sentences, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02ef — an inline line-number reference that was wrong the day it shipped, and an onboarding sentence that misdescribes what advances the clock.** 🟢 **two edits, no behaviour.** **(1)** The comment §BOARD-01-FU8 shipped above its roster reads *the `VOID_TIDE_EVENTS` beats at 21821* — `beats at 21821@37098`. In the very build that comment shipped in, `VOID_TIDE_EVENTS` sat at **21822**; at HEAD it sits at **22368**, 546 lines away. **Nothing in the repo can catch this:** `src/scripts/resolve-anchors.js:65` collects only files ending `.md`, so a bare line number inside `play.html` is outside every gate by construction. Replace it with a symbol reference (*the `VOID_TIDE_EVENTS` beats*) — the table has one definition site and needs no number. **(2)** `prompt.md:196` states *"Time is a real resource — `Wait`/rest and travel cost days"*, and cites the §BOARD-01 Void-tide bounties as its example of day-windowed content. **Travel costs no days.** `S_story.day` has exactly **one** write site in the whole engine — `S_story.day = Math.min(49, S_story.day + 1)@36270`, inside `function storyConfirmSleep()@36244`. The clock is a rest counter; distance is free. Correct the sentence to *rest advances the clock; travel does not*.
> **Why it matters more than a typo.** The missable-window design of §BOARD-01-FU8 reads completely differently under the two readings: if travel cost days, a 28-leg bounty posted for 14 days would be a race the player might lose to the map. It is not — the player loses the window only by sleeping, which is the resource the design actually taxes. An author reading `prompt.md` before touching a day-gated feature would balance it against a cost the engine never charges.
> **Verify:** `grep -c "beats at 21821"` reaches 0 · `grep -c "S_story.day *=" play.html` stays at 1 · `check:anchors` green (both edits are outside the anchored set; the comment carries no anchor because it cannot).

### §DX-02ec — one of the four parity fences is in package.json and in nothing that runs (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cs, 🟢 one word in a chain, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02ec — `check:duelparity` exists, is green, and is executed by no automated path: not by `check:walk`, not by CI.** 🟢 **add one `&& npm run check:duelparity` to the walk chain.** CONTRIBUTING's invariant #5 names **four** parity fences — `MOVER:CORE`, `ROOMS:CORE`, `DUEL:CORE`, `QUEST:CORE` — each pure, world-injected, byte-identical to its `src/js/*.js` twin and *"asserted by `src/scripts/check-*-parity.js`"*. Three of those assertions run on every walk (`check:parity`, `check:roomsparity`, `check:questparity`). The fourth is declared at `package.json:"check:duelparity"` and referenced nowhere else in the repo except one comment in `src/tests/integration/mesh-duel-client.test.js:12`. `.github/workflows/walk-invariants.yml` runs `check:walk` plus `check:arraypatch`, `check:itemchain`, `check:laddermigration` and `check:worlddiff` — **`check:duelparity` is in none of them.**
> **What that means concretely.** Edit the inlined `DUEL:CORE` copy in `play.html` instead of `src/js/duel.js` — the single most-warned-against mistake in the repo — and every gate passes. The fence that exists to catch it is never asked. It is green right now (`✓ duel parity: DUEL:CORE identical in duel.js and play.html (7987 bytes)`, run this session), which is the state of the tree, not the state of the guard.
> **Provenance, and it is a nice one.** Found by re-reading `git show <ship>:package.json` while scoring a §VM-01-F line that claimed *"`check:walk` — all four parity fences green."* On 2026-07-22 the walk ran **9 gates and three fences**; the fences were all green, so the fact was right and the attribution was wrong. Checking whether the attribution had since become true is what surfaced that it never has. **Read the gate list of the day before scoring a "gates green" line — sometimes the audit hands you a live hole.**
> **Fix:** append `&& npm run check:duelparity` to `check:walk`, beside `check:roomsparity`/`check:questparity`. **Verify:** `npm run check:walk` prints the duel-parity line; the gate count in `prompt.md` §7 and `CONTRIBUTING.md` goes 16 → 17; a deliberate one-character edit to the inlined `DUEL:CORE` block fails the walk (revert it).

### §DX-02ed — the gate-flag walker cannot see into the gate grammar the same increment introduced (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cs, 🟢 recurse three keys, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02ed — `function _gateFlagSet()@26133` walks `q.gate.flags` / `flagsAny` / `notFlags` at the top level only, so any flag nested under an `{all}` / `{any}` / `{not}` activation gate is invisible to it.** 🟢 **make the walker recurse the three combinator keys.** §VM-01-F taught the gate to be a boolean tree; `_gateFlagSet` was written before there were trees and still reads only the root object. The report that shipped the grammar **named this defect exactly**, in its §8 *"Known latent (documented, not triggered)"*: *"when activation gates adopt the AST, that walker should recurse the tree (tracked as a §VM-01-F follow-on)."*
> **It was never tracked.** No row exists in BACKLOG.md or `plan-archive.md`, at any point since 2026-07-22. The lab report is the only place in the repo where this is written down — which is precisely why the §DOC-02 program exists.
> **Still latent, and measured rather than assumed.** In Chromium over the live `QUEST_DB` at the ship build **and** at HEAD: **0** quests carry an `{all}`/`{any}`/`{not}` *activation* gate (2,823 gate-bearing quests, 2,809 of them non-legacy leaf objects), and exactly **1** carries an AST *completion* — `quest_wm_01`, which `_gateFlagSet` does not read. `_gateFlagSet` is still non-recursive at HEAD. **So the defect cannot fire today and will fire silently the first time anyone authors what the grammar was built for** — the failure mode is a *missing* flag in the set, i.e. a gate flag that no longer registers as gate-relevant, with no error and no visible symptom.
> **Fix (mechanical, no design call):** in the `for (const key of ['flags', 'flagsAny', 'notFlags'])` loop, first descend `g.all` / `g.any` / `g.not` recursively, collecting from every leaf. Six lines. **Verify:** author a throwaway `gate:{any:[{flags:['x']},{flagsAny:['y']}]}` in a test fixture and assert `_gateFlagSet()` contains both `x` and `y`; `check:walk` stays green; the live set's size is unchanged today (0 AST activation gates), which is the correct no-op proof for a fix landing ahead of its trigger.

### §DX-02dz — the parity gate says "bytes" and measures characters, and every ship record quoting it inherits the mislabel (NEW 2026-08-22 during §DOC-02cq, 🟢 one word, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02dz — `src/scripts/check-quest-parity.js:quest parity: QUEST:CORE identical@25` prints `${a.length}` and calls the result "bytes".** 🟢 **one word in one template literal.** `a` is a JS string, so `.length` counts **UTF-16 code units**; the `QUEST:CORE` fence is dense with non-ASCII (the `◆◆◆` sentinels, em-dashes, `·`), and the two figures diverge measurably. At the §VM-01-E ship build the gate printed **21,909** and the region is **22,135** UTF-8 bytes; at HEAD it prints **25,030** against **25,283**. The comparison the gate performs is a string `===`, which is correct and unaffected — **only the label is wrong.**
> **Why it is worth a word.** The figure is quoted as a byte count in ship records and lab reports as the evidence that a change was data-only (§VM-01-E §7 does exactly this, and its underlying claim is true — parent and ship both measure 21,909). Anyone who re-derives it with `wc -c` gets a different number and has to work out which witness is lying. **Measured this session, the defect is four-wide, not one:** `src/scripts/check-mover-parity.js`, `check-rooms-parity.js` and `check-duel-parity.js` all print the identical `${a.length} bytes`; `check-terrain-parity.js`, `check-gate-parity.js` and `check-rng-parity.js` do not use that shape and are clean. **Fix:** say "chars", or print `Buffer.byteLength(a, 'utf8')` and keep the word — same one-word edit in all four. **Verify:** `npm run check:questparity` green with a label that matches `wc -c` on the extracted region.

### §DX-02dv — the §VM-01-D ship record reports a test figure that cannot be true (NEW 2026-08-21 during §DOC-02co, 🟢 one sentence in a HISTORY doc, NO DESIGN CALL)

Worse, it *names* the A/B/C baseline, and all three sibling ship records — §VM-01-A, §VM-01-B and §VM-01-C — record that baseline as **286 passed / 17 failed**. So either the figure is `286/17` mis-transcribed, or D genuinely ran green at 303 and the `286` is inherited. **Same family as §DX-02du/ds/dr** — a figure copied from a sibling record rather than re-measured — but this is the first instance where **the arithmetic alone falsifies it**, with no anchor lookup needed. **Context that makes the whole cluster stale:** `bd951d7` (2026-07-22 18:58, 8 h 24 min after `c22f4f0`) retired the 17-failure "env baseline" outright — *"it was 17 stale tests, not server clobber (17→1)"* — so **every §VM-01 ship record that cites a 17-fail baseline is describing a fiction that the repo audited out of existence the same working day.** At HEAD the suite is **303/303 green** (run this session). **Fix — one clause, plus an optional sweep:** (a) correct D's figure to whatever `9f10bfe` actually produced, or mark it unrecoverable and cite the HEAD figure; (b) optionally add a one-line note at the §VM-01-A/B/C records that the cited baseline was retired by `bd951d7` hours later. **Verify:** `npm run check:anchors` (docs-only); no code touched.
> **RESOLVED BY MEASUREMENT (§DOC-02cp, 2026-08-22) — the either/or is settled: the ship record dropped the 17, and the report did not.** `src/tests/integration/quest-runtime-uqf.test.js` holds **303** cases at `c22f4f0`, `9f10bfe`, `bd951d7` **and** HEAD (counted from the source at each blob, confirming the earlier `--list` measurement), and `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-vm01d-quest-core-parity.md` §5 states the target as **286 passed / 17 env-quirk failures**, which closes: 286 + 17 = 303. So `plan-archive.md`'s *"286 passed / 0 failed"* is a **transcription loss of the failure count**, not an inherited figure and not a genuinely-green run. **Apply fix (a) as `286/17`.** The optional sweep is worth doing and now has a measured hook: `bd951d7` (2026-07-22 18:58:33) retired the baseline **7 h 28 min** after `9f10bfe` (11:30:30) and **8 h 24 min** after `c22f4f0` (10:34:06) — one working day covers the whole §VM-01 A/B/C/D landing *and* the audit that dissolved the baseline all four of them reason through. At HEAD the suite is **303/303 green**, re-run this session (3.8 min).

### §DX-02dt — the game's only two authored `choice` bits have no test that names them (NEW 2026-08-21 during §DOC-02cm, 🟢 one test, NO DESIGN CALL)

Every assertion about suspend/resume in the suite builds its own two-option fixture at LHR; nothing pins the real scene. **Browser-proved during §DOC-02cm that it works today:** seeded at DUS, the verb renders 3 option buttons and parks the generator; before the pick `creativeLiteracyToken` is false; clicking *"That book is a warning. Not a manual."* sets `nexusQ02Complete`, leaves the unpicked `nexusQ01Active` **false**, grants the Creative Literacy Token, clears `let _uqfPending = null;@6824` and removes the panel. **Why it matters:** this scene is the entire visible payoff of the VM's `yield` — it is what *"the engine can ask"* means to a player — and it is the one `choice` chain whose authored option bits (three of them, one granting an item and completing a quest) could rot without a single gate noticing. The apply-only-after-the-pick property it demonstrates is also the invariant §VM-01-G4a's render-abandon repair leans on, so it deserves a pin of its own. **Fix:** add the case above to `src/tests/integration/uqf-verb-driver.test.js` (or a small `uqf-node-verbs-dus.test.js`), asserting the option count, the nothing-applied-on-the-way-in state, and the picked-branch-only outcome. **Verify:** the case must fail if either option's `bits` array is edited.

### §DX-02ds — §VM-01-A's own safety comment cites a line number that was wrong the moment it was written, by exactly the increment's own line delta (NEW 2026-08-21 during §DOC-02cm, 🟢 one comment line, NO DESIGN CALL)

At the commit that wrote that sentence, `storyAutoSave` was at 23313.** 🟢 **comment-only correction, no behaviour, no test change.** The claim is TRUE — `_uqfPending` appears 7 times in the file and not once in `_S_DEFAULTS`, `function storyAutoSave() {@23805`, or any `setItem` path — but its pointer is dead wrong, and the arithmetic says why. `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-vm01a-execbits-coroutine.md` §7 cites the autosave line as 23238 and **is correct for its parent build** `7f2f45d`. The ship commit `c22f4f0` inserted a net **+76** lines above that point (numstat +106/−30, A+B+C landed together), moving `storyAutoSave` to 23313. The author transcribed the report's pre-change anchor into a comment living in the post-change file and never re-resolved it: **23313 − 23237 = 76**, the increment's own delta. Today it has drifted to 23805 and the cited line resolves to `sbPapersRead: false` inside `_S_DEFAULTS` — a real-but-wrong line, which reads as authoritative and sends the next reader into the wrong object. **Same laundering class as §DX-02dr** (a HISTORY doc's figure copied into an engine comment, where it reads as a measurement) with a new and mechanically checkable vector: **an anchor measured on the PARENT build is stale in the CHILD by the change's own net insertion, and that number is knowable before you paste it.** **Fix:** rewrite the pointer as the `symbol@line` form the repo already gates — `` `function storyAutoSave() {@23805` `` — so `npm run check:anchors` can keep it honest instead of nobody. **Verify:** `npm run check:anchors` resolves it; the §6.3 claim itself needs no change.

Parsed with `src/js/wbapi-core.js:_scanTokens: scanTokens@829` the answer is **13 rich / 191 lean** at that build and at HEAD. **20** counts keys in the `birkaNpcs` literal, not cards: driven in Chromium at the parent build `fed5ee4^`, calling the renderer on all twenty mapped keys produced **11 cards and 9 silent blanks** (the nine §NPC-01-SF2 later fixed), so the true widening is **11 → 203**, which is larger and better than the comment claims. **Why file it rather than shrug:** a lab report is a HISTORY doc and is read as one; an engine comment is read as a statement about the code beside it. Moving an unverified number from the first into the second is the same laundering §AUDIT-03m-FU named — and here the undercount is not inert, because the three profiles the regex missed are exactly the class that §NPC-01-SF5 and §NPC-01-SF6 had to be written to rescue. **Fix:** correct both comment lines to 191 and 11 → 203, and cite the parser rather than a grep. **Verify:** `src/tests/integration/npc-card-map.test.js` already computes `richCount`/`leanCount` in-page at line 22 — pin them to 13 and 191 so the comment and the assertion cannot drift apart again.

### §DX-02cx — `NPC_DIALOGUES[*].meta.missionBit` has 209 declarations and zero readers, and a CI gate is trusting it (NEW 2026-08-18 during §DOC-02ca, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02cx — an authored grant mechanism that was specified in data and never built in code, now load-bearing for a gate's arithmetic.** 🟢 **wire it or delete it; either way stop crediting it.** The NPC dialogue registry carries a `missionBit:` field on its `meta` blocks — **209 declarations across 203 distinct flag names**, first landing at `rinaldo_sau: {@10461` in the 2026-06-04 *"DATA AUDIT — NPC dialogue cards"* batches. **The engine has zero readers of it.** All 212 occurrences of the token `missionBit` in `play.html` are either those declarations or the unrelated `S_story.inventory.filter(i => i.type === 'mission_bit')` in the journal renderer; there is no `.missionBit` property access anywhere, so nothing ever calls `function _grantMissionBit(@26109` on their behalf. The field describes a grant that does not happen.
> **It would be harmless residue except that `check:questgraph` believes it.** `src/scripts/check-questgraph.js:// NPC/data mission-bit grant@309` folds all 203 into the host-writer pool *"(→ `_grantMissionBit` → `S_story[flag]=true`)"*, which is precisely the call that is missing. **97 of the 203 have no writer of any kind**, and 7 of those are load-bearing act-2 gates in the `waw` family — see §AUDIT-03bj, where removing this one regex reproduces the true census exactly. ***A dead data field is cheap; a dead data field a gate treats as evidence is a false negative in a correctness check.***
> **Two options.** (a) **Wire it** — a hook at the NPC-dialogue surface that calls `_grantMissionBit(meta.missionBit, …)` when the card is shown. This is probably what was intended, it fits the ontology (`function _gateFlagSet() {@26133` already treats these as first-class), and it would unlock 7 quests directly. But it fires 203 grants at surfaces nobody has re-read, so it needs a content pass. (b) **Delete the field** and let §AUDIT-03bj's relabel carry the 7. **Recommend (b) for the gate fix now and (a) as a separate, scoped content increment** — the gate must stop lying either way, and the two changes have very different blast radii. **Verify:** `grep -c 'missionBit' play.html` drops to 3 (the journal filter) under (b); `check:questgraph`'s host-writer count falls by 203 and its `written-by-nothing` rises to 57, then to 0 after §AUDIT-03bj.

### §DX-02cj — the `itemChain` grant allow-list is kept in lockstep across four files by a comment, and nothing checks it (NEW 2026-08-17 during §DOC-02bs, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02cj — `GRANT_RICH` appears in exactly one file, and no gate compares it to the runtime list it must mirror.** 🟢 **one gate function, ~20 minutes.** §EDITOR-01-D-FU(b1) widened the `itemChain` grant shape to carry rich item fields (`readText`, `bonus`, weapon stats, …) and its lab report named **four** sites that must move together: the runtime allow-list (`for (const f of ['desc', 'description'@26182`), the text codec, the (a) widget schema (`edit.html:const GRANT_RICH = [@8607`), and `src/scripts/check-itemchain.js`. **The lockstep is enforced by the comment that says "kept in lockstep" and by nothing else** — `grep -rn GRANT_RICH src/scripts/ src/tests/` returns **zero hits**, and `check:itemchain` never loads `edit.html`. A field added to the runtime but not to `GRANT_RICH` is silently unauthorable in the editor; a field added to `GRANT_RICH` but not to the runtime is silently dropped on apply — the §DX-02at class (`OPERAND_CONTRACTS` vs `BIT_CONTRACTS`), one layer down, and the same failure shape as §DX-02ch's POST field list. **It has not drifted yet, and that is luck plus discipline, not coverage:** `heal` was added to all four by §KG Increment 3 (`d6aeefd`, 2026-07-08) by an author on an unrelated track, 11 days after b1 shipped. **Fix:** in `check-itemchain.js`, read `GRANT_RICH` + `CHAIN_KINDS.grant` out of `edit.html` textually and assert `GRANT_RICH ∪ {scalar grant fields}` equals the runtime allow-list ∪ `{name,icon,type,sell,once,silent}` exactly, failing with the offending field names. **Risk:** none (read-only gate). **Verify:** `npm run check:itemchain` green; a scratch edit adding a field to one side only must go red.

The compiler, its header comment and its spec were all corrected then; only the archive was not. **The four sites in `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-editor02-mission-builder.md` were corrected by §DOC-02bq itself** and need no further edit. **Fix:** annotate the three rows — *"(superseded: this encoding was inert; `activateCond()` is called zero-arg — replaced by `gate:{flags}` at W8b `11af1e5`)"*. `plan-archive.md` is HISTORY: annotate, don't rewrite (§DX-02c / §AUDIT-03n precedent). **Same family as §DX-02ce** — older prose asserts, newer prose corrects, nothing reconciles them; machines back-propagate and prose has to be pushed. **Verify:** `grep -n "(s)=>s\." plan-archive.md` returns only annotated instances.
