Its line range — **25835–26054** — is byte-exact at the reference build `8560254^` (25835 is the first branch, 26054 the last), and the count between them is **61**, at that commit and at every `play.html` commit that day. **58 was never true.** The figure was copied forward into **`index.md:168`**, **`plan-archive.md:70`**, **`plan-archive.md:123`** and **`docs/lab-reports/lab-report-editor01d-fu-chain-ui.md:5`**. **Three independent sources in this repo say 61**, all of them younger than the error: the follow-up design lock `lab-report-editor01d-fu-b-ladder-migration.md` (*"a full classification of all 61 branches"*, `27956e4`, ~22 h later), the parity guard it built (`src/scripts/check-ladder-migration.js:5` header + the live `` `src/scripts/check-ladder-migration.js:ladder branches@265` `` tally), and §ARCH-01 Wave 7c's own commit subject (`a79c76a`, *"the 61-id hardcoded effects block … DELETED"*). **Why it still matters after the structure is gone:** all four wrong figures describe a ladder that **no longer exists** — `a79c76a` folded it into per-quest UQF `onComplete` bit chains and `f8691c1` deleted the legacy `_rollCeremonia` body, so HEAD carries **0** reward-ladder branches (the six surviving `if (id === …)` lines are `msub-*` mesh-UI panes) and **105** `onComplete` entries, **all array-valued**. The engine records the correct number in its own comment: `` `W7c folded the per-id hardcoded effects block (61 ids@30193` ``. **The lesson the row exists to record: a census error survives its own correction when the correction lands in a gate and the prose is never pushed.** **Fix:** correct the four figures to *"61-branch (retired 2026-07-03 by §ARCH-01 W7c)"*; the lab reports are HISTORY, so **annotate, don't rewrite** (§DX-02c / §AUDIT-03n precedent) — the itemchain report is already annotated by §DOC-02bp and needs no further edit, leaving `fu-chain-ui.md:5` as the one report-side annotation. **Related, not duplicate:** §DX-02cd already owns `index.md:168`'s *other* stale figure on the same line (`check:itemchain` **19 → 29**) — **do both edits in one pass, same line.** **Verify:** `grep -rn "58-branch\|58 branch" index.md plan-archive.md docs/lab-reports/` returns only annotated instances; `npm run check:laddermigration` still reports `0 ladder branches`. **⟶ PARTIAL 2026-08-17 (§DOC-02br): the report-side half is DONE.** `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-editor01d-fu-chain-ui.md:5` is now annotated in that file's §14 (61, not 58; 0 since `a79c76a`), and the row's own arithmetic was re-confirmed independently — `npm run check:laddermigration` reports **148/148 checks, 0 ladder branches** at HEAD. Also newly measured: the corpus's shortest correction interval yet — **8 min 24 s**. This report was committed at `ec34e9e` 16:57:04 still saying 58; the sibling lock `27956e4` landed at 17:05:28 saying 61. The row's "~22 h" is correct for the *original* §EDITOR-01-D report; for **this** one the right answer existed before the ink dried, and it still propagated no further. **Remaining: the three non-report figures — `index.md:168` (do in the SAME pass as §DX-02cd, which owns the other stale figure on that identical line) · `plan-archive.md:70` · `plan-archive.md:123`.**

### §DX-02cd — prompt.md §7 names one of the five gates CI runs, and the four it omits are the ones a lab-report author is most likely to break (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bo, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02cd — the repo's own verify cheat-sheet is missing four green gates, so "I ran the checks" and "CI will pass" are different statements.** 🟢 **doc-only + one figure fix, ~10 minutes.** `.github/workflows/walk-invariants.yml`'s `invariants` job runs **five** stdlib-only steps: `npm run check:walk`, then `check:arraypatch` (§WBAPI-01 ph3), `check:itemchain` (§EDITOR-01-D), `check:laddermigration` (§EDITOR-01-D-FU(b)) and `check:worlddiff` (§MESH-01-FU 9). **prompt.md §7 names only the first**, and `check:walk`'s own definition in `package.json` chains sixteen gates, none of which is any of the other four. All four are green at HEAD when run by hand (13, 29, 148 checks and "all assertions hold" respectively) — this is a coverage-of-documentation gap, not a red. **Why it bites:** all four are triggered by the same push paths as `check:walk` (`play.html`, `src/js/wbapi-core.js`, `src/js/wbapi-server.js`), so an author who verifies exactly as §7 instructs can push a change that fails CI in a step they were never told existed — the §DX-02ca failure mode in a different job, and cheaper to close because nothing is broken yet. **Fix:** add the four to prompt.md §7's fenced verify block with one-line purposes, and say plainly that `check:walk` is a *subset* of the CI invariants job. **Same row carries a figure correction:** `index.md:168` records `npm run check:itemchain` as **19 checks**; its own output says **29** (`✓ §EDITOR-01-D itemChain: all 29 checks pass`). `index.md:167`'s *"13 checks"* for `check:arraypatch` is exact and stays. **Related, not duplicate:** §DX-02ca is a gate that is actually red and undocumented as such; this is four gates that are green and undocumented at all. **Verify:** the four commands appear in prompt.md §7; each exits 0; `index.md`'s itemChain figure matches the gate's printed total.

### §DX-02cc — the ph3 PATCH gate asserts the branch that had 191 consumers and not the branch that has 3,012 (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bo, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02cc — `check:arraypatch` never exercises a plain-object field, which is the shape the path's own follow-up fix existed for.** 🟢 **two checks, ~15 minutes.** `src/scripts/check-array-patch.js` (13 checks, green at HEAD) was written on 2026-06-26 when the server's PUT dispatch still routed **plain objects** to the in-memory-only `ns.put` — so the gate's object coverage is `killGoals`, an *array of* objects, and nothing else. **§MATH-01 (`32d7bb0`, 2026-07-07) moved bare objects onto `editStructuredField`** after they were found being silently lost on file-watch reload (its own commit message: *"PUT routed plain-object fields (gate/completion) to a memory-only path"*), and **added no assertion**. Measured through `wbapi-core` at HEAD, objects are now the **larger** population: top-level `gate` on **2,823** quests and `completion` on **189**, against `bits` 2,823 / `onComplete` 105 / `itemChain` 27 / `targetMonsterKeys` 12 / `killGoals` 11 on the array side, plus 47 `battle:{…}` and one `finalBattle:{…}` on `NODE_MAP`. The path works — a bare `completion:{itemsAll:['…'],atNode:'LHR'}` and a four-level nest both serialize, patch and round-trip exactly when replayed by hand — so this is **coverage, not a red**; the point is that the one gate standing between §ARCH-01's entire quest shape and a silent write regression asserts the branch that has been *shrinking* since it was written. **Fix:** two checks in the existing file — patch a bare object field, reload, compare; patch a nested one, reload, compare — plus a line in the gate's header comment recording the §MATH-01 widening the way line 20 already records the §ARCH-01 `completeItems` repoint. **Related, not duplicate:** §DX-02h gave `WORLD_DB.monsters` its own writer precisely because the generic serializer is *wrong* there (it holds `P.<key>` code identifiers, not JSON) — that row is closed and its guard is separate. **Verify:** `npm run check:arraypatch` reports 15 checks and exits 0; both new checks fail against a stubbed `serializeJsLiteral` that drops object values (positive control).

### §DX-02ca — the MUD harness is red, `npm test:mud` exits 1, and the assertion that broke is the one that closed the §WALK series (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bm, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02ca — a keep-alive that arrives late prunes the session it was sent to keep, and the harness gives it under 300 ms of margin.** 🟢 **one constant + three doc figures, ~20 minutes.** `npm run test:mud` exits **1** at HEAD — **267 passed / 2 failed**, deterministic across three consecutive runs — and the two failures are section **[D]**, the idle-TTL-prune property that §WALK-5 Inc 4 (`8a280af`) added to close the §WALK series: *"after idle TTL, exactly one session survives"* and *"Warm (kept active) survives; Ghost (idle) is pruned"*. **The prune is correct; the stopwatch is not.** Re-created in isolation the same scenario passes (one survivor, Warm); instrumented inside the full run, `/session/who` returns **count 0** — *both* sessions were reaped. `` `src/js/wbapi-server.js:function sessionPrune()@93` `` runs at the **top of every `/api/session/*` request**, before the `look` handler refreshes `lastSeen` — which is the behaviour you want, since a client silent for longer than the TTL *should* be pruned by its own late ping. But `src/tests/mud-harness.mjs` section [D] sets `TTL_MS = 700` with keep-alive `look`s on a 280 ms cadence, and by [D] the harness has five node servers live. **Measured round-trips in one instrumented run: 217 ms, 463 ms and 644 ms** — the second keep-alive was therefore issued **744 ms** after the first, past the TTL, so Warm's own keep-alive killed it. The margin has not changed since 2026-06-26; the harness has grown **11×** (24 → 269 assertions, 251 → 1,447 lines) around it. **Consequence beyond the red:** the `mud` job in `.github/workflows/walk-invariants.yml` is a *separate* CI job (`npm ci` + `test:mud`), so **CI fails on any push touching `src/js/mover.js`, `src/js/wbapi-server.js`, `play.html` or the harness itself** — and it is not in the recorded baseline-red set, so a future session will read it as their own regression. **Fix:** give [D] real headroom — raise its `TTL_MS` (3,000 ms costs ~2 s of wall clock) and/or touch Warm once immediately after `start` so the first interval is not the slow one; do **not** touch `sessionPrune`. **Also correct three live docs that record the harness as `269/269`** — `BACKLOG.md:147`, `potential.md:173`, `plan-archive.md` — all written before the red. `index.md:179`'s *"harness 24/24"* is historically correct for Inc 4 and stays. **Verify:** `npm run test:mud` exits 0 with 269/269, three consecutive runs; the [D] section still fails if `sessionPrune`'s selectivity is broken on purpose (positive control — pin the property, not the wall clock).

### §AUDIT-03be — six sites certify that Hunt Mode was removed, and Hunt Mode is live (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bl, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03be — §TIMELESS-01 deleted Hunt Mode on 2026-06-26; §KG-01 re-added it under the identical identifiers 12 days later, and nothing updated the paperwork of the first.** 🟢 **prose + one engine comment, ~15 minutes.** `7952752` (§TIMELESS-01 Inc B) removed the whole Hunt/Stalk subsystem and left ten labelled tombstone comments; `1d6263a` (Inc D) and `790d4f3` (the FU) swept the docs to match. Then `8168f0e` (§KG-01, 2026-07-08) shipped a **different** mechanic — `baseRate × 2` capped at 0.8 plus an 80/20 bias toward monsters at or below player level, no clock cost — reusing `huntMode`, `storyToggleHunt`, `_updateHuntBtn` and a 🎯 d-pad button verbatim. **The six false sites:** (1) the engine comment on line 38113, *"§TIMELESS-01: `_updateHuntBtn` / `storyToggleHunt` removed with the Hunt feature"*, which sits **39 and 28 lines below** `` `function _updateHuntBtn()@38074` `` and `` `function storyToggleHunt()@38085` `` — both live; (2) `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md:690`, which lists `storyToggleHunt`, `_updateHuntBtn` and the `huntMode` state field under *"Removed code/state"*; (3) `docs/notes/docs-node-network.md:306`, *"no Hunt-Mode toggle — `S_story.huntMode` … removed in §TIMELESS-01"*; (4) `index.md:409`, the reverse-lookup row **Hunt Mode / stalk *(retired §TIMELESS-01)***; (5) `index.md:523`, *"Updated 2026-06-26 (§TIMELESS-01 removed `huntMode`)"*; and (6) **a separate defect in the same paragraph** — `mechanics-combat.md:688` states *"movement and battle no longer advance the clock"*, but **battle always did** by §TIMELESS-01's own D1 decision, and still does at `` `function _storyRollInit()@24624` `` (line 24650); the engine's own comment on line 28366 says only *movement* is timeless. **Only the Stalk half of the removal is real:** `storyQuestHunt`, `storyQuickWait`, `btn-hunt-toggle`, `btn-stalk-wait`, `btn-stalk-abandon`, `stalk-card` are all 0 occurrences, and `HUNTING_GROUNDS` / `_getQuestTargetKeys` / `_stalkedMonsterPick` survive only inside tombstones. **Fix:** rewrite tombstone 38113 to say the functions were removed by §TIMELESS-01 and reinstated by §KG-01 with different semantics (do not delete it — it is the only in-engine record of the first removal); split the four doc sites into a *retired (June)* / *live (July)* pair naming both mechanics; correct `:688` to *movement* only. **Related, not duplicate:** §AUDIT-03ba is stale prose beside correct code; this is prose that was correct when written and was falsified by a later commit reusing the vocabulary — the failure mode a two-way-sync rule cannot catch, because §KG-01 *did* document its own feature. **Verify:** no live doc or comment asserts `huntMode` is absent; a reader can tell the June mechanic from the July one. **⟶ EXTENDED 2026-08-14 (§DOC-02bm) — a SEVENTH site, already handled, and it is the most interesting of the seven.** `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-walk5-mud-harness.md` §8 said *"**No `huntMode`.** Anything in older notes implying a guaranteed-encounter mode is stale post-§TIMELESS-01"* — written **three hours** after §TIMELESS-01 shipped, by an author reading the file. It has been **annotated in place** by that report's §DOC-02bm rewrite, not added to this row's fix list. Two refinements it contributes: (a) the *server*-side half of the claim is still exactly true (`git log -S "s.huntMode" --all` returns **two commits, and both are lab reports** — the identifier was never in any engine), and (b) *"there is no guaranteed-encounter mode"* is **also still true**, because §KG-01's Hunt Mode is a doubling capped at 0.8, never a certainty. **Only the blanket sentence failed.** That is the shape to look for while sweeping the other six: the load-bearing error is usually a bare *"no X"* laid over a correct paragraph, not the paragraph.

### §AUDIT-03bf — `index.md` says it lists "All 193" state fields; the function has 493 and the table has 208 (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bl, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03bf — the State Fields Quick Reference claims completeness it has never had, and the claim was wrong the day it was written.** 🟢 **one sentence.** `index.md:523` reads *"Moved from `index.md`. All 193 `S_story` fields from `_S_DEFAULTS()`. Updated 2026-06-26 (§TIMELESS-01 removed `huntMode`)."* By `git log -S`, that sentence entered the repo in **`1d6263a` — §TIMELESS-01 Inc D**, whose scope line included *"state-field count"*. **At that commit a brace-depth walk of `` `const _S_DEFAULTS = ()@23062` `` returns 486 top-level fields, and the doc's own table listed 205 `S_story.` rows.** 193 matched neither, so this is a **never-correct** figure rather than drift — the number appears to have been carried in rather than measured. At HEAD the walk returns **493** (375 of them corroborated by an `S_story.<key>` read elsewhere in the file; the remainder are flag fields reached through the gate compiler or dynamic indexing) and the table lists **208** rows. **The load-bearing error is the word *"All"***: the reference has never covered more than ~42 % of `_S_DEFAULTS()`, and a reader who trusts it will conclude a field is undeclared when it simply is not tabulated. **Fix:** replace with a measured, self-dating sentence — *"the N most commonly referenced of the M fields in `_S_DEFAULTS()`; not exhaustive"* — and, since both numbers rot, prefer pointing at `npm run stats` or adding a field count to it over hardcoding M. **Note the second half of the same line is also now false** — *"§TIMELESS-01 removed `huntMode`"* — and is covered by §AUDIT-03be; fix both in one edit. **Instrument 66 in its home habitat:** the one figure an author copies out of a *document* instead of the *file* is the one that is wrong, and a count beside the words "All" and a function name reads as measured whether or not it was. **Verify:** the sentence states a measured count or defers to a generator, and does not claim exhaustiveness.

### §AUDIT-03bd — CI gate #1 counts 110 of the world's 111 terrains, because its key pattern cannot see a quoted key (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bk, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03bd — `check:invariants` is one authored node away from a false red on the gate that proves the world is walkable.** 🟢 **one regex, ~5 minutes.** `src/scripts/check-invariants.js:127` collects `WORLD_DB`'s top-level terrain keys with `` /^ {2}([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)\s*:/ `` — bare identifiers only. `WORLD_DB` holds **111** entries by a brace-depth walk and by `npm run stats`; the gate reports **110**. The missing one is the single quoted key, `'bar (Visby)':` — the Broken Tooth Tavern terrain. **Nothing is red today** because no node currently carries `name:'bar (Visby)'`, so I1's *"node references a terrain absent from `WORLD_DB`"* check finds nothing to complain about. Author one node onto that terrain and gate #1 fails, blaming the node. **The tell that this is a known class and was guarded on the other side only:** the very next scan in the same file — the NODE_MAP terrain pass at `src/scripts/check-invariants.js:142` — carries an explicit staleness guard (*"only parsed N node terrains for M coord'd nodes — regex likely stale"*). The WORLD_DB side has no such guard, so its blindness is silent by construction. **Fix:** widen the pattern to `` /^ {2}('[^']+'|[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)\s*:/ `` and strip the quotes, then add the same count-sanity guard the NODE_MAP side already has (fail if the parsed key count diverges from a brace-depth count). **Instrument 51 in a live CI gate:** a census is only as good as the pattern that took it, and the entry written in the *older* style — the one quoted key, dating to the era when terrain keys carried spaces — is the one the newer pattern cannot see.

### §AUDIT-03aw — two whole shipped arcs, 15 quests and the game's only companion have no row in any maintained home doc (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02as, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03aw — the two-way-sync rule failed silently for §WISDOM-01 and §ALCHEMY-01, and the index still files one of them as future work.** prompt.md §2 step 6 and CONTRIBUTING's two-way-sync rule are hard: *"every changed structure updates its home doc in the same increment."* Measured this pass: **`quest.md` contains 0 of `quest_wis_00`–`_07` and 0 of `quest_alch_01`–`_07`** — 15 shipped UQF quests with no register row — and **`quest.md`, `story.md`, `world.md` and `mechanics.md` return 0 hits each for `Roen`, `Ardley` and `ALCHEMY-01`.** This is not a general gap in `quest.md` (934 lines): its siblings from the same fortnight are all there — `quest_vs_01` ×1, `quest_wm_01` ×1, `quest_spark_05` ×3, `quest_courier_release` ×3. **`index.md:175` is the sharpest single line:** `| **§WISDOM-01** | — | Keel thread close: Baltic survey data arc at eastern Baltic node; "after witnessing" arc completion |` — a *planned-work* row, in the present tense, for an arc that shipped **2026-05-28** at `e339aeb` and whose Keel thread (`quest_wis_03`) has been live for 77 days. `index.md:399` then points the reader at `plan-archive.md §WISDOM-01` — **the arc's only record is in the HISTORY file**, so the shipping event and the archival happened without the register in between.
> **Why it matters more than a missing row:** Roen is the game's **only companion character** and these two arcs are his whole through-line (self-knowledge → reading other people). A cold agent greping `world.md` for the companion system finds nothing, and `quest.md` — the location-organized register prompt.md names as the home doc for quests — reports that Tilbury, Saltwick and the Intercept have no wisdom content on them. That is the §AUDIT-03z thesis from the other side: *a bijection guarantees a link, not a truth* — here **there is no link at all**, which is the one shape a doc-anchor gate cannot fail on, because a missing row has no anchor to resolve.
> **Fix (🟢, no design call):** add the 15 rows to `quest.md` under their live nodes (`LCY`/`MME`/`GCI`/`BK`→`VBY`/`ATH`/`VS`/`KIR`), a §Roen section to `world.md`, and correct `index.md:175` from a planned row to a shipped one. **Mark the reachability honestly while you do it** — §DOC-02u's §SIREN-01 lesson is that `quest.md` saying *"✅ LIVE"* about a quest no player can reach is worse than silence, and all 8 §WISDOM-01 quests plus 5 of 7 §ALCHEMY-01 quests are currently unreachable (§AUDIT-03x). **Measure the corpus before closing:** two arcs found while verifying one report is not a search — census every `QUEST_DB` id prefix against `quest.md` and report the full set of unregistered arcs, which is a two-line script and the natural seed for a `check:questdoc` phase of §DX-02s's proposed `check:docindex`.

### §DX-02w — no gate can see an `activateNode` that resolves but can never be stood on (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02r)

- [ ] **§DX-02w — `check:cellprimacy`, the gate phase §AUDIT-03x has been asking for since 2026-08-11.** Every existing gate passes on a co-located node: `check:noderegs` proves the code *resolves* in `NODE_MAP`, and it does; `check:questgraph` walks `QUEST_DB` against itself; `check:legacycodes` scans `.md` only. Nothing tests the property that actually matters — whether `S_story.currentCode` can ever hold that code. `const CELL_GRID = (() => {@9852` builds each cell in `NODE_MAP` declaration order and only `list[0]` is reachable, because `currentCode` is assigned at exactly two sites (`S_story.currentCode = destCode;@28373`, `S_story.checkpointNode || 'LHR'@26009`).
> **Wanted:** a phase that fails on any `activateNode` / `waypointNode` / `node.code === '…'` guard naming a code that is not `list[0]` in its cell, with **explicit classification** (a `CO_LOCATED_BY_DESIGN` list with a reason per entry) in the #13/#14/#16 house style — a percentage heuristic is blind to a cell that is *entirely* non-primary, and cell `32,203` holds **17 nodes**.
> **Second phase worth the same pass — the transitive one, and §DOC-02r's 19th instrument:** a quest on a *live* node can still be unreachable when its gate flag has exactly one writer and that writer sits inside a blocked node's `storyRender` block. `quest_wis_01`/`_02`/`_03` sit on live `LCY`/`MME`/`GCI` and are dead behind `S_story.wisHookReceived = true;@33520`. **A single-writer census plus the primacy map finds these; nothing else does.**
> **Baseline for the gate when it lands:** 416 nodes / 244 cells / 172 non-primary (§AUDIT-03x, re-confirmed — and re-confirmed a **third** time by §DOC-02u's independent closure, which reproduced all three figures exactly).
> **§AUDIT-03x extended by §DOC-02z — cell `10,191` is the second cell that must be scored BY CELL, and the layer's hub is a rest node nobody can stand on.** The naval layer's four blocked hosts are `SEN`←`LCY` (cell 18,180), `HFT` and `VAW`←`ALF` (cell 10,191), `ATH`←`SEA` (32,203). **Cell `10,191` holds 6 nodes from 2 independent tracks and strands 5**: the whole lake sub-map — `ALF` *North Shore Path* (primary), `HVG` *East Coast — Upper Shore*, `HFT` *South Shore — The Fishermen's Village*, `VAW` *North Shore Den* — **plus `ODD` *Oddrun's Estate* and `SIG` *Siggeir's Hall*, two Völsunga-saga nodes with no relation to the lake.** That is §DOC-02x's *"change the unit — score by cell, not by arc"* rule reproduced in a second cell. **`SEN` *Aboard the Tilbury Star* is the sharpest single node measured so far**: `SEN:{ num:10, code:'SEN'@8638` carries **8 `activateNode` quests**, `battle:{label:'Pirate ×3 + Ghost'`, `loot:'Cargo Manifest'`, `npc:'Ship Captain'` **and `sleep:true, sleepCost:3` — so it is a Warrant's Board host** (`function _boardHost(node)@37038` keys on `node.sleep`) **that no player can ever reach.** Its 8 quests are §WHODUNIT-01's four, §SPARK-01 SEA's head, §ALCHEMY-01's ship beat, and **`quest_spark_03` + `quest_spark_04` — two beats of §SPARK-01 itself**, so the parent arc is a casualty too. Third occupant of 18,180 is `STN`, the Map Shop that carries §AUDIT-03u's *"forty-two nodes"* string. **New this pass: §ALCHEMY-01 loses 5 of 7** (`roenAtSea`'s sole writer is `if (node.code === 'SEN' && S_story.roenMidlandsWisdom && !S_story.roenAtSea) {@33380`), and **the §BOARD-01-FU6 referral graph loses its one geo-spanning DIAMOND APEX** — `quest_hunt_01` can activate and can never complete, so `{ kind:'unlock', quests:['sq_2','quest_hunt2_01'] }@12902` never fires (both arms survive independently; what is lost is the sentence that makes them one pattern).

> **§AUDIT-03x — CHEAPEST INSTANCE IDENTIFIED 2026-08-12 (§DOC-02ai), and it needs no design call.** `ATH`←`SEA` (cell **`32,203`**, the 17-node cell) has been on this row since §DOC-02r/§DOC-02z as one line in a casualty list; §DOC-02ai measured what it costs and found the fix is **two lines, not a locale decision**. Both nodes belong to the SAME authored block, **ten lines apart** — `` `SEA:{ num:97, code:'SEA'@8477` `` is declared before `` `ATH:{ num:92, code:'ATH'@8487` ``, and `CELL_GRID` builds each cell in `NODE_MAP` declaration order, so the arc's own **sea** node is the primary of Athens's cell. The bill: `` `quest_areopagus: { id:'quest_areopagus'@11461` `` carries `activateNode:'ATH'` and **cannot activate**, so the Areopagus speech — the §FUTURE-01 arc's intellectual climax and the only scene in it calibrated for a non-Jewish audience — never fires; `ATH` is `sleep:true, sleepCost:5`, a **checkpoint that can never be set**; and a player who walks to Athens arrives at *"The Inner Sea"*, a shipboard scene, on dry land in Achaia. **Unlike every other member of this row, repointing is not the question and co-location is not load-bearing** — nothing wants a sea node and a city on one cell; swapping the two declarations (or giving `SEA` its own water-adjacent cell) restores Athens without touching the locale design. **Do this one ahead of the gate.**
> **And the closure runs in the POSITIVE direction too — §DOC-02z's method note.** Before writing a quest off, check `unlock` edges *and* the Warrant's Board: `_bountyPostable` never tests primacy, so a board-postable quest whose completion lands on a **primary** node is fully playable. That is what keeps §SPARK-01 SEA alive (`quest_sea_01` gate `{}`, `xp:100` under the Unknown tier's 250 cap, `completion:{ atNode:'NWI' }`). **The gate must therefore report a stranded node's quests as *board-only*, not as dead** — and it depends on §DX-02ah, which is the surface that makes the board route work at all.
> **§AUDIT-03x extended by §DOC-02u — the FIRST 100 % CASUALTY, and the case that most argues for this gate.** §SIREN-01 (the Littoral Courts) loses **6 of 6 surviving nodes and 5 of 5 quests**: `LC1`/`LC2`/`LC3`/`LC4`/`LSO` are the 3rd–7th occupants of cell `32,203` (17 nodes, `list[0]` = `SEA` — the same cell §DOC-02r found stranding `ATH`), and `LCA:{r:35,c:213},@9812` is 2nd of 2 behind `CI2`. Previous casualties left *something* standing — §CROWN-01 kept WG0 and INN reachable out of 9 nodes, §DOC-02r's prosocial family kept 23 of 51 quests. **Here there is no entry point, no partial experience and no arc-close: `{ id:'story-lca-close', nodes:['LCA'],@31350` cannot render either, so the arc cannot even end.** ***And nothing in the repo says so*** — `quest.md` lists all five as **"✅ LIVE §SIREN-01"**, which is true of `QUEST_DB` and false of the game. That is precisely the true/false split this gate exists to make legible, and it is the strongest single argument for building it: the arc is 100 % dead, every gate is green, and the docs say it works.

### §DX-02ad — `quest.md` calls itself the register of *all* quests and names 148 of 2,853 (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02x, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02ad — a bijection that shipped, was declared canonical, and quietly stopped being true; §DOC-02i's FC01 class in a second subject.** `quest.md`'s own Purpose line reads *"Location-organized register of **all quests** — implemented, planned, and specced,"* and `lab-report-meta-process-loop-expansion.md` recommendation 8 is where the claim was written down (*"The register is the canonical source of truth for quest IDs, locations, and act structure"*). Measured through the canonical `wbapi-core` parse — the same extractor `:1367` and every `src/scripts/check-*.js` use: **`QUEST_DB` holds 2,853 entries across 57 id prefixes; `quest.md` names 148 of them (5.2 %).** Broken down: the hand-authored `quest_*` family is **145 of 298 (48.7 %)**, and the other **56 families hold 2,555 quests of which 3 are named** (`ada` 235 · `ath` 120 · `lis` 95 · `zth` 80 · `flr` 75 · `blq` 60 · `kya` 60 · `hft`/`rkv`/`ost`/`rix`/`jrs` 55 each · …). **⟶ SECOND SUBJECT, SAME SHAPE, 2026-08-12 (§DOC-02ab).** `docs/story/story-arc-npc-dialogues.md` headed its transcript *"Source: `NPC_DIALOGUES` const … 6 NPCs × 4 states × 5 quotes = 120 lines"* while `const NPC_DIALOGUES = {@10396` holds **213 entries and 1,614 quotes** — a complete and accurate transcript of **2.8 %** of the table, wrong only by omission, so every spot-check passes. Its per-NPC arithmetic was off too: all six Birka pools are 5/5/5/**4**, so the true figure is **114**, not 120. Both corrected in place. **So the gate this row wants should be scoped to *any doc that declares itself the register of a named data section*, not to `quest.md` alone.** Cheap general form: if a doc's own prose names a `const` **and** a count, re-derive the count from that `const` through `wbapi-core` and fail on a mismatch.
> **The reverse direction is SPOTLESS, and that is why nobody has caught it.** Thirteen quest-shaped tokens in `quest.md` fail to resolve in `QUEST_DB` and **not one is a dead row**: ten are deliberate annotations of the form `` `quest_d0207_a1–a5` *(design: quest_cy_madness_gate)* `` — live id first, design-era name preserved beside it, which is the same discipline that let §DOC-02k score §DUNGEON-01 rather than guess at it — and the other three are prose fragments. ***So the register is wrong only by OMISSION and never by ASSERTION: everything it says is true, and it says it about one quest in nineteen.*** That is the hardest staleness to notice, because every spot-check passes — the §DOC-02i mechanism exactly (*a check that depends on a human running it reports green when nobody ran it*).
> **Fix — the cheapest gate in the family, a two-way `comm` over the parser's own key list.** `check:questindex`: `WBAPI.load()` → `Object.keys(questDb)` vs the ids `quest.md` cites. Two explicit classification lists, both in the §AUDIT-03j house style rather than a heuristic: **`NOT_A_QUEST_ID`** for the `*(design: …)*` aliases and prose fragments, and a **generated-family exemption list** naming each bulk prefix with a reason, so the register is scored against the population it is actually meant to cover. **The exemption must be written down, not inferred** — a percentage heuristic is blind to a family that is *entirely* generated, which is the same argument gates #13/#14/#16 each had to make.
> **Then decide the register's scope in one sentence and put it in `quest.md`'s Purpose line**, because "all quests" has not been true since the bulk families landed and the doc has never said so.

### §DX-02br — `navigation.test.js` suppresses encounters by stubbing the RNG the code stopped drawing (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02az, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02br — the third recurrence of the same lesson, in the file that was rebuilt to fix the second.** `src/tests/integration/navigation.test.js`'s `moveNoEncounter(page, dir)` helper wraps `cellMove` in `Math.random = () => 1` with the comment *"Suppress the per-step terrain encounter roll (Math.random >= rate → no battle)"*, and the auto-travel block pins `Math.random = () => 0.999999` for the same purpose. **The roll is `if (_seededNext() < baseRate)` inside `function _enterEmptyCell(r, c)@28420`** — §VM-01-B moved it onto the seeded stream, and `function _seededNext()@6434` draws `S_story.rngState` through mulberry32 and **never touches `Math.random`**. The stub controls nothing. It is also restored synchronously after `cellMove`, while the battle fires from a `setTimeout(…, 300)`, so it would be ineffective even if it were the right stream. The affected tests pass today only because the terrain rates involved are 0.05–0.25 and the assertions land before the timer.
> **Blast radius:** every `moveNoEncounter` call site (empty-cell shell, room layer, D-pad, sea-lane walk) plus *"WP click travels NUE → MGR (3 cells)"* and *"Shift+WP takes a single step"*. The one test that genuinely does suppress is *"a wilderness encounter halts auto-travel"* — it forces `TERRAIN_ENCOUNTER_RATE[k] = 1`, which works regardless of stream. **Fix:** suppress through the engine's own dial the way §DX-02f did — set the rates to 0 (or seed `S_story.rngState` to a value whose next draw is known) — never by stubbing a global the code no longer reads. ***A test that stubs an RNG must stub the stream the code actually draws*** (§DX-02e pinned generated coordinates, §DX-02f stubbed the wrong stream, this is the third). Also in the same file: the comment *"The invariant suite proves full reachability offline (409/409)"* is stale — the figure is **416/416**.

### §DX-02bs — `check:questgraph`'s host-writer scan does not know the `once:` idiom (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02ba, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02bs — the gate reports a soft-lock that is not one, because one writer class is invisible to it.** `check:questgraph` folds **3,575 host flag-writers** (combat / arrival / fishing / NPC-grant / readable) into its write pool before deciding what is unreachable, and it still lists **`saulConverted`** among the 50 `written-by-nothing` candidates. It *is* written: the story-stanza renderer sets it at `if (p.once) st[p.once] = true;@31599`, driven by the `NODE_PANELS` entry `{ id:'story-ks-conversion', nodes:['DAM'], when:st => !st.saulConverted, once:'saulConverted' }`. There are **5 `once:'…'` flags** in the file; `saulConverted` is the one that is also gate-read (`quest_anath`'s `gate:{flags:['saulConverted']}`, plus `quest_road_damascus`'s completion), so it is the only one that surfaces as a false positive today.
> **Why it matters:** the §LIX Damascus conversion is the *entry* to the arc §DOC-02ba was verifying, so the false positive lands on live, reachable, shipped content — and it is the second gate on the same door (the first, `anathSightRestored`, is correctly seen because the NPC-grant scanner covers `quoteFn` bodies). **Fix:** add `once:'<flag>'` to the host-writer patterns in `src/scripts/check-questgraph.js` alongside the NPC-grant scan. **The general point:** a gate that reports 50 candidates with a known-missing writer class is understating its own confidence, and every future reader has to re-derive which entries are real. ***A soft-lock detector's blind spots belong in its output, not in its author's head.***

### §DX-02bu — the §DATA-01-REVERTED row's "zero functions" claim is false (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02ba, 🟢 doc correction)

- [ ] **§DX-02bu — a closure row that closed itself with a number nobody re-measured.** §DATA-01-REVERTED (2026-07-06) closed the row as superseded on the evidence that UQF quests are *"declarative UQF-1.0 — `onPass`/`onFail` grep to 2,650+ occurrences but **zero functions** (all `[{kind:…}]` descriptor arrays)."* Measured at HEAD: there are **124 `{kind:'_legacy_fn', fn:…}` descriptors, every one carrying an inline anonymous arrow function**, all of them inside `const QUEST_DB = {@10615`. **118 take no argument and 41 of those name `S_story` directly** — closures over live game state, the exact construct §DATA-01 was opened to remove. The claim was false when written.
> **The decision it supported still stands** — 124 of **5,638** descriptors is **2.2 %**, against §ARCH-02's `{e:'hook'}` share of **107 of 122 = 87.7 %**, so UQF really did reach the goal and rebuilding `QUEST_EFFECTS` really would have been a second competing system. Only the *evidence sentence* is wrong, and it is wrong in the direction that makes the system look finished. **Fix:** amend the row to *"124 `_legacy_fn` closures remain (2.2 %), each tagged and probed by `check:questgraph`"*. **Not in conflict with §DOC-02b's *"16 `_legacyFn` left"*** — that is the camelCase kernel identifier (17 occurrences), a different symbol from the snake_case descriptor kind. ***Two spellings one underscore apart, counted separately in two rows, is how a corpus disagrees with itself without either author being careless.***

### §DX-02bv — 175 line-numbered doc rows that `check:anchors` is structurally unable to see (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02bb, 🟡)

- [ ] **§DX-02bv — zero dead anchors and 167 stale line references, in the same files, on the same run.** Six maintained home docs carry function-reference tables in `| Function | Line |` two-column form: `docs/spec/combat.md` (55 rows) · `world.md` (29) · `docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md` (29) · `story.md` (27) · `mechanics.md` (26) · `monsters.md` (9) — **175 rows total, 0 exact, 167 stale, 8 unresolvable.** A 0 % hit rate. Sampled drift on `docs/spec/combat.md` runs **+934 to +1,284 lines**; every claimed line now lands inside `MONSTER_POOL` data rather than the function it names (`roll(sides)` claimed 5483, actual 6417; `syncCharFromUI()` claimed 6743, actual 8027).
> **The gate built for exactly this cannot see any of it.** `src/scripts/resolve-anchors.js:const DOC_ROOTS@43` already walks `.`, `docs` and `lab-reports`, so all six files are in scope, and `check:anchors` passes clean at **2,730 anchors across 60 docs, 0 dead** — because `src/scripts/resolve-anchors.js:const ANCHOR_RE@48` matches only `` `symbol@line` `` **inside backticks**. The two-column table form is not *stale* to the gate; it is **invisible**. This is the §DX-02n family's shape from a third side: not a census with zero readers, but a **doc-drift class outside the scan universe** — same lesson as §BOARD-01's flag-browser prior art, *widen the scan universe rather than start over*.
> **Fix (mechanical, no design call):** convert each `| Line |` column to the anchor form (`` `function roll(sides)@6417` ``) so the gate owns them, then `npm run anchors:fix` to refresh. Alternative if the column must stay: teach `ANCHOR_RE` a second pattern for `| `sym` | NNNN |` rows. **Sub-item (🟢):** `index.md`'s footer hardcodes four live totals and two have already drifted — **37,950 vs 38,712 lines** and **~2,848 vs 2,853 quests** (416 nodes and 398 monsters still exact). `npm run stats` exists precisely so these are not hardcoded, and prompt.md §0 already says so.
> **Provenance:** this is the `lab-report-synthesis-part1-architecture.md` §API-03 / SP5 prediction of **2026-06-16**, confirmed 58 days later at **175× the scope it named** (it named one table in one document). Zero commits in the repo mention SP5 or §API-03. The report was right that the numbers would rot; nobody was wrong about the fix, because nobody looked.

### §DX-02bx — Yael's Named Report scene: the code says Act VI, its own comment and `world.md` say Act IV (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bf, 🟢 doc correction)

- [ ] **§DX-02bx — the engine comment and the maintained home doc are both wrong about the same gate, and a lab report is the only source that kept it.** The live trigger is `yaelNamedReportDelivered) {@32491`, guarded by `_npcFavor('yael') >= 2 && (S_story.actNumber || 1) >= 6 && S_story.yaelEscortUsed` at node `LHR`. Two sources disagree with it: (1) `const YAEL_NAMED_REPORT_SCENE = {@28048` — the const's **own header comment** — reads *"Layer 74; fav >= 2 Act IV+; yaelNamedReportDelivered"*, understating the act gate by two; (2) `world.md:617` says the scene *"fires at a `LLA` or `HKG` visit (fav_yael ≥ 2, Act IV+, `yaelNamedReportFired` not set)"* — **wrong node** (live gate is `LHR`), **wrong act**, and **`yaelNamedReportFired` is a phantom: 1 occurrence in `world.md`, 0 in `play.html`.**
> **Fix (mechanical, no design call):** correct the header comment to Act VI+, and correct `world.md:617` to `LHR` / Act VI+ / `yaelNamedReportDelivered`. Delete the phantom `yaelNamedReportFired` — it has never existed in the engine, so nothing can be reading it.
> **Why it matters beyond the numbers:** an author trusting either source would test the scene in Act IV, see nothing fire, and conclude the arc is broken. The scene is fine; the map to it is not. Act VI is also the correct number *narratively* — Yael files her second report late, after the player has seen the archive suppression, and moving the gate to IV would land the beat before its setup.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02bf. Distinct from §DOC-02y's §AUDIT-03x finding on the same scene, which covered the **patrol addendum's** node (`nodeSlug:'MSY'@27733`) and cell primacy, not the act gate. ***A lab report outscoring both the engine comment and the maintained home doc inverts the program's usual direction — instrument 6 both ways: the archive-facing document is not always the stale one.***

### §DX-02bw — §DX-02n's `q.xpAward` sub-item says "0 readers"; there are two (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02bc, 🟢 evidence correction)

- [ ] **§DX-02bw — a dead-field finding filed against a live field, and the engine's own comment names the other one.** §DX-02n (extended by §DOC-02e) records `q.xpAward` as *"53 occurrences, 33 on UQF quests, **0 readers** — the undocumented twin of `q.reward`."* Measured at HEAD: **two readers.** (1) The quest-completion loop — `// §D02 xpAward on side-type quest completion@30201`, `if (q.type === 'side' && q.xpAward) { S_story.xp += q.xpAward; _checkLevelUp(); }` — shipped **2026-05-26 in `8abc606`, a commit whose entire subject is *"ui stuff"***. (2) The Warrant's Board honest-reward preview, whose own comment calls it *"the live payout consumed at storyCheckQuests"* and states that **`q.reward` is intentionally NOT read — it is a dead/display-only field**. So the pair is real and the polarity in the row is reversed.
> **Scope measured:** **51 `QUEST_DB` entries carry `xpAward`, and 46 of them are `type:'side'`** — the exact shape reader (1) requires; the other 5 are `skill_check` quests whose XP comes from a `reward` bit, so for those the field genuinely is inert. **Fix:** correct the §DX-02n sub-item's evidence sentence (the field stays), and if `check:deadconsts` is ever built, this is the case that proves the census must be **per-consumer-shape**, not per-symbol: a field read behind `q.type === 'side'` is live for 46 entries and dead for 5, in the same table, under the same name.
> **Not in conflict with the rest of §DX-02n** — `LOOT_TABLE` (0 readers) and `S_story.ebReturnsCompleted` (write-only) were re-confirmed dead in this same pass. ***A "0 readers" claim is a search result, and a search that misses one line inverts the verdict for the whole field.***

> **THE FINDING — 27th INSTRUMENT: A CITATION CARRIES NO EVIDENTIAL WEIGHT.** This report summarises three sibling lab reports rather than reading the file, and **every quantitative figure it borrowed is wrong while the ones it measured itself are exact.** (1) Appendix B's `encounterChance = min(0.9, 0.1 + activeQuestCount × 0.05)` is character-for-character `lab-report-circuit-map-theory.md:378` **as it stood at `ffe7720^`** — §DOC-02f measured the shipped formula as `min(0.95, 0.1 + notoriety×0.015 + activeQuestCount×0.04)`, a whole missing term. (2) The `28.75 HP/kill` loot EV **and** the `50/25/15/10` distribution both come from `lab-report-drop-rates-balance-and-health.md`; §DOC-02j measured **21.0 HP** live, 27.75 from that report's own table, and named the cause — the two Spell Scroll rows folded into Minor to make the split round. This report copies the rounded version. (3) **And it silently CORRECTS a sibling:** its stalk boost is `6×`, while the source it cites states `const QUEST_BOOST = 3` in **two** places and §DOC-02f measured `BOOST = 6` shipped. Three borrowings, two wrong, one right, and **nothing in the prose distinguishes them** — both wrong figures sit in a formal appendix table and the right one in running narrative. ***A cited figure must be verified at the same cost as an uncited one: the corpus's error rate is not independent across reports, it is correlated by copying, and a synthesis document is where the correlation becomes invisible.*** This also refines instrument 12 — a copy of the wrong source is as bad as an illustration and looks considerably more authoritative.

Full row above.

(b) *"Short rests are free… the Cooperative DM Principle **prohibits**"* charging for rest: `function storyShortRest@25817` now spends `+1h` of `hoursSinceSlept`, and `hoursSinceSlept || 0) >= 24@25046` imposes **disadvantage on every attack roll**. Small in magnitude (3 rests = 3h of 24), opposite in direction. **Not filed as defects** — they are later design decisions (§MESH-01f, P3 exhaustion) that traded a written contract without writing the trade; recorded because this report is the only surviving statement of the original.
> **FOURTH — INSTRUMENT 8's LIMIT, STATED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED.** The report is dated **2026-05-21**; the repo's first commit is **2026-05-24**. `roll2hit.html` and `roll2hit-v2.html` — Phases I and II, the *"From Arena"* half of the title — have **0 commits in the entire history** and are absent from disk, and `32c10c5` already holds **14,377 lines / 81 nodes** against the report's *"Current State"* of 7,465 / 42. Those phases are not stale and not never-shipped: they are **unadjudicable**, and that is exactly why three arena function names could be invented without contradiction. §VII *"What Stayed"* argues the original dice engine survives untouched and names three functions that never existed — while the engine it describes **is** there (`function roll(sides)@6417` · `function rollN@6421` · `function rollD20@6496` · `function rollInitiative@7437` · `function rollMainDamage@7566`). **Thesis right, every supporting identifier wrong** — instrument 12 in its purest form, in the report's most narrative section.

> **What this is.** A curated batch of *large refactor-style suggestions and side tangents* surfaced while writing **`prompt.md`** (the operating directive) and re-reading the full project on 2026-07-22. **Every claim below was grep-verified against the live repo this session** — line/count/red figures are measured, not recalled (grep-before-building). **None of these were built** — they are logged so the main task (prompt.md + README/index sync) could finish without turning a tangent into a detour, exactly the finishing discipline `prompt.md §2.9` prescribes. Each is its own increment; **each still needs the normal gate** (lab report only if it grows to a redesign; API-first; doc sync; verify). Ordered by payoff-to-risk.

- [x] **§DX-01a — `check:walk` fully green ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28 (also closes §JUNK-01).** Both 20-day baseline reds retired; **no checker was taught an allowlist — the data was genuinely wrong, both ways, and both reds were the SAME defect.** Reproduced live first (identical to the row's figures), then: **(1) J14/J15 removed** — hand-edit per Hazard #4 with the server confirmed stopped (Hazard #1): the two `junction:true` NODE_MAP entries + NODE_COORDS rows + TGS's dangling `S:"J14"` deleted; repo-wide grep proved zero other live references (maps.md's "J14" is the *historical* Malta-approach node 104, a different entity; milepoints/ is a snapshot). **(2) Key discovery: R2's three off-component "settlements" 7,217/8,217/9,217 were exactly TGS+J14+J15** — the §KG highway tool dropped the stubs and never laid the road its own signpost promised ("The Tungas–Station 7 (Dark) Road"; SVO sits at 14,217, straight south). **(3) `build-roads.js --apply` regen** (the block's own "do not hand-edit" path; src/config/roads-pins.json untouched, unrelated region): cell-diff vs HEAD = **394/411 unchanged**, and every change is one of three intended stories — the real Tungas–Station 7 road laid (col 217 rows 8–13, TGS↔SVO), the TLL→SPB corridor rerouted off Gulf-of-Finland sea cell 10,207 (R3) via the row-8 TUO-stack corridor, and a local southern re-carve. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **all 10 gates green** — I1/I2/I3 (416/416 reachable, 1 component), R1–R4 (244/244 settlements, 0 overlaps, 410 cells 1.5%), all four parity fences + gateast 76 + rng + questgraph (0 prober-gaps, 0 nondeterminism); `quest-runtime-uqf` **303/303** (also proves the inline `<script>` parses).
