> *§DX-01b ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-22 `bd951d7` — the "~17–18-failure env baseline" was disproved (17 deterministic stale tests, no flake); 16 fixed, 17 → 1, the git-stash-diff ceremony retired; the 1 remaining red became §BOARD-01-VOID-GATE (since shipped). Full record: plan-archive.md §"Archived 2026-07-28 — §DX-01f".*

- [x] **§DX-01c — `./api.sh post monster` FIXED ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-30 `dca81ae`. WBAPI Hazard #2 is retired, and with it the API-first exception that made all 398 monsters hand-authored.** The clean fix was in scope, so the "make it fail loudly" fallback applied only to *bad input*. **The row's premise held exactly, and the root cause was one the row hadn't named: `MONSTER_POOL` is the only section in the file that NESTS another.** `MONSTER_DROPS:START/END` (5796/6269) sits *inside* `MONSTER_POOL:START/END` (5354/6270), so `insertBeforeSectionClose`'s `lastIndexOf('\n};', endAnchor)` resolved to the **trophy-drops map's** closing brace. Every created monster was spliced into a real-but-wrong object — **which is why it never threw.** Stacked on that, `serializeMonsterLiteral` wrote a schema the game does not have: `{name, ac, hp, atk, dmg, xp, tier}` — no `key`, no `dmgDie/dmgCount/dmgFlat` (so the monster had *no damage at all*), and the string tier through `Number()` → `tier:NaN`. **Shipped:** (1) `WBAPI._sectionCloseIdx` in `wbapi-core.js` — stops the search at the first nested START anchor; `insertBeforeSectionClose` now delegates, so all 12 create call-sites are covered and the class can't recur if another section is ever wrapped. (2) `WBAPI.monsters.create/validate/serialize` — the live 9-field contract, measured (398/398 entries carry exactly `{key,name,ac,hp,atk,dmgDie,dmgCount,dmgFlat,tier}`, plus `voidTainted` on 2); `key` is always emitted (a keyless entry is the §AUDIT-03e shared-slot class). (3) `serializeMonsterLiteral` **deleted**; the POST route delegates and returns **422 + the offending field list + a worked example, writing nothing**, for an incomplete body, a numeric tier, or the retired `dmg`/`xp`. (4) Seven server help/`howToFix` curls that *taught the broken body* corrected, plus `tier=boss` → `deadly` in `api-user-guide.md` (there is no `boss` tier — the example would now 422). **Verified end-to-end against the running server, not just in unit:** `./api.sh post monster key=dock_rat …` → git diff = **exactly one line, inside MONSTER_POOL, before its own `};`**; the old-style body (`dmg=3 xp=10 tier=1`) → 422 listing all six defects; game file restored to its original md5 (`d5afa4a3…`) afterward. `src/tests/integration/dx01c-monster-create.test.js` **7/7** — including a test that **pins the trap** (a naive last-`};`-before-END must still resolve *inside* MONSTER_DROPS, so a revert fails here rather than in the trophy map) and one asserting the six non-nesting sections are unaffected. `check:walk` **12/12 green**; full Playwright **708 passed / 6 failed, all 6 proved pre-existing at HEAD by stash-diff** (the 4 documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` + 2 previously-undocumented — see §DX-02f). **Docs:** CONTRIBUTING.md Hazard #2 rewritten as a working recipe that keeps the defect class as the lesson (*a write that lands in a real-but-wrong object never throws*), prompt.md §3 hazard #2, potential.md, `monsters.md` gains an Authoring section, `api-user-guide.md` §9.3. **Side-findings filed, not detoured: §DX-01i** (`del monster` is model-only) **and §DX-02g** (2 off-contract tiers).

- [x] **§DX-01i — `del monster` is model-only ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-30 `2f467f3` (jointly with §DX-01d — see that row for the full record).** The row's premise held, and **the scope was three times bigger than it named**: measuring it on a copy of the shipped file showed `monster`, `quest` **and** `node` all returning `ok:true`, all leaving `_rawSrc` byte-identical, all three surviving save+reload — the whole DELETE family, for every key, not just monsters and not just junctions. The row's suggested precedent (`the node-delete splice at wbapi-core.js ~1260`) turned out to be **dead code nothing ever called**. Original row text preserved in the §DX-01d record below.

- [x] **§DX-02g — the two off-contract tiers ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-31 `f229ede`. The row called it an authoring-surface defect. It was four live gameplay surfaces, and every one of them degraded SILENTLY.** The premise held exactly — `void_shaman:'rare'`, `void_rat_swarm:'low'`, the other 396 on contract — but the framing (*"this is an authoring-surface defect, not a gameplay one"*) was wrong, and measuring it before editing is what caught that. **`tier` is read in six places and every reader FALLS BACK rather than throwing:** `_voidEnrage(tier)` → `|| {atk:1,dmg:1,die:0}`, and The Warden matches `_VOID_ENEMY_RE`, so its §PLAY-01-B low-HP press ran at the **weakest magnitude in the game** (+1/+1) instead of hard's +3/+3 — on a sealed Act-5 guardian with a bespoke 4-item weighted drop table; `_storyRollInit` → `tierMod[t] ?? 0`, initiative +0 instead of +3; `_weightedMonsterPick` → `WEIGHTS[t] || 10`, and since `_inferTerrain` returns a *neighbouring node's* terrain, cells adjacent to **TBS** infer `epic_goblin_cave` — whose roster holds `void_shaman` — so Kazrath drew as a **random wilderness encounter at 2.5× a `hard` monster's weight** at low notoriety; `_fleeChance` → `?? 0.4` (moot for both — both match `_VOID_ENEMY_RE`, and Void enemies enrage instead of fleeing); and both threat badges rendered the literal **`RARE`** while asking for a **`.threat-rare` CSS class that does not exist**. **The tiers were derived from the corpus, not tasted:** `void_rat_swarm → easy` settled itself (6 of its 8 nearest stat-neighbours are `easy`; its exact AC/HP/atk twin is Jackalwere, `easy`). `void_shaman` was a genuine fork — its two **exact** stat-block twins (AC15/HP65/atk6 — Bandit Captain, Pirate Captain) are both `hard`, but the engine's own `_monsterLevel` puts it at 6, where 43 monsters are `medium` and only 4 are `hard` — so it was **asked with both readings measured, and the user chose `hard`**. **Shipped:** two `./api.sh put monster … tier=` calls (server restarted first, Hazard #1; stopped again after). **Evidence:** `git diff` = **exactly 2 lines**, alignment preserved; **golden parse diff across all 7 collections = exactly 2 entry diffs, both `fields=tier` alone**, 0 lost / 0 gained (416 nodes · 2,853 quests · 398 monsters · 273 npcs unchanged); new `dx02g-monster-tier-contract.test.js` **8/8**, of which **5 fail on the real pre-fix file** (restored byte-identical afterwards — the 3 that pass there are the ones that were already true); `check:walk` **14/14 exit 0**; full Playwright **740 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four and nothing new (740 = the 732 baseline + these 8). **§7½ DONE:** the pre-battle overlay at Void Shaman's Sanctum now reads a styled orange **HARD** badge instead of an unstyled `RARE`; 0 page errors. **The durable half is the inverse assertion:** the test also pins that **no tier-keyed engine map ships partial** — all 8 tier-keyed literals plus the 6 notoriety weight rows and the 5 `.threat-*` CSS rules must cover all five tiers, because a map missing a tier is this same defect from the other side and equally silent. **Docs:** `monsters.md` gained a *"the `tier` contract — five values, and it is not cosmetic"* section with the six-reader table and both derivations; its stale ⚠️ *"`del monster` only removes from the in-memory model"* warning (fixed 2026-07-30 by §DX-01d/i) retired in the same pass. **Two side findings filed, not swept: §DX-02h** (`void_rat_swarm` is unreachable content — 2 references in the whole file) and **§DX-02i** (`XP_BY_TIER` is declared and read by nothing). *(Original row: 2 monsters carry a tier outside the 5-value contract and are invisible in the worldbuilder picker — NEW 2026-07-30, found while measuring §DX-01c. The worldbuilder's monster `<select>` builds `byTier` from `TIER_ORDER` and pushes only `if (byTier[m.tier])`, so both are silently dropped from the picker, and `tierColors[mp.tier]` falls back to grey `#aaa`. §DX-01c's writer now rejects off-contract tiers, so no new ones can be created — these two predate it. **Risk:** trivial (2 field PUTs); the only judgement is which tier each belongs to.)*

- [x] **§DX-02h — the unreachable monster was one of 55, and the write path that was supposed to fix it reported success without saving ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `a047348`.** The premise held exactly — `void_rat_swarm` had 2 references, no roster, no battle, no quest — but **its framing was wrong twice**, and both were found by measuring before editing. **(1) It was not unique.** Scanning all 398 pool keys for a reference outside `MONSTER_POOL`/`MONSTER_DROPS`, **55 have none** — including its `voidTainted` twin **`void_wolf`** (identical 2-reference shape) and ~50 of the *partially* rostered Dark Fantasy Bestiary import (`fleder`, `cave_bear`, `koshchey`, `shaelmaar`, `ysbaddaden`, while neighbours `protofleder`, `warg`, `alp`, `basilisk` are live). The other 53 are filed as **§DX-02j**. **(2) The reason nobody noticed was a doc that read as if it shipped.** `world.md` §Void-Touched Monsters stated, in the present tense, that both were *"injected into terrain encounter tables at runtime when `voidPressure ≥ 6` via `_applyVoidPressureMonsters()`"* — that function occurs **0 times in the file**, and **2 of the 5 terrains it named (`dark_forest`, `mountain_pass`) have never existed as keys**. The doc was the thing that looked like evidence (§AUDIT-03l's class). **The row's own fix was blocked by a third defect: `PUT /api/terrain` accepted only `{label, icon}` — and persisted NOTHING.** It set `WBAPI.worldDb[k][f]` in memory, returned `ok:true` with the entity echoed, and never called `save()`; **`GET` then read the phantom edit back for the rest of the process's life**, so unlike the DELETE family (§DX-01d/i) the read path *corroborated* the lie. Root cause: `editField`/`editStructuredField` carried a `sectionMap` with **no `terrain` entry**, so `WORLD_DB` had no source-level writer at all — and the server's own advisory already told authors to `PUT {"monsters":[…]}`, an operation the endpoint rejected. **Shipped, API-first (endpoint before edit, prompt.md §2.5):** `terrain` added to the section map; a dedicated **`WBAPI.editTerrainRoster(key, monsterKeys)`**, because `WORLD_DB` is the one collection whose array holds **code identifiers** (`P.giant_rat`) — `editStructuredField` would emit `["giant_rat"]`, which re-parses to strings and silently drives `_monsterLevel` to 1 for the whole terrain (§DX-01c's real-but-wrong-object, third dimension); it re-parses the patched section and **proves the roster reads back before committing** (`deleteEntrySource`'s refuse-rather-than-corrupt), the server re-verifies after the disk reload, and `editField` now **refuses** `monsters` outright (patchStringField would find no quoted value and insertStringField would add a *second* `monsters:` key — §AUDIT-03a's rot). **Duplicates are allowed, not rejected**, because `cat_quarter` ships one (`fluffy_cat` ×2) and a writer that cannot express what the corpus holds cannot round-trip it — it warns. **Then one `./api.sh put terrain sewers monsters=…` placed both void kin** (user-chosen: `sewers`' single node is **SFT "Visby Sewers"**, act 5, and Visby is where `VOID_TIDE_EVENTS[21]` sights the first Void Walker; levels 3 and 4 sit inside its `giant_rat`→`vampire` band). **Evidence:** the defect proved by round trip first (`ok:true` + `GET` agreeing + **0 occurrences in the file**); `git diff` on the game file for the placement = **exactly 1 line**; **golden parse diff across all 7 collections = exactly 1 entry diff, `sewers fields=monsters`**, 0 lost / 0 gained (416 nodes · 2,853 quests · 398 monsters · 111 terrains); negative controls through HTTP (unknown field, unknown monster key) both refused with the file **byte-identical by shasum**; new `dx02h-terrain-roster-write.test.js` **6/6**, of which **5 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — the one that passes is the inverse shape guard, already true); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **750 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four, nothing new. **§7½ DONE:** at SFT the pre-battle overlay renders **VOID RAT SWARM · Visby Sewers** with a styled green **EASY** badge (AC 12 · HP ~18 · ATK +4), and the engine's own `_weightedMonsterPick('sewers')` drew the swarm 172 and the wolf 241 times in 4,000; 0 page errors. **The durable half is the inverse assertion** — *every* terrain roster entry must parse to a stat block, not a bare key string — because a roster written as JSON re-parses without error and is invisible to every other gate. **The lesson, now proved in three directions (create §DX-01c, delete §DX-01d/i, update §DX-02h): a write path's acceptance test is a ROUND TRIP, and "GET agrees with me" is not that test.** Docs: CONTRIBUTING Hazard **#6**; `monsters.md` §"A pool entry is not content until a roster names it" (with the 55-of-398 measurement and the whole-roster-not-a-delta warning); `world.md` §Void-Touched Monsters drift-corrected; `docs/api/API-README.md` + `api-user-guide.md` (which had documented adding monsters via a PUT that could not do it, in raw `curl`). *(Original row: )* The whole file holds **exactly 2** references to the key: its own `MONSTER_POOL` entry (`void_rat_swarm:@5653`) and its `MONSTER_DROPS` trophy (*Void Shard*, sell 15, `void_rat_swarm:@6191`). It is in **no** `WORLD_DB` terrain roster, on **no** `node.battle`, in **no** quest and in **no** `EPIC_BOSS_POOL` — so unlike every other pool entry it can never appear in play, and the §DX-02g row's own assumption (*"both are live in terrain rosters, so they do appear in play"*) was true only of `void_shaman`. Now that its tier is on contract it is at least *selectable* in the authoring picker, which is what makes placing it cheap. Decide: place it in a Void-tainted terrain roster (it carries `voidTainted:true`, so the §TIDE-01 / Void-pressure surfaces are the thematic home) or retire it with its drop. **Grep first** — a swarm is exactly the kind of entry a planned arc reserves. **Risk:** low (one `./api.sh put terrain … monsters=` or one delete, which now actually persists — §DX-01d/i).

- [x] **§DX-02i — `XP_BY_TIER` deleted; it was not a stale copy of the shipped XP model, it was a different one ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `a047348`.** Premise held: one occurrence, its own declaration. **User chose delete over annotate**, and the divergence was measured before removing it rather than asserted: the shipped formula is `(S.enemy.ac || 10) * (S.opp.maxHp || 10)` (in `_grantMissEffortXp`, with the §XP-01 dials as percentages of that product), and against it the table paid **1.9×–6.5× less at every tier** — `deadly` a flat 1000 against a shipped mean of **4,095** across 33 monsters (min 1,649, max 12,012), `medium` 100 against a mean of 638. So adopting it would have silently cut the whole curve, not merely rescaled it. **Deleting it cost four dependents**, which is why the cheaper "comment it" option was on the table: `dx02g-monster-tier-contract.test.js`'s `TIER_KEYED_SITES` entry (would have failed *anchor not found*), `dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js`'s resolver fixture (**swapped to `const EFFORT_XP_PCT`**, a live §XP-01 dial with exactly one occurrence), `check-npcregs.js`'s `NOT_NPC_KEYED` classification, and `index.md`'s Design Constants row — **whose text was itself wrong**, claiming the table was *"kept for reference — L12+ uses `AC × maxHP`"* when nothing consulted it at **any** level. A ten-line retirement note stands where the declaration was, naming the shipped formula and the measured ratios, so the next session that greps it finds the reason instead of a tempting table. **Side correction on the path:** the comment two lines below read *"Level 20 = 680,000 XP"* while `XP_LEVELS` ends at **195,000** — no entry has ever been 680,000; corrected in place (`index.md` had the right number all along). **Evidence:** `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0** (`anchors:fix` refreshed the 23 hints my ±9-line shift drifted); the §DX-02i assertion in `dx02h-terrain-roster-write.test.js` **fails on the pre-fix tree**; full Playwright **750 passed / 4 failed**, the documented four. *(The illustrative `XP_BY_TIER → XP_BY_TIER_RETIRED` rename examples in `src/scripts/resolve-anchors.js`'s comments are left alone — they are hypotheticals, and §DX-01e's ship record depends on that name for continuity.)* *(Original row: )* `const XP_BY_TIER = { trivial:25, easy:50, medium:100, hard:250, deadly:1000 };` had **one** occurrence in the file — its own declaration. Battle XP is computed as ≈`AC·maxHP` (and the §XP-01 effort grants are percentages of that), so this table is not merely unused, it encodes a *different* XP model than the one that ships — the exact "authoritative-looking but fictional" shape §AUDIT-03l warns about, one grep away from being wired up by a future session that assumes it is live. Decide: delete it, or comment it as a retired model. Do **not** wire it in without a balance pass — 2,850 quests and the whole level curve are calibrated against the shipped formula. **Risk:** none (delete) / high if naively adopted.

- [x] **§DX-02l-FU ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-04 `3684b67` — the assertion was narrowed to a runnable shape, and the standing Playwright baseline is FOUR reds again. The row's premise held exactly and its own preferred fix was the right one.** Confirmed the red at HEAD before touching anything (6 passed / 1 failed on a clean tree), and confirmed the row's diagnosis was **understated**: the loop aborts at its first failing file, so it reported `prompt.md:197` only — `CONTRIBUTING.md:145` (Hazard #7 itself) is a second hit the run never reached. **Shipped:** `curlInstructions(src, endpointRe)` replaces the line-level filter — it tracks fenced blocks (` ``` `/`~~~`), and outside a fence considers only **inline code spans** and a line that literally begins with `curl` (allowing a `$`/list-marker prefix). A *sentence* naming both tokens is prose and passes; a **copyable command** is an instruction and fails. Widened one notch in the row's own direction: the endpoint pattern is now `/api/(save|snapshots)` — `./api.sh snapshots` is a wrapper of the same vintage and was guarded by nothing. **The durable half is the positive control**, because narrowing an assertion is otherwise indistinguishable from deleting it: a second test runs the detector over four synthetic **instruction** shapes (fenced curl · inline-span curl · bare line · list-item `curl -XDELETE …/api/snapshots`) and asserts each is caught, then over four **prose** shapes (Hazard #7's own sentence · this test's own name in a doc bullet · *"use `./api.sh save`, never curl"* · a fenced curl to a *different* endpoint) and asserts none is. **Evidence:** file **8/8** (was 6/7); **negative control on a REAL doc** — appended a fenced `curl -s -XPOST …/api/save | jq` to `docs/api/api-user-guide.md`, the narrowed assertion went **red**, restored with `git checkout` + `shasum -c` **OK**; `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **771 passed / 4 failed / 0 flaky** = the `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four alone, so the fifth red is retired and the 769/5 baseline recorded by §AUDIT-03k is superseded. **No doc prose was bent** — CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7 and prompt.md §7 still say what they said; `prompt.md` §7 records the retirement in the §DX-02e/§DX-02f house style. **The lesson, and the third instance of the class: the test was wrong, not the thing it tested** — and when a checker and a document disagree, the document that *records why the checker exists* is the last one you should rewrite. Original row follows. **§DX-02l-FU — the "no author-facing doc curls /api/save" assertion fails on PROSE about that very defect (found 2026-08-04 during §AUDIT-03k; a pre-existing red, not a regression).** `dx02l-save-snapshots-cli.test.js:190` flags any line in `api-user-guide.md` / `API-README.md` / `prompt.md` / `CONTRIBUTING.md` containing both `curl` and `/api/save`. **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7 — written by §DX-02l itself in `91d3650` — now contains the sentence *"verifying §DX-02k needed raw `curl`"* on the same line as `POST /api/save`,** so the gate reads a historical account of the defect as an instruction to commit it. Proved pre-existing: the test fails at HEAD with a clean tree (stash-verified, `shasum -c` OK), so the §DX-02l ship record's *764 passed / 4 failed* predates its own last doc edit. **This makes the standing baseline FIVE reds, not four**, until it is fixed. **Fix shape — a design call, which is why it is a row and not a detour:** either narrow the assertion to an actual *instruction* shape (a fenced command line / a line starting with `curl`, not a sentence mentioning both tokens), or reword Hazard #7 so the two tokens never share a line. Prefer the first — the second makes the prose serve the regex, and this repo has already paid once for a doc bent to a checker. **Risk:** low (test or doc, no engine).
- [ ] **§DX-02j — 53 more `MONSTER_POOL` entries are authored content nothing can reach (measured 2026-08-03 during §DX-02h).** Scanning all 398 pool keys for a single reference outside `MONSTER_POOL`/`MONSTER_DROPS`, **55 have none** — no `WORLD_DB` roster, no `node.battle`, no quest, no `EPIC_BOSS_POOL`. §DX-02h placed 2 (`void_rat_swarm`, `void_wolf`); **53 remain**, each with a stat block and a trophy drop that no player can ever earn. They are not a deliberate reserve: ~50 sit in the **Dark Fantasy Bestiary** block (`play.html` `// ── Dark Fantasy Bestiary`), which is *partially* rostered — `fleder`, `cave_bear`, `koshchey`, `shaelmaar`, `ysbaddaden`, `garkain`, `slyzard` are orphaned while their immediate neighbours `protofleder`, `warg`, `alp`, `basilisk` are live — so this is an import that was wired up halfway, not a plan. The rest: `lemure`, `unicorn`, `black_bear`, `void_warlord`, `slyzard_matriarch`, `dragon_of_fyresdal`, `bruxa_corvo_bianco`. **Decide per group, not per monster** (rostering 53 one at a time is 53 design calls): the deadly named four read as boss/set-piece material wanting a `node.battle` or a quest, while the medium/hard bestiary bulk wants one or two terrain rosters. **The durable half is a gate**, and it needs the corpus clean first: a `check:monsterreach` in the §AUDIT-03j/n mould — **explicit** classification, an unlisted orphan FAILS — so a monster authored tomorrow cannot go unreachable silently. Note the trap that hid this class: `world.md` described a runtime injection (`_applyVoidPressureMonsters()`) that **does not exist**, so the doc made two orphans look rostered; re-measure, never read placement off a doc (§AUDIT-03l). **Risk:** low per roster write (`./api.sh put terrain … monsters=` now round-trips, §DX-02h), but the encounter-weight balance of a terrain changes with every addition — add a few, eyeball §7½, do not bulk-dump 50 into one roster.

- [ ] **§DX-02n — two engine structures with ZERO readers, one of them carrying a doc comment that names a reader it does not have (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02b).** The §DX-02j class in a second dimension: not unreachable *content*, but unread *code and state*. **Two measured members.** (a) **`LOOT_TABLE` is dead data that documents itself as live.** `` `const LOOT_TABLE@24441` `` declares a full d100 loot table with the trailing note *"→ doc: docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md §D100 Loot Table (d100 result → item; used by `_rollD100Loot()`)"* — and the identifier `LOOT_TABLE` occurs **exactly once in the entire 38,707-line file**: on that declaration line. `_rollD100Loot()` reads `_D100_TABLE` (`` `const _D100_TABLE@24516` ``) instead. So the comment asserts a caller that does not exist, which is strictly worse than no comment, because the note is what a reader greps for and `docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md` may be documenting the wrong table on its strength. **First job is to decide which table is canonical** — compare `LOOT_TABLE`'s rows against `_D100_TABLE`'s before deleting either; if they disagree, the *shipped* loot curve is `_D100_TABLE`'s and the doc needs re-anchoring, not the code. (b) **`S_story.ebReturnsCompleted` is a write-only shadow of `ebReturnDone`.** Both are initialised in `_S_DEFAULTS()` (`` `ebReturnsCompleted: {}@23086` ``, `` `ebReturnDone: {}@23085` ``) and both are written by the same function on adjacent lines (`` `S_story.ebReturnDone[ebCode] = true@30363` `` and `S_story.ebReturnsCompleted[ebCode] = true` at 30360). `ebReturnDone` has **12 read sites** — `_curseScore()`, `_missionComplete()`, the victory screen, the node tint, and the 20 `quest_e*_return` completions via `flagsPath:['ebReturnDone.XXX']`. `ebReturnsCompleted` has **zero**, and persists into every save file meaning nothing. **This is the quietest form of Hazard #2** — *a write into a real-but-wrong object never throws* — and here it is a write into a real-but-**unread** object, so even the round-trip acceptance test hazard #5 prescribes would pass it green: the field saves, reloads, and is still never consulted. **The durable half is a gate**, the §AUDIT-03j/n house style: a `check:deadconsts` phase that flags a top-level `const`, or a `_S_DEFAULTS()` field, whose identifier occurs only at its own declaration/write sites, with **explicit** classification (a deliberate reserve must be listed with a reason) so an unlisted one FAILS. Measure the corpus first — the two found here were found by hand while verifying an unrelated report, which is not a search. **Needs no design call for (b)** (delete the field + its two writes; it is unread by construction). **(a) needs a one-line call** once the two tables are diffed: delete `LOOT_TABLE`, or repoint `_rollD100Loot()` at it. **Risk:** low. **Verify:** for (b), grep proves 0 readers before and after, and a save round-trip is unchanged; for (a), a diff of the two tables recorded in the row, then `npm test`'s loot pins. **⟶ TWO MORE MEMBERS, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02h), both §CROWN-01 Amendment A.** (c) **`waneStoneDCReduced`** — one writer (`` `set:['waneStoneDCReduced']@11994` ``, `quest_wane_spawn.onComplete`), one declaration (`` `waneStoneDCReduced: false@23202` ``), **no third site in 38,707 lines**. Its lab report specifies it as *"future attempts on quest_wane_02 use DC:10"* — and `quest_wane_02` ships `retryable:false`, so there is no retry to discount even if a reader existed. A field written to record a rule the engine has no way to apply. (d) **`Marsh Seaweed`** — a *drop item*, not a field: it occurs **exactly once in the file**, at its own push (`` `name:'Marsh Seaweed'@7061` ``, from 5 monster keys), carrying `desc:'Mire seaweed. Burns to trace iodine salt at the inn hearth.'` **Nothing consumes it** — `quest_iodine_02`/`_03` require `Swamp Kelp` only. So it is vendor trash whose own description names a craft that does not exist (it is therefore also an §AUDIT-03v-cluster instance). **This widens the proposed `check:deadconsts` scope a THIRD time** — after `q.xpAward` (quest-entry fields) and `_setActivePath`'s dead `toCode` (parameters), now **item objects pushed to `S_story.inventory` whose `name` is never read back**. Neither (c) nor (d) needs a design call for deletion; (d) has the alternative of *giving* it a consumer, which is the more likely intent. **⟶ A FIFTH MEMBER, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02i), and it widens the scope a FOURTH time — to a dead alternative inside a fallback chain.** (e) **`visitedNodes`** — the activation-gate reader is `` `st.visited || st.visitedNodes@22055` ``, and `visitedNodes` occurs **exactly once in 38,712 lines**, in that expression. It has **one commit in the file's entire history**, and it is not a `_S_DEFAULTS()` field at HEAD *or* in the 2026-05-24 archive build. The live field is `visited`. So the right-hand branch is unreachable and always was: defensive code written for a field that was never declared. **A top-level scan cannot see this and neither can a field scan** — the identifier appears only as an operand, so `check:deadconsts` needs a phase for *alternatives in a `||` chain whose identifier has no declaration*. Deleting the branch is a no-op by construction; **no design call.** **⟶ (a) IS NOW MEASURED AND ITS DESIGN CALL IS ANSWERED, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02j) — the two tables were diffed and `_D100_TABLE` is unambiguously canonical.** `LOOT_TABLE` is a **d20** table (20 entries: 8 Minor · 2 Spell Scroll · 5 Healing · 3 Greater · 2 Superior — its own doc comment calling it a *d100* table is a third error in that one line); `_D100_TABLE` is 7 weight-100 rows including flashbang and gold, which `LOOT_TABLE` has no concept of. **The shipped loot curve is `_D100_TABLE`'s, so the fix is delete `LOOT_TABLE`, not repoint anything.** Expected potion healing differs materially — **27.75 HP/kill** under the dead table vs **21.0 HP/kill** live — so anything balanced against the doc was balanced against the wrong curve. **Two doc-side defects fall out, and they are the reason this is worth more than a deletion.** (i) **Both home docs already claim the array is gone.** `docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md:253` says *"`LOOT_TABLE` | (removed) | Old d20 drop table — replaced by `_D100_TABLE` (§DROP-01 2026-06-05); definition now a comment stub"* and `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md:193` says *"`LOOT_TABLE` (old d20 array, Layer 25) removed; replaced by comment stub."* **There is no comment stub — HEAD still declares all 20 entries.** So two docs record a cleanup that never happened, which is the inverse of the usual rot and strictly more dangerous: a reader who greps the doc concludes the code is already clean. (ii) The archive kills the doc's own date — `LOOT_TABLE` had **exactly one occurrence at `32c10c5` (2026-05-24) too**, so it was already unread **twelve days before** the §DROP-01 commit the doc credits with replacing it. *(§DOC-02g's rule again: a migration note is a claim about the past, written from memory rather than from the diff.)* **Still no design call** — delete the array, delete the false pointer comment, correct both doc rows to *"removed 2026-08-xx"*. **⟶ SIX MORE MEMBERS FROM ONE FEATURE, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02k, §DUNGEON-01) — and one of them argues the gate must run the census in BOTH directions.** All six are `_S_DEFAULTS()` fields with writers and **zero readers**: (f) `voidFluxImmunityChoice` — one writer, always the literal `'fire'`, and it is the **entire reward** of `quest_d0209_a2`, a DC 12 Arcana check whose passText promises *"One spell effect will behave normally in this chamber"*; (g) `` `S_story.spiritDefeated = false;@21799` `` — **a `_legacy_fn` that sets a defeat flag to `false` at the moment of victory**, which is the sharpest member in the whole row because it is inert either way, so **no test and no gate can distinguish the bug from the intent**; (h) `mazeSolvedChecks` — three writers stepping 1 → 2 → 3, no reader, and the third writer is unreachable anyway (§AUDIT-03y); (i) `voidMazeEntered` — one `flag_write`, no reader, likewise unreachable; (j) `tribbleGladesFed` — one writer, no reader; (k) `cyMadnessTable` — one writer, no reader, and it directly contradicts its own lab report's §4, which states that `cyMadnessRoll` is *"the only persistent effect"* of failing the save. **The direction-widening point belongs to the sibling row §DX-02u:** `voidFluxCleared` is the **mirror image** of every member above — three readers and **no writer** — so it is not dead weight but a **broken dependency**, and a `check:deadconsts` scoped to *unread* identifiers walks straight past it. Whatever this gate ends up being, it must census **readers and writers separately and fail on either being zero**, with the gate-leaf flag names in `QUEST_DB` as the cheapest corpus to run it over (every one is a string literal). **§DOC-02n +3, all in the Layer 47 fishing block:** `baitFishingActive: false@23107` (1 occurrence, its own declaration — it was specified to suppress re-render during a bait-combat loop that was never built); `fishingQuestFlags['searched_'+loc]@30512` (write-only **and a COMPUTED key**, so a literal grep for a gate name never finds it — the §EPIC-01 concatenation hazard in miniature); and `fishingQuestFlags.q01`. **Plus a FIFTH widening of `check:deadconsts`:** `_magicTierAllowed(magic) {@24509` is a live function with two callers, and **both callers sit inside unreachable branches** of `_rollD100Loot` (§FISH-02) — a reader/writer census scores it live, so the gate must also ask whether a caller is itself reachable. **§DOC-02p +2.** (l) **`const JOURNAL_ENTRIES = {@22424`** — a five-entry table of authored Froberger quotes keyed by node, with **exactly one occurrence in 38,712 lines: its own declaration.** Its inline comment reads *"dead code — 5 node-specific Froberger quotes (entries 7/14/23/31/41); superseded by `FROBERGER_JOURNAL` delivery system"*, and unlike the §AUDIT-03aa tombstones **this comment is TRUE** — a useful negative control for the detector that row wants, since it proves the shape *"comment asserts death"* must be checked against the symbol, not assumed wrong. Note the five texts are **not** the five that live in `FROBERGER_JOURNAL` under those numbers; they are an earlier, shorter draft, so deleting the constant deletes the only copy of five authored paragraphs — archive them in `src/sources/froberger-journal-all-entries.txt` (already the constant's home doc) before removing. (m) **`S_story.curseScore`** — 3 occurrences, 0 readers, and not declared in `_S_DEFAULTS()`; filed on its own as **§AUDIT-03ac** because the write is advertised to the player as a mechanic and shadows the live `function _curseScore() {@28191`. **⟶ (b)'s ORIGIN FOUND, 2026-08-12 (§DOC-02ab), plus 2 more shapes in the same twelve lines.** `S_story.ebReturnsCompleted` was **specified**: `lab-report-npc-dialogue-system.md` §IV lists `S_story.ebReturnsCompleted >= 20` as a mission bit, and the engine duly writes it beside `S_story.ebReturnDone[ebCode] = true;@30363` — while `allEbReturns: Object.keys(NPC_DIALOGUES).length > 0@23656` reads the **sibling**. ***So a dead field usually has a document behind it, and the document usually reads as a requirement — which is why deleting one should be preceded by `grep`ping the lab reports for its name.*** Two more, both in `_missionComplete`: (p) that same `allEbReturns` line opens with a conjunct over an **immutable non-empty literal**, so it is always true and guards nothing — a *dead guard* rather than a dead field, invisible to a reader/writer census because both operands are live; and (q) `journalHalf: (S_story.journalEntriesRead || []).length >= 9,@23657` is named *half* while asking for 9 of `FROBERGER_JOURNAL`'s 41 entries — **22 %** — a bit whose NAME is the only documentation of its threshold and disagrees with it. **§DOC-02aa +2, and (n) is a SHAPE the row has not held before.** (n) **`nextFrobergerComplete`** — declared at `ngMemoryDelivered: {}, nextFrobergerComplete: false, frobergerLetterFound: false,@23125` and occurring **exactly once in 38,712 lines**, with `git log -S "nextFrobergerComplete ="` returning **0 commits in the file's entire history**. It is neither read nor written — every prior member of this row is write-only or read-only, so a census built to find an *imbalance* between readers and writers walks straight past it; the only signal is *occurrence count = 1*. Both `lab-report-ng-plus-remembrance.md` and `docs/story/story-arc-ngplus.md` stated that `quest_ng_01` sets it (both corrected 2026-08-12). (o) **`entry42Read`** — one writer, `S_story.entry42Read = true;@30676`, executed as a **side effect of rendering the journal sidebar**, and **zero readers**; the lab report claimed it gates `quest_ng_02`, which actually gates on `entry42Written`. It is the inert cousin of §DX-02ae's Act-VIII farewell getter: a render path mutating persisted state, harmless here only because nothing consumes it. Deleting either is a no-op by construction; **no design call.** **MEMBER ADDED 2026-08-12 (§DOC-02ai): `silarJoined`.** `` `S_story.silarJoined = true;@22534` `` is the field's ONLY write and there are **zero readers** in 38,712 lines — declared in `_S_DEFAULTS()` beside the other 22 §FUTURE-01 flags, set inside the Antioch NPC `quoteFn`, and never consulted by a gate, a panel, a quest or the character sheet. Silas/**Silar** is the source arc's second-journey partner and the structural replacement for Barnabas after the split, so **the player recruits a named companion the engine does not know exists.** Exactly the `ebReturnsCompleted` shape: it writes, it saves, it reloads, and the round-trip acceptance test Hazard #5 prescribes passes it green. Either give it a reader (an NPC row, a favour tier, or a `flags:['silarJoined']` gate on the second-journey quests, which is what the arc's own structure implies) or delete it — but **decide, because a companion flag with no consumer is indistinguishable from one whose consumer was lost.** **+1 (2026-08-12, §DOC-02ak): `baitFishingActive` — one occurrence in 38,712 lines, its own declaration at `tackleboxZoneUnlocks: {shore:true@23107`.** Never written, never read; the *sibling* field declared on the same line (`tackleboxZoneUnlocks`) has three live consumers, so this is the weakest shape in the family — **declared-only**, below even write-only, and it slipped through because a reader scanning that line sees a field that is plainly in use immediately beside it. **ak ✅ shipped 2026-08-12** — see the §DOC-02ak row. **al ✅ shipped 2026-08-12** (`lab-report-story-codoex-curse-of-knowedge.md`, 489 → 348 lines — the **third VOICE-not-tokens report**, and the cleanest split: **~26 authored prose specimens, 1 survives (4 %)**, while everything it quoted as existing and marked *keep* is still live. **All 3 name failures are its own SECTION HEADINGS contradicted by its own body** — *Voss*/Muffat, *Crane*/Sweelinck, *Finn*/Froberger, all 0 commits ever → **the body wins every time**. The lone survivor **changed channel** — dialogue → journal reported speech at `entryNum:14, nodeCode:'LCY'@27198`. **Its praised exemplar is byte-exact in self-labelled DEAD CODE** (`const JOURNAL_ENTRIES@22424`, 0 readers → §DX-02n +1) while live entry 41 is a rewrite built on *"The curse is knowing"* — **the report's own subtitle**. **But the STYLE shipped: concrete-numeral share of node text 28 % → 62 %, mean length 166 → 572 chars.** → **30th instrument: a style guide is obeyed by its PRINCIPLES and ignored by its EXAMPLES.** Filed **§AUDIT-03am** 🟡 — the retitle landed on the **starting node** carrying the report's subtitle, *Pinker's term for a prose defect*, into the first line every player reads.) `lab-report-sp4-documentation-sync-pass.md` §FC07 called both fields *"wired"*; one was. **+1 (2026-08-12, §DOC-02al): `const JOURNAL_ENTRIES@22424` — one occurrence in 38,712 lines, its own declaration, zero readers**, and the engine self-labels it: *"dead code — 5 node-specific Froberger quotes (entries 7/14/23/31/41); superseded by FROBERGER_JOURNAL delivery system"*. Unlike the rest of the family this one is **not** a lost consumer but a **content orphan**: it holds the only surviving copy of five authored Froberger quotes, including the entry `lab-report-story-codoex-curse-of-knowedge.md` singled out as its exemplar of good writing (`NUE: { num:41@22429` — *"I didn't tell him I don't have a sibling"*), which no player can reach. `FROBERGER_JOURNAL@27184`'s live entry 41 is a complete rewrite that keeps one clause of it. **Either delete the const or re-home the five texts** — but read them first; deleting is the only member of this family where the fix destroys authored prose rather than a dead pointer.

It is the shipped rename of the §WISDOM-01 report's `stoic_letter` (**0 commits ever**), whose specified payoff was *"the Stoic's letter of introduction to VS"* — so the reward is an introduction to the archive the player must **already be standing in** for the arc to have started. Two fixes, both cheap: give the letter a consumer (a `favorMin`-style leaf or an archive-tier line at `VS`), or drop the `flag_write` bit and the `_S_DEFAULTS()` field together. **The durable half for the proposed `check:deadconsts`:** this is a *quest-authored* flag, written by a `flag_write` bit rather than by engine JS, so a census that walks `S_story` assignments in the script region alone never sees the writer — the gate must read **`QUEST_DB` bit payloads as writers**, exactly as §DX-02u argued it must read gate-leaf flag names as readers.

Its five writers are the Yael arc's `_legacy_fn` closures (`@21372`/`@21385`/`@21398`/`@21411`/`@21427`), each doing nothing but `S_story.ceremoniaYaelAct = N` beside a `mission_bit` that already records the same fact. **The 2026-05-26 spec declared it as a mirror** — *"mirrors `quests[q.id]==='done'` state"* — and a mirror is what shipped. (e) **`q.xpAward` — 53 occurrences at HEAD, 33 of them on UQF-1.0 quests, and no reader anywhere in the file.** Every live mention outside a quest body is an §ARCH-01 migration comment; the only code hit is an unrelated battle-local `` `const xpAward@25292` ``. It is the **undocumented twin of `q.reward`**, which the engine *does* label — *"`q.reward` is intentionally NOT read — it is a dead/display-only field"*`@37045`. **An author reading `xpAward:200` beside a `reward{xp:200}` bit cannot tell which one pays**, which is the §DX-02n thesis at its sharpest. **Scope change:** the proposed `check:deadconsts` must therefore cover **quest-entry fields**, not only top-level `const`s — (e) is invisible to a top-level scan. **Needs no design call for (c)/(d)** (delete field + writes; unread by construction). **(e) needs a one-line call**: sweep the 53 fields, or label them dead in-file the way `q.reward` already is.

> **⟶ EXTENDED 2026-08-12 by §DOC-02q — one more member, and a shape worth naming: a write-only flag whose ABSENT READER WAS SPECIFIED IN PROSE.** `` `kenickieMarketUsed: false,@23120` `` has exactly three occurrences — its `_S_DEFAULTS()` declaration, `` `S_story.kenickieMarketUsed = true;@33056` `` on first purchase, and nothing else. `lab-report-kenickie-chronicle.md` §II-B states that it *"is used to conditionally alter Kenickie's greeting on subsequent visits"* and quotes the variant line; that string has **0 commits ever**, so the reader was designed, documented and never built. It persists to `localStorage`, reloads, and is never consulted, so the round-trip acceptance test Hazard #5 prescribes passes it green — §DX-02n's defining property. **Why it matters for the gate's design:** this member reads as a *deliberately simple instrument* rather than as residue (the report even defends choosing a boolean over a counter at length), which is exactly the case a human reviewer waves through — the argument for a mechanical reader/writer census rather than judgement.

(g) **`` `name:"Innmother's Key"@23535` `` — 1 occurrence in 38,712 lines, 0 readers.** It is pushed into `S_story.inventory` with `icon:'🗝'` and a `storyMsg` announcing *"The room is free from here"*, but nothing ever tests for it: the free-room effect runs entirely on `S_story.freeBookingUnlocked`, set on the line **immediately above** the push. So one `if` block writes a flag, an item, and a second flag, and only the first is ever consulted. **Why this member matters to the gate's design:** (g) is an inventory *item* (the §DOC-02h class) and (h) is a declared-and-written *field* (the §DOC-02ak class), and they are **adjacent lines in one block** — a scan that covers only one of the two shapes reports this site as half-clean. The proximity is also the reason it survived review: three consecutive writes read as thoroughness, not as redundancy. **Additional defect on (g):** the key carries `drop:false` and **no `type` field**, so `_storyDeathSaveFall()`'s `` `const critTypes = new Set(['shard', 'key']);@25965` `` filter cannot see it and the key — the one item in the game whose entire identity is *key* — is dropped to the corpse on death. That half belongs to §DX-02ax. **No design call** on (g)/(h): delete the item push and the flag, keep `freeBookingUnlocked`, keep the `storyMsg`. **Verify:** kindness ≥ 5 still prints the message and still makes `INN` sleep free; `grep -c "Innmother's Key"` → 0.

- [ ] **§DX-02ax — `drop:false` is a documented protection with five writers, zero readers, and two real items depending on it (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02am).** 🟡 **small design call — enforce or delete, but not both.** `drop:false` occurs **5 times** in `play.html` and is **read 0 times**. The death routine `` `function _storyDeathSaveFall()@25950` `` decides what survives by **item type**, not by the flag: `` `const critTypes = new Set(['shard', 'key']);@25965` `` keeps shards and keys and `` `S_story.inventory = [...critItems, Object.assign({}, STARTER_DAGGER)];@25992` `` sends everything else to the corpse. **For the two tattoos that carry it the flag is harmless** — tattoos live in `S_story.tattoos[]`, never in `S_story.inventory`, so they are safe by *container* and the flag is ornamental. (The lab report's own §III-B asserted the flag "is checked by `_storyDeathSaveFall()`" **in the same sentence** that says the routine "never touches `S_story.tattoos[]`"; the second half is the true one.) **The cost is on the two ordinary inventory items that also carry it and have no `type`:** (1) `` `name:"Innmother's Key"@23535` `` — 0 readers, so losing it costs nothing today (→ §DX-02n (g)); (2) `` `name:"Glut's Gift", icon:'🍯'@31456` `` — a **real gated item**, tested by `` `some(i => i.name === "Glut's Gift")@34462` `` and consumed by `quest_glut_06`, so a death while carrying the jar sends it to the corpse and stalls the return quest until the body is reclaimed. Recoverable, so this is a stumble and not a soft-lock — and **currently unreachable anyway**, because HG1 is non-primary in cell 25,206 (§AUDIT-03x); the moment that row is fixed, this one becomes live. **Only two items in the whole file carry `type:'key'`** (`Cipher Scrap@21270`, `Scholar's Note@21324`), so the protected set is much smaller than the flag's use implies. **The design call is which direction:** (a) teach the death routine to honour the flag — `i.drop === false || critTypes.has(i.type)` — which makes the documented contract true everywhere in one line; or (b) delete `drop:false` from all five sites and give Glut's Gift `type:'key'`, which keeps a single mechanism. **(a) is recommended**: it is smaller, it matches what four documents and the character-creation screen already tell the player, and it makes the flag usable by future authored items without a type taxonomy decision. **Risk:** low either way. **Verify:** die while carrying Glut's Gift → the jar is still in inventory and `quest_glut_06` still completes; shards and keys still survive; the corpse still receives ordinary loot and all gold.

- [ ] **§DX-02az — an NPC carries a dialogue tier no code can ever unlock (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02an).** 🟢 **no design call.** `` `vonn: { meta: { name:"Adjutant Vonn"@10412` `` ships `impartial`, `quote` **and** a `friendly:` array (*"You're one of the few who's actually read the manifest. Most people just shout at the gate."*) — and **nothing in 38,712 lines writes favor for `vonn`**: no `_setNpcFavor('vonn'`, no `{ kind:'favor', npc:'vonn' }`, no `npcFavorability.vonn`. The 2026-05-25 lab report specified him as a **static NPC that does not accumulate favorability**, which the engine still honours; the `friendly` tier was added later (§NPC-01-B's profile expansion), presumably by shape-matching its siblings. **The defect class is new to the family: not a dead FIELD but a dead VALUE inside a live field** — the object is read on every card render, the array is simply never selected. `check:deadconsts` as currently scoped (top-level consts · quest-entry fields · parameters · item objects · `||`-chain alternatives · dead handlers on live entries) cannot see it; the wanted phase is *"a tier keyed by a state the writer set never produces."* Two ways out, both cheap: delete the array, or give Vonn one favor write and let the line pay off — he is the only Conclave officer in the game a player can be *right* about. Related: §DX-02n's roster, §AUDIT-03n's tier machinery.

- [ ] **§DX-02ay — the ten-field Chronicle ledger shape is declared three times, and the factory built to prevent that is called by neither copy (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02am).** 🟢 **no design call.** `` `const _STAT_ZERO = () => ({ kills:0@23914` `` is the canonical factory and is used correctly at its three runtime sites (`_statTally`'s two null-guards and the respawn reset). But `_S_DEFAULTS()` does **not** call it — it hand-writes the same ten fields twice, at `` `careerStats: { kills:0@23146` `` and on the `runStats:` line immediately below. Three copies of one shape now exist and **all three currently agree** (same ten keys, same order), which is the only reason this is a hazard rather than a live bug: the next field added to the Chronicle has to be added in three places, and a save round-trip will happily carry a two-field-out-of-date ledger forward because `_statTally` back-fills any missing key with `|| 0`. **There is no ordering obstacle** — `` `const _S_DEFAULTS = () => ({@23062` `` is itself an arrow const whose first *call* is `` `S_story = _S_DEFAULTS();@23403` ``, several hundred lines after the factory is defined, so `_STAT_ZERO()` resolves fine at call time. **This is the §STATE-INIT drift class one object deeper**, and the file's own comment at `@22999` — *"seed only … keep `_S_DEFAULTS()` authoritative"* — warns against exactly this pattern one level up. The original lab report specified `careerStats: _STAT_ZERO()` and that is simply what should ship. **Risk:** very low. **Verify:** replace both literals with `_STAT_ZERO()`; a fresh load produces a `S_story` with the identical ten-field ledgers; the §STATE-INIT fresh-load regression test still passes.

- [ ] **§DX-02o — a quest-id string literal in the engine can resolve to nothing, and no gate can see it (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02d).** §AUDIT-03j's class — *a missed lookup renders nothing* — in the **quest-id** dimension, which is the one node/npc-style dimension with no fence. **The live instance:** `` `crov:['quest_pit_training','quest_drunk_fight']@23644` `` inside `_hasActiveQuestFor`. There is no `quest_drunk_fight` entry in `QUEST_DB` — `git log -S` returns **1 commit**, the reference itself, so it was never a quest; the drunk pit fight shipped as an engine event (Layer 41's `_drunkFight` branch). `S_story.quests['quest_drunk_fight']` is therefore permanently `undefined`, `_hasActiveQuestFor('crov')` silently drops half its roster, and Weckmann's `questActive` dialogue tier can only ever fire for `quest_pit_training`. Nothing throws. **Corpus measured, and it is small:** **302 distinct `quest_*` literals in the file, 301 resolve, 1 does not** — the only other two misses (`quest_whisper_`, `quest_wane_`) are legitimate `startsWith` prefixes and must be exempted, not fixed. **The gap is structural, not incidental:** `check:questgraph` walks `QUEST_DB` against itself (write-sets, gates, reachability) and never reads engine JS; `check:noderegs` mentions `QUEST_DB` **zero** times. **The durable half is a gate**, the §AUDIT-03j/n/m house style: a `check:questrefs` phase resolving every `'quest_*'` literal outside the `QUEST_DB` section against the live id set, comment-aware (the §AUDIT-03f requirement), with an **explicit** `NOT_A_QUEST_ID` classification list (the two prefixes, plus the `quest_item` type string) so an unlisted miss FAILS. **No design call** — the one live hit is a delete (drop the dangling id from the array) and the rest is the fence. **Risk:** low. **Verify:** the gate exits 1 on a planted bogus id and 0 at HEAD after the delete; `_hasActiveQuestFor('crov')` still returns true while `quest_pit_training` is active.

- [x] **§DX-02k — the argless `save()` was not a stray test call: it was the server's own per-write path, and it stamped a 5.4 MB snapshot on every PUT ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `2ead185`.** The premise held — `save(outputPath)` fell back to `getStampedName()` and left dated files behind — but **both of the row's framings were wrong, and the measurement is what showed it.** **(1) The destination is the process CWD, not "next to the module."** `getStampedName()` returns a **bare filename**, and `fs.writeFileSync` resolves a bare name against `process.cwd()`. Proved by loading the repo's HTML from a scratch directory: the snapshot landed in the scratch directory. It read as "the repo root" only because that is where the server runs — a script run from anywhere else littered *there*, silently. **(2) The argless callers were not scripts. The SERVER was.** `saveAndRestart` and `saveAndVerify` — the persist step behind **every** successful PUT/POST/DELETE — ran `WBAPI.save()` argless, then `copyFileSync(snapshot, GAME_FILE)`, and **never removed the snapshot**. Two more argless sites sat in the batch endpoints. So the row's own proposed fix, *"make the argless form server-only,"* would have left the actual litter source untouched. And the snapshot was never a backup of the pre-write state — **it holds the NEW text**; its only job was to be the byte source of the copy, which makes it pure redundancy the moment the copy lands. **The check the row asked for came back the other way, too:** `archive-snapshots.sh` / `watch-snapshots.sh` / `monitor-snapshots.py` do depend on the stamped *name*, but `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md` documents their input as a **deliberate `cp`** — so the per-write stamping was not feeding a feature, it was drowning one: a curated session history turned into per-keystroke noise (patch chain last advanced **2026-07-09**, no watcher running, **6 orphans / ~32 MB** in the root). **Shipped:** `save(dest)` writes exactly where told and **refuses with no argument**, naming the alternative in the error · new **`WBAPI.saveStamped([dir])`** — the deliberate dated backup, landing **beside the source file** (`POST /api/save`, `wbapi save`, the audit-fix + junction-nuke endpoints; the last four also had the bare-name CWD bug) · new server-only **`saveGameFile()`** — temp beside the game file + **atomic rename**, so nothing is left to sweep *and* a reader can never catch a half-written game file; on failure the temp is kept and its path returned. **Evidence:** the CWD claim proved by a scratch-directory probe **before** any edit; **the pre-fix test run demonstrated the bug on itself** — stashing the fix and re-running dropped **two more 5.4 MB snapshots into the repo root**, which is the whole defect in one observation; new `dx02k-save-destination.test.js` **6/6**, of which **5 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — the passer is the `save(dest)` round trip, already true); live server round trip = **3 successful writes** (`act=1` idempotent, `act=2`, `act=1` back), each verified on disk by grep and through `GET`, ending at the **original shasum** with **0 new snapshots and 0 temp files**; `POST /api/save` still returns a dated backup beside the game file, so the archive surface is intact; `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **756 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four (+6 new tests over the 750 baseline), with `multiplayer-presence:171` **flaky, retried green** — a first run showed it red on both attempts and a second full run reproduced the documented baseline exactly, so it is the known flake, not a regression; root swept to **one** `roll2hit-v3*.html`, and two full suite runs added none back. **The durable half is the inverse assertion in the test: no shipped code may call `WBAPI.save()` with no destination.** **The lesson: "did it persist?" is only half of a write path's acceptance test — the other half is "and nowhere else."** A stray write throws nothing, changes no behaviour, and is found only by looking at the directory. Docs: **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7**, `index.md` (Version Snapshots + `wbapi-core.js` persist surfaces), `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`, `docs/api/api-user-guide.md` (which described `POST /api/save` as how writes reach disk — they already did), and annotations on the three historical notes that still traced the old stamped path (`lab-report-wbapi-architecture.md` §4.2, `data-code-migration-into-cells.md`, `cell-resume-prompts.md`). *(Original row: `save(outputPath)` falls back to `this.getStampedName()`, which writes a dated `roll2hit-v3-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.html` snapshot **next to the module — the repo root**. That is right for the server's autosave, but any test or one-shot script calling `save()` argless leaves a multi-MB file behind on every run; two such snapshots are sitting in the root right now from earlier sessions, invisible because the pattern is gitignored. `dx02h-terrain-roster-write.test.js` was fixed to pass `save(tmp)` explicitly. Decide: have `save()` refuse when the destination would be the repo root outside a server context, or add a `saveTo()` and make the argless form server-only; either way sweep the existing snapshots. **Risk:** low, but check `archive-snapshots.sh` / `watch-snapshots.sh` first — one of them may depend on the stamped-name behaviour.)*

- [x] **§DX-02l — the dated-backup surface had no `./api.sh` wrapper, and the sweep it wanted would have destroyed the history it exists to keep ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `91d3650`.** The premise held exactly as filed: `./api.sh save` answered `✗ Unknown command "save"`, `src/api/wb.js` held **0** occurrences of `save`/`snapshots`, and three lines of the *author-facing* guide (`api-user-guide.md` §17.13 and both worked-example workflows) told authors to reach the surface with `curl -s -XPOST …/api/save | jq` — the golden rule broken in the docs that teach it. **Shipped API-first, endpoint before wrapper (prompt.md §2.5):** `GET /api/snapshots` (the dir, per-file size/mtime, total, and whether the patch chain already holds it) and `DELETE /api/snapshots` (nonce-guarded like every other destructive route), then `./api.sh save` and `./api.sh snapshots [--sweep] [--force]`. `snapshot` had to be added to the nonce endpoint's `validTypes` — it is the one type with **no collection behind it**, since the sweep destroys files, not entries, so the key resolution is skipped rather than run against `undefined`. **The row's framing of the sweep was the thing that changed on contact.** It called the disposal *"a `rm` a human has to remember"* — but `archive-snapshots.sh` **already disposes of them**, and it does so by diffing each snapshot into a `milepoints/patches` delta and *then* `rm`-ing the file. So a blunt sweep is not a chore automated, it is **the patch chain's input deleted before it is read**: an unarchived snapshot is the only copy of that state. `--sweep` therefore deletes only what the chain already holds (`<stem>.patch` present, or named by `_last.name`) and **names `./archive-snapshots.sh` in the skip reason**; `--force` is the deliberate discard. **Evidence:** live round trip on the real server before any test — `./api.sh save` → 5.2 MB backup beside the game file → `snapshots` listed it `unarchived` → `--sweep` **kept it** and said why → `--sweep --force` freed it, with `play.html` at its **original shasum** and the root back to one file; new `dx02l-save-snapshots-cli.test.js` **7/7**, and **all 7 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — nothing here is vacuous); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **764 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four, **0 flaky**. **No HTML shipped and no §7½ — authoring surface only; nothing a player sees changed.** **The durable half is what the sweep is allowed to name:** a decoy suite (`roll2hit-v3-notes.html`, `-2026.html`, `-20260709.html`, `-20260709-0341.html`, `.htm`, `.html.bak`) survives a **forced** sweep beside the game file, because only an exact `-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.html` stamp is reachable — the stamp is the entire guard between *delete the backups* and *delete the game*. The test also pins the row's own lesson: **no author-facing doc may reach `/api/save` with `curl`.** **The lesson: an endpoint whose only documented client is `curl` is not a finished write path — the wrapper is what makes it real, and "it has no wrapper" is usually visible in the docs before it is visible in the CLI.** Docs: `docs/api/api-user-guide.md` (§17.13 rewritten + both workflows + the endpoint card), `API-README.md` (new *Dated backups* section + 3 mapping rows), `wbapi-help.md`, `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`, `index.md`, `prompt.md` §3 + §9, **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7** (which now carries the reporting half: the directory is gitignored, so `./api.sh snapshots` is the only thing that will ever tell you what is in it), and `./api.sh help` gained a *Persistence* block under SERVER LIFECYCLE saying plainly that you do **not** need `save` after a write. *(Original row: )* Verifying that the dated-backup surface still worked needed a raw `curl -X POST http://localhost:1367/api/save`, because `./api.sh save` answers `✗ Unknown command "save"`. prompt.md §3's golden rule is that authoring never falls back to raw `curl` — *"if `./api.sh` lacks a command you need, add the endpoint + a named `./api.sh` wrapper"* — and here the endpoint exists while the wrapper does not, which is the same gap read from the other end. Small: an `src/api/wb.js` command that POSTs `/api/save` and prints the `backup` / `primary` paths. Worth doing together with a `./api.sh snapshots` (list/sweep the root's dated files) now that they are produced only on request — the sweep is currently a `rm` a human has to remember. **Risk:** trivial; read-modify of the CLI dispatch table only, no game-file writes.


- [x] **§DX-01d — the junction-node CLI hazards ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-30 `2f467f3` (closes §DX-01i in the same increment). Hazards #3/#4 are retired and a new Hazard #5 records the defect class.** Taken as the board's named "unambiguous pairing" while §AUDIT-03d's design call stays open. **The row's `del node J##` half was right about the symptom and wrong about the scope, and that was the finding:** measured in-process against a *copy* of the shipped file (real game file never written), `monster:fish_01`, `quest:mq_1` **and** `node:RUH` each returned `ok:true`, each left `_rawSrc` **byte-identical**, and each **came back after save+reload**. It was never junction-specific and never monster-specific — **every `delete()` in `wbapi-core.js` was the single line `delete WBAPI.<collection>[key]`**, and the server's generic `DELETE` handler ended at `json(...)` with **no `saveAndVerify` at all**, so nothing reached disk even in principle. `WBAPI.deleteNodeSource` — the one function that *did* patch the source — was **dead code with zero callers** (and its private brace-walker missed `/* */` comments and left the `NODE_COORDS` row orphaned anyway). **This is the exact mirror of §DX-01c: a write path that reports success without persisting. Same standing lesson both directions — the failure is silent because nothing ever throws.**
  **Shipped (1) the deleter:** `WBAPI.deleteEntrySource(section, key)` — one source-level excision for all four types, **verify-or-revert** (after the splice the section's depth-1 key *multiset* must differ by exactly one instance of `key`, else the splice is rolled back and the delete fails loudly instead of corrupting the section), clamped past nested START anchors so a `MONSTER_POOL` delete can never reach into the trophy-drops map (§DX-01c's real-but-wrong-object trap, mirrored). **(2) one scanner, not two:** `check-dupkeys.js`'s proven comment/string/regex-safe `scanTokens` **moved into `wbapi-core.js`** and is imported back by the gate (`WBAPI._scanTokens`), so the audit gate and the writer can never disagree about what an entry is — a private copy would drift, which is the §AUDIT-03f silent-drop class again. **(3) cascades:** a node takes its `NODE_COORDS` row, a monster takes its `MONSTER_DROPS` trophy — no orphan left for `./api.sh audit`. **(4) the server persists and proves it:** the generic `DELETE` now routes through `saveAndVerify` with a new **`postVerify`** hook (a delete's proof of success is an *absence* in the re-parsed collections, which the `expectedFields` path cannot express) and returns `deleteVerified:true`, or a 500 if the entry survived. **(5) `highway --execute` deprecated and refused**, up front before the coordinate fetch (so it costs nothing and needs no server), with the real recipe in the error: `ROAD_RUNS` + `build-roads.js --apply`, and `./api.sh reachability` as the authority for why waypoints were never necessary. `deleteNodeSource` kept as a thin delegating wrapper. **Verified:** new `src/tests/integration/dx01di-delete-persists.test.js` **8/8** — round trip per type, both cascades, **the 20 largest nested UQF bodies each excised individually** (largest 3,350 bytes of braces/strings/arrow-fns; each must cost exactly one entry), verify-or-revert, the `blockedBy` guards still blocking with the source byte-identical, and the highway refusal. **End-to-end through the live server, twice, each a byte-identical no-op:** `post monster dx01i_probe` → `del monster` → `grep -c` **0** (the row's exact reproduction, which used to leave **1**); `post node ZZP` → `del node` → **0**; game file `shasum 018ee943…` **unchanged** both times. `check:walk` **12/12 green (exit 0)**, `check:dupkeys --selftest` 4/4 on the relocated scanner. Full Playwright **717 passed / 4 failed** — the baseline is **exactly** the documented four in `worldbuilder-crud-arrays.test.js`, plus the known `multiplayer-presence` flake (passed on retry); 717 = the 710 baseline + these 8, minus the flake counted separately. **Docs synced:** CONTRIBUTING.md Hazards #2/#3/#4 updated and a new **Hazard #5** written to keep the class (*whenever you add a write path, the acceptance test is a round trip — save, re-parse, assert the change survived*), prompt.md §3 hazards #4/#5, `api-user-guide.md` §10 (cascade/verify/guard table), `API-README.md`, and `./api.sh help` — whose §12 `del` section now states the guarantee and whose §21 no longer advertises **eleven** `highway --execute` invocations that would die. *(Original row: "Fix or deprecate the junction-node CLI hazards — both documented as Hazards #3/#4 and the upstream cause of §DX-01a's J14/J15 reds; `highway --execute` drops sparse `junction:true` nodes and zero road cells, `del node J##` desyncs model from file.")*

- [x] **§DX-01e — Retire line-number anchor drift ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-31 `57d79d7`. The row asked for a convention; the measurement is what made the case: 43 of the 50 anchors in the live docs were stale, and every single one of CONTRIBUTING.md's ten.** **The convention:** an anchor is one token, `` `symbol@1234` `` — the **symbol** is the pointer (any literal substring of the line meant, optionally file-qualified: `` `src/js/wbapi-server.js:function seededNext@1147` ``), the **number is a cached hint**. That is §DX-02e/§DX-02f's lesson in a third place: *pin the property, never the generated coordinate* — a line number is generated by every edit above it. **Measured before building:** of 14 bare anchors sampled at HEAD, **9 pointed at unrelated code** (`22104`, cited for `_rollSkill`, had become a prof-bonus line; `31618`, cited for the §CELL-03 position sync, an `aurosBlueprintKnown` branch); repo-wide the bare form appears **720** times across 27 files. **Shipped:** `src/scripts/resolve-anchors.js` — audit (exit 1 on findings) · `--fix` · `--legacy` (migration tracker) · a lookup mode that replaces `grep -n` (`node src/scripts/resolve-anchors.js execBits`) · `--selftest`; **`check:walk` gate #15 `check:anchors`**; `npm run anchors` / `anchors:fix`. **The gate's failure condition is deliberately narrow: a DEAD symbol fails, a drifted number only warns.** Failing on drift would re-create the per-increment tax the row exists to remove, and `anchors:fix` refreshes all of them in one command. Resolution is **identifier-boundary aware** — found while building the negative control: plain substring matching let `XP_BY_TIER` → `XP_BY_TIER_RETIRED` still 'resolve', so a *rename*, the exact thing the gate is for, would have passed. **Migrated (the live docs only):** CONTRIBUTING.md 10 · mechanics.md 3 · docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md 2 · **every bare anchor in BACKLOG.md's OPEN rows, 0 left**. Closed ship records, `plan-archive.md` (211) and the lab reports (~300) keep theirs — **history is annotated, not rewritten** (§DX-02c/§AUDIT-03m precedent). `potential.md`'s 36 are the only live-doc remainder → filed as **§DX-01e-FU**. **Two stale claims surfaced by re-anchoring and corrected in place** (both in CONTRIBUTING's Host/Script policy, both about work that has since shipped): the seeded-RNG bullet still said *"the client rolls the same encounter from `Math.random()` … until §VM-01-B lands"* — §VM-01-B landed, the client draws `_seededNext()`, and what still diverges is the *weighting*, not the stream; and the `_legacy_fn` bullet cited `quest_1367_f_plague` as *"rolls `Math.random() > 0.5` inside quest data"* when **no `_legacy_fn` line in the file contains `Math.random` any more**. **Evidence:** 6-phase `--selftest` (dead · stale · fresh · fix · boundary · qualified); **negative control on the real game file** — renamed `const XP_BY_TIER` in place, gate exits 1 naming `BACKLOG.md:312`, file restored and `shasum -c` **OK**; `dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js` **4/4**, and the migration test **fails on the pre-migration docs** (`git stash` of the 4 doc files → 1 failed / 3 passed); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; the audit reports **50 anchors across 4 docs, 0 dead**. **No HTML shipped and no §7½** — docs + tooling only; nothing a player sees changed. *(Original row: BACKLOG.md carries 182 bare 5-digit line anchors; they rot on every HTML edit, and nearly every §VM-01 row ends with "re-grep the anchors before the next edit — they will drift." Propose a lightweight, durable convention + a tiny `src/scripts/resolve-anchors.js`. **Payoff:** removes a per-increment tax and a whole class of stale-pointer bugs. **Risk:** low; adopt incrementally, no big-bang rewrite.)*

- [x] **§DX-01e-FU — migrate `potential.md`'s 36 bare anchors ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `f4bc624`. 35 of the 36 pointed at unrelated code, and four of the claims they anchored had gone stale in the direction that matters: the engine grew the capability the file is organized around being unable to do.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's design question still unanswered, took the last open §DX-01 row. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `45afe1f`, server stopped, single live claude session. **The premise held and understated itself.** Measuring every cited line at HEAD before writing anything: **only `5327` → `const ENEMY_DB` had not moved**; `21722`, cited for `execBits`, is now a Mother-Mimic quest body; `25558`, cited for `BOSS_COMMANDER_AUROS`, an `sbo-refocus-bar` style write; `26822` is past the end of a section entirely. **The method that mattered was deriving each symbol from what the PROSE means, never from what now sits at the cited line** — the anchors are stale in both directions, so the line is not evidence. **Symbols were chosen for unique resolution** (all 32 verified to exactly 1 hit before writing) because `--fix` picks the hit *nearest* the cached number — a vague symbol with 15 hits would let the tool silently pick the wrong one. **The migration forced four stale claims into the open, each corrected in place using the file's own `~~strike~~ + ✅` drift idiom:** `execBits` is no longer *"a straight-line `for` loop — no branch, no suspend"* but a generator (`*execBits(bits, ctx)@22223`, §VM-01-A/D); `choice`'s *"empty handler"* is the engine's first **suspending** opcode (`*choice(bit, ctx)@22319`) and `renderChoiceBlock`, which the file says *"was never written"*, no longer occurs in the file at all; **`unlock` is authored 18 times** (§BOARD-01 became the consumer §POT-H1 predicted) so the *"four dead opcodes"* finding is now three; and `textVariants` has two consumers, not *"exactly one"* (§TIDE-01-A added HKG). `item_check` is worse than recorded — `ctx._itemCheck` now occurs **exactly once in the file**, so the write reads back nowhere. **Evidence:** `--legacy` **671 → 635, exactly −36**, and `potential.md` reports **0**; the audit reports **94 symbol anchors across 6 docs, 0 dead**; **`npm run anchors:fix` reports "no stale line hints to refresh"** — every number written was exact, which is the round-trip proof; **negative control on the real game file** — renamed `const _ssjNpcs` in place on line 32427, gate exits 1 naming `potential.md:176`, file restored and **`shasum -c` OK** with an empty `git diff`; `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; `dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js` **5/5**, the new test **failing on the pre-migration file** (38 bare anchors received) and passing after, restored byte-identical. **No HTML shipped and no §7½** — docs + tooling only. **The durable half is the new test, because the gate cannot provide it:** `check:anchors` fails on a dead *symbol*, but a bare `` `31732` `` resolves to nothing and is invisible to it — so nothing but this assertion stands between the seed inbox and a slow slide back to line numbers. **The lesson: a doc that is read to BUILD FROM is a live doc, and rule 3's "re-grep before building" is not optional when 97% of its own pointers are wrong.** Docs: CONTRIBUTING.md's Doc-Anchor Policy now records that every live doc is migrated; `potential.md` rule 3 carries a dated pass note stating plainly that **only the anchored claims were re-measured** — the rest is still `43bd09c`-era. **Side finding filed, not swept: §DX-01e-FU2.** *(Original row: `potential.md` is the live seed inbox* — its anchors are *read to build from*, so they carry the same stale-pointer risk the parent row just retired, and they were measured at `43bd09c` (the §POT-PROMOTE preamble already warns several had drifted by 2026-07-23). Everything else still on the bare form is **history** (`plan-archive.md` 211 · the lab reports ~300 · closed ship records) and is deliberately left alone — annotate, don't rewrite. Method: name what each anchor points at, keep the old number, then `npm run anchors:fix` (the tool refuses to guess a number for a symbol it cannot find, so a dead pointer surfaces as a gate failure rather than a silent re-point). Re-run `npm run anchors --legacy` after to see the remaining count. **Risk:** none (doc-only, gate-fenced).)*

> *§BOARD-01-VOID-GATE ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-23 `37f8ccb` — `dayMin`/`dayMax` gate-grammar leaf in `QUEST:CORE`; the 3 Void-tide quests migrated off their `activateCond` closures; `quest-runtime-uqf` 303/0 (no known red). Full record: plan-archive.md §"Archived 2026-07-28 — §DX-01f".*

### §DOC-02 — Lab-report verification program (NEW 2026-08-11)

> **Premise (user directive 2026-08-11):** the **107** files in `docs/lab-reports/` are the project's design record, and most were written *before* the thing they describe shipped. They are HISTORY docs (`src/scripts/legacy-codes.js`) — never rewritten to hide what was believed at the time — but a spec that was never checked against the code reads exactly like a spec that was. The program: **take the oldest report by mtime, re-measure every claim against `play.html`, rewrite it short and IEEE-shaped** (abstract · method · as-built inventory with `symbol@line` anchors · spec→shipped delta table · risk-register outcome · defects → BACKLOG rows), and file everything it finds. **The house rules that govern this work:** claims that did not ship are marked **NOT SHIPPED** and *kept*, never deleted (a silently removed claim reads as one that held); never read a node code off a doc table (§AUDIT-03m); and annotation without verification launders a wrong claim into a confident-looking live one (§AUDIT-03m-FU). **Ordering note (corrected 2026-08-11 by §DOC-02b — re-measured, the §DOC-02a figures were wrong):** the directory holds **107** `.md` files, not 109 (`git ls-files docs/lab-reports/ | wc -l`; six were deleted by `120d617`). **44** of them shared one mtime (`2026-06-09 11:06:54`, a bulk copy) when the program opened — so "oldest" within that block is resolved alphabetically — and there are **45 distinct mtimes** in all. Each verified report gets a fresh mtime and leaves the block, so the block shrinks by one per increment (43 after §DOC-02a, 42 after §DOC-02b); **re-derive the ordering from `stat`, never from this note's arithmetic.**

The finding is where inside one document the accuracy lives.** Its §IV–§V data and build listings are **verbatim transcriptions with no measured error** — `WIRE_GLYPH` byte-identical in all eleven entries, `_routeSegments()` byte-identical, the seven junction coordinates exact, and both `NODE_MAP` samples exact in *every* content field (the report's `CI` is HEAD's `LHR`: `num:1`, `name:'city'`, "City Streets — Birka", `npc:'City Guard Captain'`, `loot:'Bloodstained Map'` — the node survived, the key was renamed). The predictor is not *"does the passage cite code"* but ***"could the author copy it"*** — a table or a function body can be pasted; a ten-step call sequence must be reconstructed, and memory supplies plausible names the file never held. **Sharpest single measurement: the Hunt/Warp dialog was built, styled, and never once shown.** At `32c10c5` the overlay, card, and all three buttons exist with the exact labels the report quotes; every JS reference to it is two bulk close-all arrays and one `classList.remove('visible')` — **nothing ever adds `visible`**, no handler is bound, no field is written. The real `storyCorridorTravel` branches on the persistent `S_story.huntMode` toggle instead, so **the report's central thesis — *"the choice is the mechanic"* — is the one thing the layer never implemented.** Same verdict §TIMELESS-01 later recorded for the Stalk modal, reached independently about a different screen. **Corpus cross-check settled a §DOC-02c error:** the two Layer-9 reports disagree on `CI`, and the archive says this one is right — "The Thieves' Den" was never a node label but `HUNTING_GROUNDS['city'].displayName`, so §DOC-02c's Appendix A conflated a terrain display name with a node label (its `AL`/`BQ` entries are likewise reworded, not transcribed).
