instrument 8 is what separates a rename from an error, and nothing else can.*** **THE HEADLINE → §EPIC-01: the rename updated every place the code SPELLS the identifier and missed the one place it COMPUTES it.** Seven sites derive an epic quest id by concatenation — `` `const primaryId = 'quest_' + ebCode.toLowerCase() + '_primary';@30346` ``, `` `const returnId = 'quest_' + pb.nodeCode.toLowerCase() + '_return';@36612` ``, `` `'quest_' + node.code.toLowerCase() + '_primary';@35892` `` and four more — and **all seven are byte-identical to the archive, where they resolved** (`'EF'.toLowerCase()` → `quest_ef_primary`, which exists). `c1d5a94` (2026-05-29) renamed the `NODE_MAP` keys, the `EB_NPC_DIALOGUE` keys, `` `const _EB_CODES = [@28030` ``, every `completion:{ battles:[…] }`, every `waypointNode` and even the `failText` prose — **but not the forty `QUEST_DB` ids, still `quest_ef_*`**. So the runtime computes `quest_prn_primary`, which does not exist. **Consequences: (1) all 40 authored epic quests are ORPHANED** — `` `if (!q || q.type === 'epic' || !q.activateNode) continue;@37020` `` excludes them from arrival-activation by design, and the chip handlers address a different namespace, so 40 titles/descs/hints/passTexts/failTexts and 20 `reward` values are unreachable content; **(2) the ENTIRE payment layer is unreachable** — `` `function _storyEbReturnBeat(ebCode) {@30358` `` has exactly one caller, gated on a key whose only writer is guarded by `if (QUEST_DB[returnId])`, which is falsy, **so no gold is ever paid, no reward item is ever granted and no return beat ever renders for any of the twenty contracts**, `ebReturnDone` is never written, and the `allEbReturns` milestone (needs ≥5) plus both end-game tallies are permanently zero; **(3) the NPC chip STICKS** at *"⚔ EPIC … awaits"* forever, because nothing completes a phantom key; **(4) a raw code reaches the player** — the accept toast falls back to *"📋 PRN"*. The combat half still works because `defeatedBattles[pb.nodeCode]` never goes through `QUEST_DB`: the boss can be killed, the player just never gets paid. **75 days live (2026-05-29 → 2026-08-12), and no gate can see it — `check:questgraph` walks `QUEST_DB` against itself and never evaluates a COMPUTED id; this is §DX-02o one level deeper.** **FINDING 2 → §AUDIT-03x extended: 8 of 20 contract chains are unreachable under co-location** — 5 battlegrounds non-primary (`IST`→`SEA` in a 17-node cell · `OTP`→`WG0` · `CAI`→`AHB` · `KTM`→`INN` · `HAV`→`HOR`) and 4 quest-giver nodes non-primary (`MSY`→`WG0` Gwynne · `SDQ`→`WG0` Wane · `NAS`→`VBY` Carrick · `BK`→`LHR` Mordus, the §AUDIT-03y node again); `HAV` is broken at both ends; 12 chains intact. Sharpest case: Zephyrine stands at `AHB` and sends the player to a vault **in her own cell** the engine will not render. **§AUDIT-03x's 416 / 244 / 172 figure is CONFIRMED** by independent recomputation. **FINDING 3 → §AUDIT-03v/w cluster, +10 and the widest instance yet:** `` `const EB_STORY_ITEMS = {@26283` `` declares 11 items, 10 referenced, **each with a `desc` naming a specific permanent mechanic** (swamp surprise immunity, waived river tolls, free sea passage, 3 days of suppressed desert encounters, Shard-roll advantage, upgraded Stunned, unlimited ADV vs Void, free pirate restock, Warrant passage) — **and each item name occurs exactly once in 38,712 lines: its own declaration.** No `effect` field, no readers; and `` `S_story.inventory.push({ name: si.name, desc: si.desc, icon: si.icon || '✨' });@30378` `` **drops `type` and `sell`**, so the seven items carrying a sell value can never even be sold. Plus two name collisions (`Crimson Warrant` is an EB item, Shard #4's completion item *and* the §BOARD-01 faction; `Sand Cipher` is an EB item and Shard #5's) and **`` `kazrath_journal:@26294` `` — 1 occurrence, 0 consumers**, the §DOC-02h *Marsh Seaweed* shape → **§DX-02n +1**. **FINDING 4 → §EPIC-03: negotiation is STRICTLY DOMINATED and its failure branch inverts the spec on both halves.** `` `const accepted = _ebNpcNegotiated ? d.paymentCeiling : d.paymentFloor;@30343` `` — the CHA check (`` `S_story.abilityScores || {}).cha) || 8;@30290` ``, DC 17) gates only the *damage*: decline → floor, pass → ceiling, **fail → ceiling AND 1–4 non-lethal damage**. The report specified *"failure not punished beyond losing the margin; NPC doesn't become hostile"* — HEAD punishes it with a scripted beating **and** does not withhold the margin. On the **16 of 20** contracts where ceiling = floor the whole exchange is cosmetic. Two riders: `` `Math.max(1, Math.floor(Math.random() * 4) + 1)@30310` `` and the d20 three lines above are **unseeded** `Math.random()` writing `S_story.hp` (invariant #6 → **§DX-02m**), and the shared failure panel is hardcoded masculine (*"Then **he** moves…"*) for whichever of the twenty NPCs was asked — **ten of whom are women** → **§EPIC-03**. **FINDING 5 — instrument 12 holds and the illustration is again the weak half.** Everything transcribable is exact; the one hand-drawn artefact, the ASCII grid excerpt, **disagrees with the report's own coordinate table in four places** (`EK` drawn at col 17 vs a tabulated 16, `EM` at 9 vs 8, `EW` at 4 vs 3, and `EP` drawn into **two** adjacent cells) — and the archive backs the *table* on `EK`/`EM`. Same split in the dialogue: **22 of 40 quoted lines shipped verbatim** (12/20 `opening`, 10/20 `warning`) while the narrative `wound`/`returnBeat` prose shipped **0 of 40 verbatim** — condensed into fields by a later hand, which is why the 20 long profiles were replaced by a 20-row register (the §DOC-02h house move, with the condensation measured rather than assumed). Coordinate table **16/20** and compass directions **14/20** against the archive; the 4 coordinate misses ARE 4 of the 6 direction misses (four nodes were relocated and their approach re-derived), `EK` shipped as a bespoke `spire:'EK'` link rather than a compass field, `EG`'s specified `SE` was flattened to `N` — **while `ED`'s `SW` shipped exactly, so the engine did support diagonals and the report's diagonal was honoured once and dropped once.** **NOT SHIPPED and kept (7):** the ten named reward effects, the Froberger Entry-13 variant and the sleep-delay dialogue variant (both **0 commits ever**), the three separate bonuses absorbed into negotiation ceilings, Star Fragment's 450gp sale value, `objectiveText` (§ARCH-01, schema-wide), and the 20–30 %-below-ceiling opening rule (`paymentOpening === paymentFloor` on 20/20). **MEASUREMENT HAZARD RECORDED, and it is the pass's own mistake:** a first reachability run reported **399 nodes / 155 non-primary** and filed a "correction" to §AUDIT-03x. The extraction regex capped node codes at four characters, so six-character keys (`FSLINN`, `MESFRY`, …) were missed in `NODE_MAP` while their trailing four characters (`LINN`, `SFRY`) matched in `NODE_COORDS`, minting seventeen phantom "coords-only" codes and a plausible, entirely false delta. **`check:dupkeys` printing `NODE_MAP=416` is what caught it, after the report had already been written.** ***A census regex is a claim like any other: check its total against a gate that already counts the same set before trusting any delta derived from it.*** **Evidence:** IEEE-shaped (abstract · 6-instrument method · as-built inventory · node-code translation table · **35-row two-way delta table** · 5 findings · 20-row NPC contract register · 7-item NOT-SHIPPED appendix · defect table); **~25 new `symbol@line` anchors, every one verified literal and unique BEFORE writing**, none containing a pipe inside a markdown table (the §DOC-02f hazard); `check:anchors` **551 → 574 across 19 → 20 docs, 0 dead**; `check:legacycodes` / `nodeindex` / `dupkeys` / `noderegs` / `npcregs` all **exit 0**, run individually — the `check:walk` chain was never invoked and no detach artifact appeared. The 95 stale line hints are pre-existing; `anchors:fix` was NOT run. **Docs-only: no HTML, no tests — the 935/4 (940) Playwright baseline stands untouched.**

Pair it with `git log -S` (accelerator four): **zero commits proves non-existence; absence at HEAD proves nothing.** **A seventh, added by the same pass: weight a report's tables and function bodies as evidence, and its traces, walkthroughs and sequence diagrams as claims.** §DOC-02f's data listings had a zero error rate while its execution traces carried five deltas including three identifiers with no commits ever — in one file, by one author, on one day. The predictor is not whether a passage cites code but whether the author could **copy** it. **An eighth, and it is a gate hazard rather than a method: an anchor whose text is not a LITERAL substring of the line it points at is DEAD even when the line is perfectly alive.** §DOC-02f found **5 such anchors left by §DOC-02e** plus 1 of its own, and none of the six symbols had moved — all six lines existed, unchanged, at the cached number. The three ways to mint one, written here **without their `@line` suffixes so this row does not itself mint three more** — a counterexample inside backticks *is* an anchor to the gate, which is how §DOC-02f briefly re-broke it: **markdown table pipe-escaping** (an anchor reading `q.retryGateDays \|\| 1`, where `\|` is the table escape and the source says `||`), an **ellipsis** standing in for elided code (`checkLevelUp: () => { … _checkLevelUp(); }`), and **whitespace normalisation** (an anchor reading `const d20 = …` where the source has `const d20  =` with two spaces). The fix is always to shorten the anchor to the shortest unambiguous *verbatim* fragment — `` `q.retryGateDays@6811` ``, `` `checkLevelUp: () =>@22348` `` — never to widen it into a pretty quotation. **Related and worth stating plainly: §DOC-02e recorded `check:walk` 16/16 green while carrying those 5 dead anchors**, because the run hit the known harness detach artifact and never reached gate 15. *When the detach artifact fires, the gates that did not run are not green — re-run them individually before recording a result.* **Scope one report per increment** — §DOC-02a produced 2 audit rows + 2 doc corrections, §DOC-02b 1 defect row + 1 ordering correction, and §DOC-02c 2 defect rows, all from a single report each. **A fourth accelerator, added by §DOC-02c and now mandatory for any report with dead symbols:** run `git log -S "<symbol>" -- play.html` on every symbol the census marks dead. It costs one command per symbol and separates **RETIRED** (shipped, later removed — the report was true when written) from **NOT SHIPPED** (never existed under that name — the report was written from intent, not from the file). §DOC-02c's headline finding came from exactly this: `stalkModal` and two of its DOM ids return **0 commits ever**, while the feature they describe shipped three days later under different names. *A report cannot make this distinction about itself; only history can.* **A fifth accelerator, added by §DOC-02d:** when a report names a character two ways, `grep` **both** names in the engine before writing the delta table — the split is usually still there as two keys, and it is the highest-value finding a content report can yield (§AUDIT-03n cost a whole row to repair one). **Three accelerators, now proven three times:** (1) the arc's *maintained* home doc (`story.md`/`world.md`) is usually already correct and annotated, so the lab report's job is the **delta table**, not a second copy of it; (2) the fastest first measurement is always `grep -c` on every key the report claims to have added — a missing entry is a delta, a present entry still needs its *fields* compared; and (3) **for any report that enumerates symbols, batch the whole name list through one `grep -c` loop before reading a line of it** — §DOC-02b's 197-symbol census took one command and immediately partitioned the report into 180 live claims and 17 dead subsystems, which is the shape of the delta table. **One thing to watch for that §DOC-02b found and §DOC-02a did not:** a long report can **contradict itself**, and the tiebreak is mechanical — *believe the section that cites a line number.*

> **THE HEADLINE — the one report that could count its subject with `ls` did not.** The directory held **71 files**; the report says **65** in its title, status line, abstract, hypothesis, finding, §II layer table, conclusion, appendix heading and footer. Four quantities are in play and none is 65 as used: the body enumerates **65 slots over 62 distinct files** (`kindness-calculus`, `la-riva-grief-arc`, `naval-campaign-layer` hold two each); the appendix has 65 distinct rows; body ∪ appendix = **66**; and **five files are named nowhere** — `loot-drop-weapon-economy`, `plan-cleanup-v13`, `plan-cleanup-v17`, `plan-cleanup-world-builder-arc`, `timeline-history-completed`. > **Report-rot: essentially zero, and unusually clean.** All seven §II design constants are **exact against the build it was written against** — `play.html` at **14,377 lines** (the report's own headline figure, exact), **370** `MONSTER_POOL` entries, **66** `WORLD_DB` terrains, **76** `NODE_MAP` nodes, **107** `_S_DEFAULTS()` depth-1 fields, **41** `FROBERGER_JOURNAL` entries, **6** `NPC_DIALOGUES` keys — and the corpus figure *"37 Markdown documents"* is exact at `59a9e0d`. **The single wrong number is the single number it did not measure:** *"23 lab reports"*, cited twice, copied from `index.md`, which said 23 while **22** existed on disk. *Instrument 9's gradient with an unusually clean edge — the author's own measurements held, the citation did not.* **Two survival results worth keeping:** `FROBERGER_JOURNAL` is **41 at the archive and 41 at HEAD, the only design constant in the program that has not moved in 79 days**; and `_S_DEFAULTS()` survives at **104 of its 107 fields (97%)** across a 2.69× file growth and the §ARCH-01 migration, with only `hearthHome`, `lastCorridorCells` and `voidSignClicked` removed — the strongest structural survival the program has measured, and the one architectural prediction the report made that can be checked numerically.

> **⟶ (2026-08-11) — §DOC-02b ✅ SHIPPED `d5b1cb9`: the biggest report in the directory turned out to be a survival result, not a rot result — and its most useful defect is that it disagrees with itself.** On "continue", took `lab-report-architecture-full.md` by the §DOC-02 ordering rule — 1,009 lines / 58 KB, the 2026-05-22 whole-file architectural review, the report with the most surface to rot because it inventories the *entire* engine. **Existing-Work-First:** the same uncommitted user recolor is still in the tree (HP bar gradient / light-green playfield / theme-ink die numerals, self-labelled *"user recolor 2026-08-06"*) — **left untouched and unstaged**, this increment is docs-only; two claude processes; every number re-measured from HEAD. **Method was a four-part census** and it is the accelerator worth reusing: all **197** named functions and constants batched through one `grep -c` loop *before reading a line of the report*, which immediately partitioned it into live and dead; then each claimed object literal brace-matched out of the file and its fields counted; then every arithmetic claim read at its live definition; then every "N entries" claim counted. **The headline inverts the expectation the program was built on: 180 of the 197 symbols still resolve, and all four structural theses hold at 5.5× the file** — 14,377 → **38,707 lines**, 76 → **416 nodes**, 370 → **398 monsters**, 66 → **111 terrains**, ~107 → **193** `_S_DEFAULTS()` fields, 6 → **204** NPC profiles, 26×16 → **90×360**. Flat file scope, one mutable state object per mode, full-section rewrite (`storyRender` is now **1,490 lines** with 28 `innerHTML` writes per pass), synchronous click-to-pixel: none of them is what any of the eleven intervening tracks had to fix. **The rot is all inventory, in four blocks with one track each:** §WALK/§NAV-01 deleted the compass graph outright (`N:`/`E:`/`S:`/`W:` now **0 occurrences** in `NODE_MAP`) plus `GATE_LOCKS`, `CORRIDOR_CELLS`, `buildCorridorMap`, `_routeSegments`, `_corridorTerrain`, `_wireGlyph`, `storyMove`, `storyCorridorTravel`, `triggerCorridorEncounter`, `_bfsPath`; §ARCH-01 replaced the quest shape (`objectiveText` **0×**, `completionCheck` **0×**; `schema:` 2,823× / `bits:` 2,824× / `gate:` 2,836×, 16 `_legacyFn` left); §CELL-13 removed the whole "Hearthing and Teleport" section (`storyPortal` / `storySetHearthHome` / `storyUseTransmort` / `S_story.hearthHome` all **0×** — only the removal comments survive); §VM-01-G-FU absorbed the five narrative-modal launchers into `NODE_HOOKS`/`NODE_PANELS`. Also worth knowing: the `MONSTER_POOL` entry shape lost `cr`/`xpVal`/`terrain[]` entirely (**0 occurrences each**) for `{key,…,atk,dmgFlat,tier}`, and **6 of the 11 rows in the report's render-target table name DOM ids that do not exist** (`#story-desc`, `#story-vendor`, `#story-inventory`, `#story-journal-overlay`, `#story-char-overlay`, `#history-cards` — the live main target is `story-text-box`). **Six formula claims measured wrong, and three were wrong when written, not stale:** the corridor `encounterChance = min(95, 10 + notoriety×1.5 + activeQuestCount×4)` **has no expression in the file at any point** (encounter rate is per-terrain data, roads at 0 — active quest count has never fed encounter probability); `_calcPlayerAc()` has **no DEX term**; `_extraAttackCount()` has **no Action-Surge doubling branch**; `_notorietyWeights` is 6 brackets not 4 and **deadly is never 0** (so "deadly is impossible at low notoriety" never held); `_rollD100Loot` floors at a Minor Healing Potion, not "nothing"; and *"`roll()` is the only function that calls `Math.random`"* is false at **54 sites** — Story-Mode state draws the seeded stream (§VM-01-B), with the residue tracked by the open **§DX-02m**. Two "no X" claims needed qualifying rather than correcting: **no CDN still holds exactly** (0 external `<script src>`, 0 external `<link href="http…">`), but there is now an author-time build step (the four `*:CORE` parity re-inlines) and two servers beside the file (WBAPI `:1367`, and the opt-in §MESH-01 mesh — 12 `fetch`/`WebSocket` sites), so *"no server exists"* and *"nothing is required to play"* stopped being the same sentence. **The single most useful finding is that the report contradicts itself.** §VII.E and §XI.E give different `_curseScore()` weights (`×3/×1/−5` vs `+2/+1/−1`), different ranges (−5…+55 vs −20…+40) and different `_covenantStanding()` labels — and **HEAD resolves both against §VII.E, which is the only one of the two that cites a line number**; §XI.E's `Covenant Keeper (True)` tier gated on `pitWins ≥ 5` + `ebNegotiated ≥ 5` is not a stale claim, it **never shipped**. *The half of a document that points at code is the half that is right* — the argument for `symbol@line` (§DX-01e), available three months before the repo adopted it. **What held exactly, and is now pinned:** 41 journal entries with `readAloud:true` on precisely **10**; 20 Epic Bosses / 20 EB givers / 5 Sweelinck variants; XP `AC × maxHP` and gold `0.1 × AC × maxHP`; the notoriety formula; `_missionComplete()`'s 12-bits-at-8; the 5 covenant tiers at `maxScore` −6/0/7/14/∞; the Void Tide days **3/7/14/21/28/35/42**; the save format. `check:anchors` **210 anchors across 10 docs, 0 stale**; `check:legacycodes` **OK** (10 swept docs, `docs/lab-reports/` is HISTORY by classification); `npm run check:walk` **exit 0, all 16 gates, no detach artifact this run** (5th consecutive run without one). **Docs-only: no HTML, no tests, no §7½ — the 935/4 (940) Playwright baseline stands untouched.** **Stated scope limit:** this pass verified structure, counts, formulas and symbol *liveness*; So there was **no sweep to do**, exactly as the row predicted, and the work was the instrument. **The finding is about the instrument, not the codes.** A naive codeish-token scan reproduces §AUDIT-03m's noise problem one level up — **943 hits over 189 tokens** (`NPC` 151× · `NODE` · `ADV` · `HP` · Roman act numerals). Narrowed to the **two-letter** shape of the retired code space it collapses to **23 tokens**, of which **3 are node codes and 20 are jargon**. It also catches a shape nobody had named: a **real** historical code missing from the `maps.md` legend, so `npm run nodes` never mapped it (`FR`→`AMS`). **Limit stated and asserted:** two letters only — a three-letter code written from memory is not caught, and the test says so rather than letting silence read as coverage. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0** bare · 5 planted controls (2 positive exit 1, 3 negative exit 0) · `audit03m-legacy-codes.test.js` **15/15**, positive control = new tool + HEAD's `world.md` → **3 fail**, gate exits 1 · Playwright **796 passed / 4 failed** (documented four; the flake at `multiplayer-presence:171` passes 7/7 re-run). **Next:** §AUDIT-03d is still the gating row and still needs the user's staging design call.

> **⟶ (2026-08-04) — §AUDIT-03m ✅ SHIPPED `b399318`: the two live narrative docs stop speaking 26×16, and the sweep is a measured tool with a gate — but the codes turned out to be the symptom, not the disease.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's staging question still unanswered, took the top row needing **no design call** (the last board named m as the mechanical one). Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `69a298f`, server stopped, one other claude process, and the row's premise re-measured live rather than trusted. **The premise held; the row's own warning was the design problem.** A naive scan returns **4,663 hits and most are noise** — `DC` is a difficulty class **435** times, `EB` abbreviates *Epic Battleground* **163** — so a token replace would have produced exactly the nonsense the row predicted. **Shipped:** `src/scripts/legacy-codes.js` / `npm run legacy`, which reads the LEGACY CODE MAP out of the **generated** node index (never re-typed) and counts a token only in a node context, with ambiguous codes classified **explicitly** (#13/#14 house style) → 4,663 → **2,517 real, 230 in live docs**. Every doc is **SWEEP / PENDING / HISTORY** and an **unclassified one fails**; **HISTORY is annotated, never rewritten** (2,287 references left alone — the tool *refuses* `--annotate` on a lab report or the archive). `world.md` **77 annotations / 81 lines**, `story.md` **193 / 160**, in two forms: inline `` `LHR` (historical `CI`) `` when sparse, live codes + **one** trailing key when a line carries 3+ (ten interleaved parentheticals is a wall). **The finding worth more than the sweep:** `world.md`'s node sections describe a **retired navigation model** — measured at HEAD, **0 of the 416 live nodes carries `N`/`S`/`E`/`W`** (§CELL-01 stripped them) and `J1`/`J6`/`J7` are gone — so *"Node connections from CI (**verified against `NODE_MAP`**)"* is unverifiable today, and annotating the codes **alone would have laundered a false claim into live-looking ones**. It now carries a historical-model banner (edge graph · junction stubs · the `R03,C16` projection vs live r 2–73 / c 154–249) pointing at `./api.sh location`. **Three tool bugs caught in review, all fixed in the tool and re-generated from a clean checkout rather than hand-patched:** it wrote **inside code fences**, it **nested backticks** on already-backticked codes, and it missed a **single-code table cell**. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0** (new gate #16); negative control on the real doc — a bare *"north of SL"* appended to `world.md` → **exit 1**, restored `shasum -c` **OK**, empty diff; `audit03m-legacy-codes.test.js` **7/7** including a **positive** control (6 shapes that must still be caught) and a **negative** control (9 that must not), because a detector narrowed into uselessness and a deleted one look identical in a green run (§DX-02l-FU); full Playwright **778 passed / 4 failed / 0 flaky** — the `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four and nothing else. **No HTML, no §7½** (docs + tooling; server stayed stopped). **Limits recorded, not hidden:** an ambiguous code is only flagged on a node-context line, and **a fenced block is code** — so a dead code in a *runnable example* escapes the gate. One did (`./api.sh get node CY`, months dead) and was fixed by hand to `HKG`. **Side finding filed, not swept: §AUDIT-03p** — `_voidFlavorLine`'s **function-local** `CLUSTER` map has **11 of 16 dead node keys**, so the void-pressure flavor line never renders at eleven authored places, and **gate #13 cannot see it because it scans only top-level registries.** **Next: the 7 PENDING live docs (230 refs, no design call, named on every gate run) — or §AUDIT-03p (engine, needs §7½), §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row, needs the staging call), §DX-02j / §AUDIT-03o / §DX-01e-FU2 / §AUDIT-03i (design calls), §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

`choice`'s *"empty handler"* is the engine's first **suspending** opcode (`*choice(bit, ctx)@22319`), and `renderChoiceBlock` — which the file says *"was never written"* — no longer occurs in the file at all; audit **94 anchors across 6 docs, 0 dead**; **`anchors:fix` reports "no stale line hints to refresh"** — every number written was exact, the round-trip proof; **negative control on the real game file** (renamed `const _ssjNpcs` on line 32427 → gate exits 1 naming `potential.md:176`; restored, `shasum -c` **OK**, `git diff` empty); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; `dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js` **5/5**, the new test **failing on the pre-migration file** and passing after, restored byte-identical. **No HTML shipped and no §7½** — docs + tooling only. **The durable half is the new test, because the gate cannot provide it:** `check:anchors` fails on a dead *symbol*, but a bare `` `31732` `` resolves to nothing and is invisible to it — nothing else stands between the seed inbox and a slow slide back to line numbers. **The lesson: a doc read to BUILD FROM is a live doc, and "re-grep before building" is not optional when 97% of a file's own pointers are wrong.** Docs: CONTRIBUTING's Doc-Anchor Policy records that every live doc is now migrated; but `choice` still has **0 authors**, so the re-triage should follow one exemplar quest, not precede it. **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §DX-02h/i (both small), §AUDIT-03o (measured, design call), §DX-01e-FU2 (design call), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-07-31) — §DX-01e ✅ SHIPPED `57d79d7`: a doc anchor now names a SYMBOL, and 43 of the 50 anchors in the live docs were pointing somewhere else.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's design question still unanswered, took the last open §DX-01 row — the one the board named first among the no-design-call alternatives. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `a19919f`, server stopped, 4 claude processes present but the game file untouched. **The premise held and understated itself.** Sampling 14 bare anchors at HEAD before writing anything, **9 pointed at unrelated code** — `22104`, cited for `_rollSkill`, had become a prof-bonus line; `31618`, cited for the §CELL-03 position sync, an `aurosBlueprintKnown` branch — and the bare form appears **720 times across 27 files**. **The convention shipped is one token, `` `symbol@1234` ``:** the **symbol** is the pointer (any literal substring of the line meant, optionally file-qualified — `` `src/js/wbapi-server.js:function seededNext@1147` ``), the **number is a cached hint** refreshed mechanically by `npm run anchors:fix`. It is §DX-02e/§DX-02f's lesson in a third place — *pin the property, never the generated coordinate* — because a line number is **generated** by every edit above it. **`src/scripts/resolve-anchors.js`** carries audit / `--fix` / `--legacy` (a migration tracker) / a lookup mode that replaces `grep -n` / `--selftest`, and is wired as **`check:walk` gate #15 `check:anchors`**. **Its failure condition is deliberately narrow — a DEAD symbol fails, a drifted number only warns** — because failing on drift would re-create the per-increment tax the row exists to remove. **Building the negative control is what found the real hole:** plain substring matching let `XP_BY_TIER` → `XP_BY_TIER_RETIRED` still "resolve", so a *rename* — the exact case the gate is for — would have passed; resolution is now **identifier-boundary aware**, and a `placeholder` phase keeps `--fix` from re-pointing the docs' own `` `symbol@1234` `` example. **Migrated (live docs only):** CONTRIBUTING.md 10 · mechanics.md 3 · mechanics-combat.md 2 · **every bare anchor in BACKLOG.md's open rows** — `plan-archive.md` (211) and the lab reports (~300) keep theirs, because **history is annotated, not rewritten** (§DX-02c/§AUDIT-03m). **Re-anchoring surfaced two stale CONTRIBUTING claims, corrected in place:** the seeded-RNG bullet still said the client rolls encounters from `Math.random()` *"until §VM-01-B lands"* (it landed; the client draws `_seededNext()`, and only the *weighting* still diverges), and the `_legacy_fn` bullet cited a quest that *"rolls `Math.random() > 0.5` inside quest data"* when **no `_legacy_fn` line in the file contains `Math.random` any more**. **Evidence:** 7-phase `--selftest`; **negative control on the real game file** (renamed `const XP_BY_TIER` in place → gate exits 1 naming `BACKLOG.md:312`; restored, `shasum -c` **OK**); `dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js` **4/4**, with the migration test **failing on the pre-migration docs** (stash of the 4 doc files → 1 failed / 3 passed); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; audit reports **56 anchors across 5 docs, 0 dead**. **No HTML shipped and no §7½** — docs + tooling only. **Side work filed, not swept: §DX-01e-FU** (`potential.md`'s 36 bare anchors — the only live doc left on the old form). **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §DX-02h/i (both small), §AUDIT-03o (measured, design call), §DX-01e-FU, §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-07-30) — §DX-01d + §DX-01i ✅ SHIPPED TOGETHER: every `del` in the API reported success and changed nothing on disk. Not just `del monster`, not just junctions — the whole DELETE family, for every key.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's design question still unanswered, took the pairing the last board named first (*"the two `del` defects are one class — cheaper paired"*). Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `e4c03a0`, server stopped, a 2nd claude process present but the game file untouched. **Measured before editing, in-process against a COPY** (`play.html` never written): `monster:fish_01`, `quest:mq_1` and `node:RUH` each returned `ok:true`, each left `_rawSrc` **byte-identical**, and each **came back after save+reload**. Both rows described the symptom correctly and **understated the scope by 3×** — every `delete()` in `wbapi-core.js` was the single line `delete WBAPI.<collection>[key]`, and the server's generic `DELETE` handler ended at `json(...)` with **no `saveAndVerify` at all**, so nothing reached disk even in principle. The precedent §DX-01i pointed at (`the node-delete splice at wbapi-core.js ~1260`) was **dead code with zero callers**, and its private brace-walker missed `/* */` comments and orphaned the `NODE_COORDS` row besides. **This is §DX-01c in mirror image — a write path that reports success without persisting — and the lesson is the same in both directions: nothing throws, so the failure is silent.** **Shipped:** `WBAPI.deleteEntrySource(section,key)`, one source-level excision for all four types with **verify-or-revert** (the section's depth-1 key *multiset* must lose exactly one instance of `key`, or the splice is rolled back and the delete fails loudly rather than corrupting the section) and clamped past nested START anchors so a pool delete can never reach the trophy-drops map; **the `check:dupkeys` scanner moved into `wbapi-core.js`** and imported back by the gate, so audit and writer share ONE scanner (two would drift — the §AUDIT-03f class); **cascades** (node→`NODE_COORDS`, monster→`MONSTER_DROPS`) so no orphan is left for `./api.sh audit`; the server's DELETE now routes through `saveAndVerify` with a new **`postVerify`** hook — a delete's proof is an *absence* in the re-parsed collections, which `expectedFields` cannot express — returning `deleteVerified:true`; and **`highway --execute` deprecated + refused up front** (before the coordinate fetch, so it needs no server) with the real recipe in the error. **Evidence:** `dx01di-delete-persists.test.js` **8/8**, including **the 20 largest nested UQF bodies excised one at a time** (largest 3,350 bytes of braces/strings/arrow-fns, each costing exactly one entry); **end-to-end through the live server twice, each a byte-identical no-op** — `post monster dx01i_probe` → `del monster` → `grep -c` **0** (the row's own reproduction, which used to leave **1**), and `post node ZZP` → `del node` → **0**, game file `shasum 018ee943…` unchanged both times; `check:walk` **12/12 exit 0**; full Playwright **717 passed / 4 failed** — the baseline is exactly the documented four in `worldbuilder-crud-arrays.test.js` plus the known `multiplayer-presence` flake (passed on retry). **No HTML shipped and no §7½ — authoring-surface only; nothing a player sees changed.** **Docs:** a new **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #5** carries the class forward (*whenever you add a write path, the acceptance test is a round trip — save, re-parse, assert the change survived*), Hazards #2/#3/#4 updated to ✅, prompt.md §3 #4/#5, `api-user-guide.md` §10, `API-README.md`, and `./api.sh help` (§21 no longer advertises **eleven** `highway --execute` invocations that now die). **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §AUDIT-03j (9 registry keys naming non-existent nodes + a `check:walk` gate), §DX-02g (2 field PUTs), §DX-01e (line-anchor drift — the last open §DX-01 row), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-07-30) — §DX-02f ✅ SHIPPED: the two undocumented reds were STALE TESTS, not mechanic drift. The Playwright red set is now four, in one file, and prompt.md §7 records all of it.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's design question still unanswered, took the row the last board named as the clean alternative. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `dfeeff4`, server stopped, a 2nd claude process present but the game file untouched. **Both §DX-01c side-findings were re-measured from the mechanic outward before touching a test — and the good news is the mechanics are fine: the §XP-01 effort grant (`6891`) and §BOARD-01-FU8's ESCALATED standing both measured correct at HEAD, so both ship records stand.** **The row's "deterministic, not flaky" claim was the thing that was wrong, and it was the tell.** `effort-xp:67` injected its failed check with `Math.random = () => 0.01` — but **§VM-01-B had moved `_rollSkill`'s d20 onto the seeded stream** (`QuestRuntime._rollSkill` → `E.rng()`, `22104`), so the stub controlled nothing, the check rolled freely, and the test failed precisely when the roll *passed* and paid the full `150` instead of the 38 effort share; 3 bare `--retries=0` runs went **fail / pass / fail** (DEX 8 vs DC 13 → `d20+1`, a ~45% coin flip — the "reproduces on a bare re-run" reading came from the 2-retry config). `warrants-board:999` was subtler: **every standing assertion already passed** (ESCALATED `+4`, idempotent, `questDone`) and only `posAfter ≠ posBefore` failed, because the test hand-set `playerR/C = 5,9` alongside `currentCode = 'RVP'` — a state no step can produce — so `storyRender`'s §CELL-03 sync (`31618`) corrected the coords to RVP's real cell (17,180) and the test read the correction as a warp. **No jump travel, no player move.** **Shipped (test-only, `play.html` untouched):** `effort-xp` forces the fail through the engine's own dial via a scoped `forceFail(id, fn)` that raises `sc.dc` to 999 and restores in `finally` — the same idiom `warrants-board` already used for a forced PASS (`sc.dc = -100`); `warrants-board` derives its stand-on cell from `NODE_COORDS[q.activateNode]`. **Evidence:** both suites **28/28 on three consecutive `--retries=0` runs** (no flake masking); full Playwright **710 passed / 4 failed** (the documented crud-arrays four); `check:walk` **12/12 exit 0**; diff = 2 test files +21/−7. No §7½ — nothing a player sees changed. **Durable fix (the row's real ask): prompt.md §7 now carries the FULL red set with the date last re-proved**, plus two lessons — *a test that stubs an RNG must stub the stream the code actually draws*, and (a second time after §DX-02e) *pin the property, never a fabricated or generated coordinate*. **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call), else §DX-01d+i (the two `del` defects together), §AUDIT-03j (9 registry keys naming non-existent nodes), §DX-02g (2 field PUTs), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**
